- C1 on 10 cells (all cable types, cell cap 4 partitions / 200 k DMIPS): α = 1 (job 644): min cost 8,107.29 (gap 0.29 % after 1,800 s; α = 0: 7,961.27, +1.8 %), the 57-SC hub splits into S24 (35 SCs) and S30 (24) plus S10 (4) and S17 (2); phase 2 unfinished (cable 68.8 m, gap 29 %). α = 3 (job 645): no feasible design found in 1,800 s (bound 8,635, no incumbent) — the design exists in principle (total demand 345 k vs 10 × 200 k) but the lazy model's heuristics did not reach a feasible point; to check with a longer run / feasibility-oriented settings. Lite sweep (7 levels) fine: 3 → 4 units, 6 → 8 partitions, cost 8,590 → 9,917.

### 16.8 C2 implemented (2026-10-20): single failures and standby instances

- `uncertainty.json: failures` (block `--failures`, in `--uncertainty`): `critical_asil_min` 3, `recovery_ms` 50, `classes` cell/switch/cable. `chains.json` gained `ftti_ms` (brake/steering 50, motor 100, light 1000 — provisional, section 17).
- Variables: `r[i,j]` standby cell, `hot[i]` (only when the smallest FTTI of the SC's chains ≥ recovery, else hot forced), `hr = r AND hot`. Static: one standby per critical SC on another cell, partition of its ASIL and its hardware there; hot standbys consume resources in the capacity constraints and are packed; cold standbys reserve capacity only per failure scenario.
- Standby flows: variants of every base flow with the standby instance at one or both ends (standby ↔ primary partner, standby ↔ standby partner, sensor attachment → standby, standby → actuator attachment); a standby flow of a cold standby needs a path (cables) but carries no nominal traffic.
- Callback: for every scenario (active cells, switches, used cables) and every critical SC, the primary or the standby must be alive with every flow to a critical partner served by an alive partner instance over a selected path avoiding the failed element; non-critical partners may be lost (degraded mode); peripheral devices are single, so the failure of their own attachment element is out of scope. Violations add a no-good cut on the positions and selected paths involved. Cell failures also check the capacity of the cells receiving the activated cold standbys (nominal + hot + activated ≤ partitions × capacity) and cut linearly.
- Reporting: `solution.standby` {sc: cell, hot/cold, partition}; diagram lists standbys in their partitions (dashed = cold); `solve_info.failure_cuts`, `critical`.
- First smoke run: infeasible because the survival check demanded non-critical partners; fixed (skipped). Re-run pending.
- C2 smoke (lite, 4 cells, α = 0, failures on): proven min cost 11,310.25 against 8,589.74 without the failure constraints (+31.7 %, the price of surviving every single cell/switch/cable failure), 4 units, 7 partitions, 36 standbys, all cold (every chain FTTI 50 ms ≥ recovery 50 ms, and cold is cheaper than hot), 1,490 failure cuts among 22,310 lazy cuts, phase 1 63 s; cable phase hit its 60 s limit (gap 11 %). Standbys concentrate on S09 (29) and S25 (6).
- Two defects found and fixed in the first C2 runs (2026-10-20): (1) the survival check demanded non-critical partners (made every lite instance infeasible) → non-critical partners may be lost; (2) a solution whose pairs had no selected path yet failed the survival check and the no-good cut excluded the *placement* — an invalid cut (the fault was the missing path, handled by the path cuts). Symptoms: "optimal" costs that differed between runs (11,310 / 11,342 / 11,305 on the lite instance) and 10-cell runs with failures or α = 3 that found no incumbent in 30 min. After the fix the lite incumbent is 11,293.92 (gap 1.2 % at the 300 s limit; valid cuts are weaker, so the proof takes longer).
- Solution repair (same date): a rejected heuristic solution with a valid placement is completed in the callback (direct paths for every pair with traffic, cable counts from the loads, chain and failure checks re-run on the completion) and handed back to Gurobi in the next MIPNODE callback (`cbSetSolution` / `cbUseSolution`); `solve_info.repairs_used`. Lite: 3–4 repairs per solve. Runs 655 (α = 0 + failures) and 656 (α = 3) restarted with the fixed code.
- α = 3 on 10 cells is infeasible under the provisional chain budgets, not merely hard: with the budgets tripled (job 657) the first design appears after 11 s (10,322 after 60 s, gap 19 %), with the original budgets (jobs 645, 649, 656) no design in 30 min. Reason: the α = 1 design already uses 65–95 % of its chain budgets with two crossings per chain (3.6–7 ms per crossing with the robust constants); at α = 3 the ASIL-C group (≈ 244 k DMIPS) must spread over 2–3 cells, chains cross 3–5 times and the 10–20 ms budgets cannot hold. Consequence for the method: the tolerated growth has a ceiling set by timing; the lazy model cannot prove infeasibility quickly (the latency, path and failure constraints are not in Gurobi's model), so levels without a design within the limit are reported as "no design found", and the budgets of section 17 decide where the ceiling lies.
- Confirmation (job 657, 10 cells, α = 3, chain budgets × 3): design found after 11 s; after 1,800 s cost 9,305.78 (gap 6.6 %), 5 units (S20 24, S01 20, S24 14, S17 5, S30 2), 8 partitions, tightest chain 47.6 / 60 ms with 4 crossings — i.e. 2.4× the original 20 ms budget. 17 repaired solutions were used. Confirms that the original budgets, not the capacity, make α = 3 infeasible on 10 cells.
- C2 on 10 cells (job 655, α = 0, all blocks, 3,600 s + 600 s): cost 10,694.33 (gap 1.47 %; without failures 7,961.27, +34 %), 5 units: primaries S30 (44 SCs), S20 (7), S10 (5), S01 (5), S17 (4); 47 standbys, all cold, 32 of them at S20 (which therefore carries DSP + HW_ACC as the second accelerator cell), 5 each at S01, S10, S30; 7 partitions. Cost split: partitions 3,500, hardware 4,200 (two DSP + HW_ACC pairs), ports 245, peripheral cables 2,253, backbone 496. Tightest chain 14.9 / 20 ms. 115 k lazy cuts of which 295 failure cuts, 7 repaired solutions used. Cable phase unfinished (67.4 m, gap 33 %). Diagram results/c2a0_655/solution_1_architecture.png. Interpretation: the single-failure guarantee is paid mostly through the duplicated accelerator hardware at the standby cell and one extra partition, not through many extra cables.
- Switch failure refinement (2026-10-20): a switched cell's ETH ports are the switch's ports, so the failure of the switch also kills the ETH links ending at that cell (not only the relayed paths); smoke run unchanged (11,277, gap 1.3 % at 300 s; no switch in the lite design).
- C2 completed (2026-10-20 night): (1) chain latency in every failure scenario over the surviving instances (first alive variant per link: primary–primary, primary–standby, standby–primary, standby–standby) must stay within `degraded_bound_ms` (`chains.json`, provisional 2 × nominal budget); enforced as lazy cuts whose indicator also carries the variables that killed the preferred variants, so the cut is valid only for that configuration; a chain broken by a lost non-critical member is degraded mode, not a violation. (2) `solution.failure_scenarios`: per scenario the element, the activated cold standbys, the CPU load they add per cell, every chain's scenario latency against its degraded bound (status ok / over bound / broken). (3) Switch failure also kills the ETH links ending at the switched cell. Smoke (lite): 11,249.62 (gap 0.75 % at 300 s), 36 standbys (1 hot, 35 cold), 10 scenarios, none over the degraded bound; e.g. losing S01 activates 21 cold standbys adding 51 k DMIPS at S25.
- Cell-cap defect found in the overnight sweep (2026-10-20 night): constraint 1d enforced partitions × partition capacity ≤ cell cap, i.e. with the provisional values at most 2 partitions per cell (the `max_partitions` 4 was dead), which is why α = 0 of job 704 cost 8,095.65 with 5 units instead of the 7,961.27 / 3 units of job 640 (the 4-partition hub S30 hosts 86.9 k DMIPS / 34.0 k / 43.0 k with margins, well inside 200 k / 64 k / 128 k). Fixed to the documented semantics: hosted demand of the unit over all partitions (margins, growth and hot standbys included) ≤ cap × derating × y, plus the cell-level analogue of the cold-standby capacity cut (nominal load + hot + cold standbys activated by one cell failure ≤ cell cap). All 10-cell results above that were solved with the caps (jobs 644–657, 704/705 before the restart) used the stricter 2-partition rule and are to be re-read with that in mind; job 640 (no caps yet) stands. Smoke run (lite, 4 cells, failures): see the results note that follows; sweeps 704/705 cancelled and restarted with the fix.
- Smoke run with the fixed cell cap (lite, 4 cells, failures on, 300 s): proven min cost 10,955.16 (was 11,277 at gap 1.3 % with the 2-partition rule), 4 units, 7 partitions (S01 now holds 3), 36 cold standbys, 1,586 failure cuts among 13,394 lazy cuts, 5 repairs, phase 1 62 s, phase 2 16 s (cable 60.36 m); offline check: nominal and every cell-failure load within the cell caps (worst S01 RAM 54 %). Sweeps restarted with the fix as jobs 708 (no failures) and 709 (failures) at 14:56; expectation: α = 0 of 708 reproduces the 7,961.27 / 3-unit design of job 640.
- Restarted sweep, first check passed: job 708 α = 0 → 7,961.27 / cable 57.02 m, both phases proven in about 4 min, identical to job 640 (3 units, 6 partitions).
- Start solutions (2026-10-20, user decision, no flag): every solve of a sweep (growth level, Pareto extreme or point) receives the variable values of the previous solve as a Gurobi start (matched by variable name, `optimize(start=...)`, `self.last_values`). Only a starting incumbent: Gurobi verifies it through the lazy callback and drops it if the new level's constraints reject it, so proven optima and Pareto values are unchanged (only the reported design may differ between equal-cost alternatives). The dead `--warm_start` flag is gone. Smoke: lite 4 cells, failures on, levels 0/1/2 plus α = 1 standalone for comparison (results below).
- Explicit traffic mode removed (2026-10-20 evening, user decision). What `lazy_traffic` did: the backbone traffic can be written into the MILP in two ways. Explicit mode (`lazy_traffic: false`, ETH only): one continuous variable `lnk[t,u,v]` per flow and site pair (1 when the flow starts at u and ends at v) and per-path volume variables `f`, with static constraints 'volume of a pair = Σ f over its paths', 'f ≤ volume · sel' and 'link load ≤ cables × capacity'; 95 k `lnk` columns at 33 cells made every B&B node cost 25–30 k simplex iterations. Lazy mode (`lazy_traffic: true`): none of these variables; Gurobi's MIPSOL callback checks each integer solution and adds only the violated constraints as lazy cuts ('Σ_p sel ≥ start + end − 1', 'cap · comm ≥ Σ vol · (start + end + sel − 2)'), later also the chain-latency and failure-scenario cuts, which exist only in this mode. B3 (section 16.3) showed identical optima and a far faster search (10 cells 11 s vs 115 s; 33 cells proven in 2 h), so lazy has been the default since. Now the explicit code (`lnk`, `f`, C-NET-1/1b/4, `conservation_cut`) is deleted, the callback is always on, `buses.json: backbone` keeps only `candidate_paths` and `path_link_cut`. Smoke after the removal: see the note below.
- Start-solution smoke (lite, 4 cells, failures on, 300 s per level): mechanism works (1,840 of 1,840 variables handed over, no duplicate names; α = 0 proven 10,955.16 as before). At α = 1 the α = 0 design is rejected by the new capacities ('User MIP start did not produce a new incumbent'), so the level starts cold; with the 300 s limit the sweep's α = 1 incumbent (12,032, gap 9.2 %) and a standalone α = 1 run (11,832, gap 7.6 %) differ only because neither finished. Equality of proven optima with and without a start: checked on the no-failure lite sweep (note below).
- `--vehicle_layout` (2026-10-20 evening, user request, opt-in flag): main.py routes the cables of every solution (`solution_paths.py`) and renders the iso/top views in headless Blender (`build_vehicle.py`) into `<output_dir>/vehicle_layout_<i>/`, next to the solution JSON and the architecture diagram, instead of the separate `results/vehicle_layout/solution_<job>/` folders. Blender launcher from `$BLENDER`, else `blender-headless`/`blender` on the PATH, else `~/blender/blender-headless`; a config copy without `routing_space.npz` falls back to `configs/` for the routing graph. Cycles renders on the CPU when the job has no GPU (fallback already in build_vehicle.py). The sbatch runs carry the flag.
- Smoke set after the explicit-mode removal (lite, 4 cells, 300 s per level, all proven): no-failure sweep α = 0 / 1 / 2 → 8,352.46 / 8,384.39 / 8,860.25 (cable 56.85 / 58.70 / 63.13 m; α = 0 was 8,589.74 before the cell-cap fix); standalone α = 1 without a start solution → 8,384.39 / 58.70 m, identical to the sweep's level with the start (proven optimum unchanged by the warm start); failures α = 0 → 10,955.16 / 60.36 m, identical to the pre-removal smoke. Smoke folders removed from results/ afterwards.
- results/ cleaned (2026-10-20 evening, user request): all earlier job folders and logs (v1 baselines 241–246, B/C test runs 367–657, cancelled 704/705), smoke runs, old solution renders (results/vehicle_layout/solution_*), layout variants and voxel checks are deleted; their numbers stay in this file. Kept: the running sweeps 708/709 and the reference layout renders results/vehicle_layout/final, final_band (+ the Blender MCP add-on file). `--vehicle_layout` smoke (lite, 4 cells, CPU render): 4 min 15 s in total, iso/top/legend/layout.blend written to the run folder.

### 16.9 Independent solution checker and regression test (2026-10-20 evening)
- `tools/check_solution.py <run dir | solution_<i>.json>`: re-evaluates every model rule on a stored solution with plain Python, reading the raw configs and the architecture itself (no import from optimizer.py, optimizer_utils.py, architecture_loader.py, config_reader.py, models.py). Rules: placement on candidate cells; partitions (site and ASIL of every member, counts, max per ASIL and per cell, no empty partition, per-partition capacity of primaries + hot standbys × derating); cell caps (hosted demand incl. hot standbys); hardware of primaries and standbys; redundancy; AI contention; peripheral attachments (ETH to an active node, shared buses at the driver's cell); ports per cell and interface (ETH incl. the 16 switch ports, shared buses with ASIL groups and bus bandwidth, direct buses); shared-bus trunks; backbone cables (active ends, switch-only node degree ≥ 2); per-flow routes (type able to carry the volume, direct or one Ethernet relay among the k−1 candidate relays, switch at the relay, a cable of the type on every link, one path per pair and type); nominal link capacity (flows of cold standbys silent); chain latency (robust formula); standbys (other cell, cold only if FTTI ≥ recovery); survival in every cell / switch / cable scenario; degraded chain latency; cold-standby capacity per cell failure (pooled per ASIL and per cell); every cost and length; Pareto limits. Beyond the model, as warnings: packing of the activated cold standbys into partitions, link capacity after a failure. main.py runs it on every run (result in the log); exit code 1 on a violation.
- Solution JSON gains `candidate_sites`, `partition_counts`, `flow_routes` (per data flow: instances, cable type, path) and `shared_trunks` (output only).
- Report defect found by the checker and fixed: `comm_link_peak_load` and the volumes in `backbone_routes` counted the flows of cold standbys, which carry nothing in the nominal state (lite failure case: S01–S09 CAN reported 597, nominal 97). Now nominal; routes used only by standby flows stay listed with volume 0.
- Model gap found by the checker (warning only, not changed, user decision pending): link capacity is enforced in the nominal state only. After a failure, with every flow on its first surviving variant (the rule of the degraded latency check), the lite failure design overloads CAN links in 5 scenarios, e.g. failure of cell S01: S09→S25 CAN 215 > 100. Option: lazy capacity cuts per scenario, like the degraded latency cuts.
- `tools/regression_test.py` (`srun -p interactive -c 8 --mem=16G -t 00:45:00 dse/bin/python tools/regression_test.py`): lite architecture, 4 cells, 300 s per solve; nominal 8,012.64 / 67.839 m; growth α 0 / 1 / 2: 8,352.46 / 56.8483, 8,384.394 / 58.695, 8,860.2475 / 63.1326; failures α 0: 10,955.162 / 60.3599 — all proven; the checker must pass every solution; checker self-test: 22 deliberate breakages of the solutions or their configs must each be reported with the broken rule. First full run 2026-10-20: PASSED in 7.5 min (21/21 and 14/14 detected). Outputs in results/regression/ (overwritten each run, summary.txt).

### 16.10 Link capacity after a failure (2026-10-20 evening, user decision)
- Gap found by the checker: link capacity was enforced in the nominal state only; after a failure the traffic moved to the standbys could exceed the cables (lite failure design: the failure of S01 put 215 on the S09→S25 CAN link of capacity 100; 5 scenarios overloaded).
- Model: in every failure scenario the traffic that keeps running — the alive flows of primaries and hot standbys, plus per data flow its first surviving variant (the variant rule of the degraded latency check, which activates the cold standbys it needs) — must fit the remaining cables (capacity × count per direction and type; a failed cable pair/type carries nothing). Lazy cuts in the callback; extra cables can always repair it, so it never rejects a placement. The repair heuristic sizes its cable counts for the post-failure loads as well.
- Failure cuts made strictly valid at the same time: the reason a flow variant is dead is now its whole configuration (both endpoint variables, the cable-type choice, the path) instead of the path variable alone, in the survival no-good and in the degraded latency cut; the nominal capacity cut counts a standby flow only while that standby is hot. Before, a design differing only in the cable type of a flow or in the attachment of a device could in rare cases be cut off, so proven optima were not strictly guaranteed with failures on.
- Lite failure case (4 cells, α = 0): proven 10,961.42 / 58.64 m (10,955.16 / 60.36 m without the rule): 3 units (S01 36 primaries, S09 10, S17 4), 36 cold standbys (S09 28, S01 8), backbone S01–S09 FlexRay + two SPI links instead of two CAN links; phases 94 s + 83 s (62 s + 16 s before); 20,241 lazy cuts, 6,816 failure cuts. Checker OK (687 checks in 21 rules). Regression expectation updated.
- Job 709 (failure curve, running since 14:57) still uses the model without this rule and without start solutions: its curve is the variant without post-failure capacity.
- Regression after 16.10: PASSED (nominal and growth cases unchanged; failure case 10,961.423 / 58.6409 proven, 167 s; checker self-test 22/22 and 14/14).
- Job 708 finished (10 cells, no failures, 1,800 s + 600 s per level, model before 16.10 — which does not affect runs without failures): α 0 → 7,961.27 (3 units, 6 partitions, proven), 0.5 → 7,961.27 (proven), 1.0 → 7,989.52 (proven), 1.5 → 8,000.29 (proven), 2.0 → 8,448.98 (4 units, 7 partitions, gap 0.61 %), 2.5 → 9,068.80 (5 units, 8 partitions, gap 4.8 %, cable gap 34 %), 3.0 → no design within 1,800 s (the provisional chain budgets, as job 657). Checker OK on all six (old JSON format: per-flow checks limited where a site pair has several cable types). results/curve_nofail_708/cost_vs_growth.pdf.
- Small fixes: visualize_growth_curve wrote its .dat over a non-.pdf figure name; the growth sweep's stop message no longer claims "capacity exhausted" (it cannot tell infeasible from time limit).

### 16.11 Bus lines, step (a): devices on multi-node buses (2026-10-20 evening, user decision: do all three bus points)
- Decisions (user): (1) the two directions of a bus share its bit rate; (2) multi-node buses — several units and devices on one CAN / LIN / FlexRay line; (3) load-dependent latency (bus arbitration, switch port queues). Implemented in steps (a) devices on lines, (b) unit-to-unit traffic over lines, (c) latency; solve time measured after every step on 10 cells (references: no failures = job 708 α 0, 134 s + 155 s; failures = job 795, model a4bcf7e).
- Model (a): per bus type up to `buses.json: backbone.bus_lines` lines (provisional CAN 4, LIN 3, FlexRay 2); line variables: open, length, ASIL group (high = devices of ASIL ≥ 3 software), unit members, device members. Every bus device with software sits on exactly one line of its type; the unit of each component driving it is a member, and with failures on also the unit of that component's standby (the device stays reachable when the primary's cell fails — a dual-access bus); members are units that exist; one ASIL group per line; the device volumes of a line share its bit rate; one port per line membership (+ point-to-point cables of that type). Cable length of a line = exact Steiner tree of its members in the complete graph over the candidate sites and all devices with the channel cable lengths (Dreyfus–Wagner, `optimizer_utils.steiner_tree`; exact, hence monotone, so the lazy cut len ≥ ST(S)·(Σ members − |S| + 1) is valid); static bounds: pairwise distances and half the sum of each member's distance to its nearest possible co-member (Euler tour); symmetry breaking: lines open in order, the k-th device of a type only on lines 1..k. Replaces the shared-bus attachments, trunks and per-device home-run cables.
- Chains: a device on a line reaches its unit in one bus crossing (kbit / bit rate + hop bound, robust like any crossing), added at the chain's first (bus sensor) and last (bus actuator) component, nominal and degraded; reported as `chain_latency[*].device_bus_ms`.
- Report/tools: `solution.buses` [{id, type, units, devices, asil_group, length_m, steiner_m, tree, device_load}]; the checker re-checks lines with its own Steiner implementation (classic rooted Dreyfus–Wagner; equal to the optimiser's to 1e-15 on 150 random sets); diagram lists bus devices under every member unit with the line; `solution_paths.py` routes the line trees.
- Lite results (a): nominal 7,828.42 / 54.71 m proven (8,012.64 / 67.84 m with the old trunk model); CAN1 = S01 + all four CAN actuators (5.44 m); LIN lights and climate on separate lines (different ASIL groups).
- Defect found on the way (lexicographic solve, affects every model with bus lines): Gurobi completes a start solution's continuous variables by an LP; the bus lengths then dropped below their Steiner trees because callback cuts do not survive an optimize call, so the phase-2 start (the phase-1 design) was rejected and, when phase 2 found nothing else, the level was reported as "no design" (lite growth α = 2 lost its 8,743.42 design; diagnosed with a cut-site trace: the rejecting cut was the Steiner cut). Fix: Steiner cuts are kept as model constraints between phases and re-solves, the trees of a sweep's start solution are added up front, phase 2 starts explicitly from the phase-1 design, and a phase 2 without any solution keeps the phase-1 design (phase status NO_SOLUTION).

### 16.11 (cont.) Step (b): unit-to-unit traffic over bus lines (2026-10-20 night / 10-21)
- Model: bus types (CAN, LIN, FlexRay) no longer have point-to-point cables between units; a flow of a bus type between two units takes one line both units belong to (one hop; candidate path p = line p; one line per unit pair and type), static `sel <= unit membership` and `line length >= distance(u, v) * sel` (the bus counterpart of the path-link cut); a line is opened for devices or for unit traffic. Capacity of a line = its devices + the unit traffic of both directions (lazy cuts, nominal and after every failure); a line failure is a cable-class scenario that kills the traffic on it. Repair heuristic: a bus-type flow takes the first line both units are on, else moves to Ethernet if allowed. Report: `backbone_routes[*].line`, `flow_routes[*].line`, `buses[*].traffic_load`, `bus_line_limit_reached`; checker: route on a line with both members, line capacity (devices + traffic), line failures; diagram: a bar over the member units of a shared line.
- Fixes found on the way: phase-2 start rejected by a too tight lexicographic tolerance (1e-9 relative; bus lengths and rounded integers are recomputed by Gurobi within its tolerances) → tolerance max(0.01, 1e-6 relative), far below the 1e-4 optimality gap; a crash when phase 2 kept the phase-1 design (log line with an empty gap) → fixed; checker self-test mutation for cables skipped line routes → new mutation (unit taken off the line that carries its traffic).
- Speed: with 4/3/2 interchangeable lines (CAN/LIN/FlexRay) none of the lite cases was proven within 300 s (step (a): all proven in 1–3 min); extra exact Steiner bounds (device groups with unit pairs, unit triples) halved the nominal cost phase only. Diagnosis by experiment: the same lite nominal case with 1/2/1 lines is proven in 20 s + 28 s with the identical optimum 7,828.65 / 54.47 m (4/3/2: 133 s + cable phase unproven): empty lines of one type are interchangeable and the search enumerates their renumberings.

### 16.12 Open decision: number of bus lines (to discuss, 2026-10-21)
- Deferred by the user (2026-10-21): the automatic determination is discussed later, possibly together with the data pass; until then 1/2/1 stays. Next: finish step (c).
- Now: `bus_lines` CAN 1, LIN 2, FlexRay 1 set by hand (the counts follow the devices, not the cells: four CAN actuators in one ASIL group fit one line; LIN needs two lines for the two ASIL groups; FlexRay has no devices). A run warns when every line of a type is in use (`bus_line_limit_reached`), which then means more lines might give a cheaper design.
- User's view (agreed): a hand-set maximum is arbitrary; the optimiser should choose the number of lines. Proposed: (1) an automatic upper bound from the data, large enough never to bind (devices per type + enough lines for unit traffic); (2) remove the slowdown by a canonical identity of the lines instead of a small limit — lines ordered by their first member (devices first, then units): `member i on line k+1 ⇒ line k has a member with index ≤ i` (implied by lexicographic ordering of the membership vectors, hence valid: excludes only renumberings) together with the existing device rule; Steiner bounds only on lines that can hold devices. Risk: at 33 cells the pair × line routing variables grow with the bound; to be measured.

### 16.13 Step (c): load-dependent latency (2026-10-21)
- A crossing of a routed flow costs per hop its own transmission (message kbit / bit rate), the per-hop bound of the type and the queue in front of it: every other message on the same resource is sent once before it (no priorities assumed). Resources: a directed point-to-point cable (the output port of the sender or switch; parallel cables are not credited, i.e. one queue per direction — conservative) or a bus line (both directions, unit traffic and the devices of the line). Messages: the nominal traffic (not the flows of cold standbys); after a failure the traffic that keeps running plus the activated variants. The end devices of a chain (bus sensor first, bus actuator last) cross their line with its queue. Robust latency multiplies the whole crossing (own + queue) by the safety factor and adds the synchronisation and platform constants per crossing, as before.
- Cuts: nominal and degraded chain cuts get, besides the own crossing of each flow, one term per message sharing a resource with it, (kbit / rate) * [both there], lower-bounded by ind_t + ind_m − 1 (device messages: their line-membership variable); valid for every solution. One pass per failure scenario now yields the carried traffic and the picked variants (`scenario_states`), used by the post-failure capacity and the degraded latency, also in the repair heuristic. Shared helpers in optimizer_utils (`route_resources`, `resource_messages`, `crossing_ms`, `device_ms`, …) feed the callback, the chain report and the failure report; the checker re-implements the bound independently.
- Bus utilization limit `buses.json: backbone.bus_max_utilization` = 0.7 (provisional; the queue bound assumes a bus that is not saturated): devices + unit traffic of a line, nominal and after a failure, stay below 70 % of its bit rate.
- Expected effect with the provisional data: message sizes are large (SC links 10 Mbit/s at 10 Hz = 1,000 kbit per message; camera 80 Mbit/s at 30 Hz = 2,667 kbit), so queues add several ms per shared hop; budgets may bind much earlier. To be re-read after the data pass.
- Regression time limit raised to 900 s per solve (phase 2 at least 120 s): with (b) some lite cases were not proven within 300 s.
- Committed 2026-10-21 (bus lines (a)-(c)) with known limits: the solver is much slower with (b) and (c) (lite growth α 1 not proven within 900 s; 10 cells (b) without failures 7,851.77, gap 0.9 % after 30 min where (a) proved 7,785.08 in 293 s), and the lite failure case found no design within 900 s even with chain budgets × 3, so the failure side of (c) is not yet verified by a checked solution. Next (user, 2026-10-21): performance first — repair heuristic that keeps queues short (spread traffic over Ethernet and other paths), canonical line identity / automatic line count (16.12) — then the failure-model extensions (zone failures, redundant device links, lost-function metric).
- Diagnosis of the lite failure case (2026-10-21, jobs 830 and 873). The callback now counts rejections and cuts per check (`[callback]` lines every 60 s and after each phase, `solve_info['callback_stats']`). Normal budgets (830): 668 candidates, all rejected; the few that reached the repair failed at chain latency. Every chain budget and degraded bound × 100 (873): first design after 1 s, 11,206.53 after 900 s (gap 4.06 %, cable phase gap 19 %), checker OK (786 checks, 23 rules): survival, capacities and bus lines are jointly satisfiable, the latency rule is what blocks. That design (chosen without regard to latency) is 4–8 × over the normal budgets (steering 152.7 / 20 ms): (1) software messages routed over the single CAN line (1,000 kbit at 100 Mbit/s = 10 ms each), so every CAN actuator crossing waits 44 ms instead of 5.6 ms; (2) Ethernet crossings cost 14–25 ms, each waiting for 10–20 other 1,000-kbit messages on the same cable direction. With the robust constants a 10 ms positioning chain leaves room for at most one unit-to-unit crossing on an otherwise empty cable. A design within the normal budgets probably exists (chain primaries on one unit, standbys on another, no software traffic on the CAN line; not yet shown), but the solver learns latency only from rejected candidates, each cut excluding one combination. Remedies: a valid placement-level chain bound in the model itself (unit crossings × minimum crossing time + end-device crossings ≤ budget), realistic message sizes (data pass, §17), priority classes (user decision; so far "no priorities").

### 16.14 Chain bound in the model, data pass, priorities (2026-10-21, user decisions)
- Chain latency bound in the model itself (valid; the exact rule with queues stays in the callback): a chain flow whose ends are on different sites costs at least its own robust crossing on its fastest eligible type; `cross[t] >= src_j - dst_j`. After the failure of cell c, for chains whose flows all join two critical SCs (survival guarantees such a chain is never broken): a flow a -> b with the primary of a at c crosses for sure when the standby of a is on neither instance of b (`z >= x[a,c] + 2 r[a,j] - x[b,j] - r[b,j] - 2`, and with a, b exchanged); bound against the degraded budget. Alone it did not help with the old data (job 880: 819 of 819 candidates rejected by chain latency), because the queues of the 1,000-kbit messages dominated.
- Data pass for message sizes and bit rates (user: "make the data realistic"): values and sources in §17 and in the configs (`data_source`, `msg_source`; estimates marked). New architecture field `msg_kbit` (size of one output message of a component, kbit; with `rate_hz`); loader and checker read it, without it a link keeps the old default (10 Mbit/s).
- Priorities (user, 2026-10-21: "as in real vehicles"; `buses.json: backbone.priorities`, default on): fixed-priority, non-preemptive queues as on CAN (identifier arbitration) and Ethernet (802.1Q classes). A chain message gets the tightest budget of its chains (deadline-monotonic), standby copies that of the base flow, end devices that of the chain; all other traffic last. A message waits for every message of equal or higher priority once and for one frame of lower priority at most (`cable_types.frame_kbit`). Cuts: equal/higher-priority terms as before, plus one blocking term for the lower-priority message with the longest frame (valid); repair heuristic and checker use the same queue. FlexRay and LIN are time-triggered in reality; here they keep the generic queue plus their per-hop bound (conservative simplification).
- First results (jobs 905/906, lite, 4 cells): nominal Pareto run, 5 points all proven (cost 8,101.04 ... 10,142.50, cable 56.29 ... 46.97), checker OK on all 5. Failure case (all uncertainty blocks) alpha 0: 11,205.69 proven in about 30 s, cable phase proven — before, no design in 900 s. alpha 0.5: 11,722.40 not proven in 900 s (gap 4.4 %, cable gap 19.9 %); now the cold-standby capacity check rejects 98 % of the candidates (8,134 of 8,320), not latency: next performance target. Job 906 hit its 30 min wall time in alpha 1 (wall time too short for a 7-level sweep), so no checked failure solution from it; regression (job 923) run with the new data for that.
- Regression with the new data and priorities (jobs 923/924): all four cases proven and checked in about 1.5 min (before: up to an hour); expected values updated in `tools/regression_test.py` (nominal 8,101.0385 / 56.2920; growth alpha 0: 8,491.7525 / 48.9631, alpha 1: 8,531.7405 / 52.5428; failures 11,205.688 / 58.2231); checker self-test 25 of 25 (failures) and 16 of 16 (nominal) broken solutions detected; REGRESSION PASSED.

### 16.15 Cold-standby capacity in the model (2026-10-21, user: "yap")
- Committed state before this step: 6207729 (data pass, priorities, chain bound; regression passed).
- Problem: in the failure sweep from alpha 0.5 the lazy cold-standby capacity check rejected 98 % of the candidates (8,134 of 8,320): the solver placed standbys without knowing whether the host has room once they wake up, and each cut excluded one combination only.
- Constraint 1h: after the failure of cell c, every cell j holds its load, its hot standbys and the cold standbys activated there, per ASIL within its partitions and per unit within the site caps. act[i,c,j] >= x[i,c] + r[i,j] - 1 - hot[i] (continuous, exact at integer points); hosted load per (cell, ASIL, resource) as one variable so each (c, j) constraint stays short. Size: lite 540 constraints; at 33 cells about cold critical SCs x 33 x 32 activation variables (to be measured). The callback check stays as a safety net.
- Regression (job 929): all four optima unchanged (valid), checks OK, self-test 25/25 and 16/16; the lite failure case no longer shows standby-capacity rejections (94 s instead of 58 s at alpha 0: larger model). Full failure sweep (alpha 0 ... 3): job 930.
- Full sweep job 930 stopped by the user (2026-10-21: tests use one chosen alpha, sweeps only for the paper). Instead the regression got a failure case at alpha 1 (`failures_a1`, expected 11,663.795 / 54.4529, proven in the sweep). Job 938: all five cases proven and checked (failures_a1 in 523 s), self-test 25/25 and 16/16, REGRESSION PASSED.

### 16.16 Lost-function metric; future work (2026-10-22, user decisions)
- Future work (user, 2026-10-22): zone (common-cause) failures and the automatic number of bus lines (16.12) are not done now; they go to the future-work list of the paper.
- Committed state before this step: 03d90e8 (cold-standby capacity in the model, failure test at alpha 1).
- Lost-function metric (reported, not a constraint): per single-failure scenario, the non-critical components (no standby) that become unavailable: their cell failed, a bus device of theirs is on the failed line, a command to an Ethernet actuator of theirs has no surviving route, or an input is not delivered (no surviving variant, or its source component lost; transitive over the inputs). `failure_scenarios[*].lost_functions` lists them; `lost_functions` in the solution gives non_critical, scenarios, worst (with worst_scenario) and mean per failure. The checker recomputes every list independently and the summary; regression mutation `m_lost` (a lost function dropped from a scenario) must be reported.
- Second data pass (2026-10-22): per-hop bounds as in §17 (ETH 0.015 ms, FlexRay 5 ms), SPI 9 Mbit/s (HG1120 brochure: SPI 2-9 Mbit/s, data rates 100/600 Hz), V2X CAM 700 B at 10 Hz (C2C-CC TR 2052: 200 to >700 B; ETSI EN 302 637-2: 1-10 Hz), audio 1.4112 Mbit/s (IEC 60908). No reputable source found yet for: cable cost/weight per metre by type, body/comfort message periods, IMU/GNSS message sizes, CAN/LIN per-hop bounds (stay marked as estimates). Regression rerun for the new expected values: job 953.
- Cost model in USD applied (2026-10-22): see §17 F. Connector prices (USD 8–20 per sealed pair) and the harness factor ×2 come from supplier figures (low confidence); Ethernet cable per metre is an estimate (no bulk price found). Regression rerun with the new costs: job 956.

### 16.17 Device link redundancy (2026-10-22, user decisions)
- Why: the failure model guaranteed a running instance of every critical component, but not that the instance still reaches its devices (an IMU wired only to the failed primary's unit, a CAN actuator whose only line fails). Closing this makes the fail-operational claim hold on the device side and makes the design pay for it.
- Directly wired devices (IMU on SPI, GNSS on UART) of critical software: a second cable from the device to the unit of the standby, with a port there (cost, length, ports in the model, the report and the checker). Real devices offer the second port (HG1120: SPI and RS-422; GNSS receivers: several ports).
- Bus devices of critical software (steering, motor, brakes on CAN; ASIL-3 lights on LIN): two lanes = two lines of the type, each with the units of the primary and the standby (as dual-channel fail-operational steering / brake actuators: two windings, two controllers, two communication channels; IEEE Access paper on dual-winding EPS, SAE 2017-01-1485). Every lane must meet the chain budget on its own (after a failure any lane may be the one left): the end-device crossing counts with its slowest surviving lane; a line failure is survived by the other lane (no longer out of scope for these devices). Line limits raised to CAN 2, LIN 3 (HVAC, ASIL 2, keeps one LOW line). Line symmetry rule generalised: numbering lines by first use, a device may use the lines its predecessors opened plus as many new ones as it has lanes.
- Cameras and lidars (user, 2026-10-22: leave them): no change. The element a camera / lidar is attached to counts as part of the device (its failure is out of scope); every other single failure must still let the data reach a running driver instance (already enforced by the survival rule). A camera has a single port, so dual homing is not possible; a redundant network path after the first switch (IEEE 802.1CB) is already what the survival rule demands.
- Checker: two distinct lanes for devices of critical software with both instances' units on each lane; the standby's own cable to directly wired devices in costs and ports; slowest-surviving-lane latency; lost functions and degraded latency treat only single-lane devices as lost with their line. Regression mutation `m_lane` (a device loses its second lane).

### 16.18 Pareto axes: decided 2026-10-23 (cost x robustness radius ρ; history below)
- Discussed at length with the user, no decision. Agreed so far: cost is one axis; α is a scenario parameter, not an objective; a Pareto front is the trade-off of independent objectives, not a parameter sweep; robustness is more than growth, it includes how much failure a design tolerates. ITSC's cost–cable Pareto is not the paper's point (and cable is now cheap). Candidates for a robustness objective, from the literature: reliability / MTTF (Streichert, Glaß et al. 2007; Glaß et al. DATE 2009, ICCAD 2010), tolerated failures / QoS(f) = share of safety-critical applications running after f failures (Weiss, Weichslgartner, Reimann, Steinhorst, DATE 2020), robustness as expected extra cost of design revisions (Lukasiewycz, Glaß, Teich, DATE 2010), objective uncertainty as an interval (Graf et al., DAC 2015), price of robustness / uncertainty budget Γ (Bertsimas & Sim 2004). Proposed but not accepted: a single load margin r (software and traffic × (1+r) under single failures). Open question: the measure of failure tolerance (number of simultaneous failures, zone failure, or share of functions surviving per scenario) and whether load margin is a separate axis. To be decided before the paper experiments.
- Sensitivity analysis (user, 2026-10-23, noted for the paper experiments): every uncertainty parameter without a source (software margins δ, AI margin, network safety factor σ, platform overhead, thermal derating, AI contention limit, growth profiles) is halved and doubled; report the change of cost and topology (archetype). Also agreed: δ = measurement uncertainty of today's demand (small, measured), α = OTA growth (scenario); the formula demand × (1+δ) × (1+α·g) stays, the meaning of δ changes (no growth inside δ).
- Sources for the uncertainty margins (research 2026-10-23, one agent, 12 searches / 8 fetches): software execution-time variability: Apollo 5.5 worst/mean end-to-end 203/74 ms (2.8×), Autoware 1.12 210/102 ms (2.1×) (Liu, Wang, Zhao, "COLA", arXiv 2305.07147, 2023, preprint, CARLA simulation; low confidence); DNN inference jitter: range/mean YOLOv3 33 %, Faster R-CNN 31 %, SSD-MobileNetv2 49 %, Deeplabv3 13 %, LaneNet 344 % ("Understanding Time Variations of DNN Inference in Autonomous Driving", arXiv 2209.05487, 2022, Table I, Jetson Xavier; low confidence); middleware: ROS 2 up to +50 % over raw DDS, RELIABLE +15 % at p95 (Kronauer et al., IEEE MFI 2021; relative only); thermal: AEC-Q100 ambient grades G0 150 / G1 125 / G2 105 / G3 85 °C (Renesas table), Jetson AGX Orin throttles between 99 and 105 °C junction (NVIDIA thermal design guide); accelerator contention: contention-unaware mapping loses up to 70 % performance (Dagli, Belviranli, HaX-CoNN, PPoPP 2024). Not found: network jitter factor, absolute per-hop middleware latency, memory variability, per-location in-vehicle ambient temperatures (ISO 16750-4 not reachable), 2-vs-3-model GPU slowdown. Unread leads: Kramer et al. WATERS 2015 Table V (WCET/ACET factors), Alcon et al. RTAS 2020 / RTS 2023, Lin et al. ASPLOS 2018.
- Applied 2026-10-23 (user: use these values for now, note the rest): ai_margin 0.33, network_safety_factor 1.0, platform 2 ms kept (motivated), thermal classes mapped to AEC-Q100 grades, ASIL margins kept as assumption; sources and provisional status in the configs (`_source`). Regression expectations to be redone (job 1001).
- **Decision (2026-10-23, user):** two objectives, one Pareto front: f1 = total cost (USD; partitions, sites, hardware, ports, cables per metre), f2 = the robustness radius ρ. Cable length is no longer an objective: it stays inside the cost and is reported per solution together with the cable weight (weight_per_meter from buses.json, currently unused, to be reported). Formalisation (robust optimisation, Bertsimas & Sim 2004 "price of robustness"; Ben-Tal, El Ghaoui, Nemirovski 2009): every requirement is a constraint g_k(x, ξ) ≤ 0 under uncertain ξ in a set Ξ(ρ) that grows with ρ; an architecture's contribution to uncertainty is the smallness of the largest ρ it tolerates, r(x) = sup{ρ : g_k(x, ξ) ≤ 0 ∀ξ ∈ Ξ(ρ), ∀k}. One common ρ scales all uncertainty margins of uncertainty.json together: software demand ×(1+ρ) (today's α), per-hop network latency ×(1+ρ) (today's network_safety_factor), AI inference margin ×(1+ρ); failures are not scaled: the single-failure fail-operational constraints hold at every ρ. The front is the curve cost(ρ) from the existing growth-sweep mechanism (each point = min cost under Ξ(ρ); monotone constraints make each point Pareto-optimal, no ε-constraint needed). Reported per point, not optimised: expected function loss over single failures (lost_functions weighted by ASIL and by the FIT-based scenario probability, FIT values to be sourced per unit class), cable length and weight. Rejected: three separate radii (three 2-D fronts, as in the ITSC paper's style) as too complex; lost functions or load margin as objectives. This answers the proposal's WP V.1-1 ("architecture that minimises its contribution to uncertainties; sources: system failures and latency induced by topology") with failures as constraints and topology latency inside ρ. Implementation: bind the three margins to ρ in uncertainty.json (no new CLI flags), reuse the α sweep, add the weight report and the FIT-based loss report; templates (central / domain / zonal) are evaluated on the same axes.

### 16.19 Real-vehicle scale and the Autoware measurement (2026-10-23, user decision)
- User (2026-10-23): the method is SW/HW co-design to find the best architecture; it is only meaningful if the problem is close to reality. The current 65 clusters with invented demands and chains are not. Priority therefore: first the real software architecture (Autoware node by node, measured; non-AD domains from literature/standards), then run the tool at that scale; speed and the Pareto axes afterwards.
- Research (agent, 14 searches / 8 fetches): premium car 70–100 ECUs, 2,000–3,000 software-related functions, 250–300 driver-visible, ~100 M lines of code (Charette, IEEE Spectrum 2009); up to 70 ECUs, 5 buses, >800 functions (DATE 2010 paper, snippet); per ECU tens to hundreds of SW-Cs, engine ECUs up to ~1,500 runnables, ASIL share of SW-Cs QM 41 %, A 7 %, B 14 %, C 10 %, D 28 %, periods 1–1000 ms (5 ms 31 %, 10 ms 25 %), cause-effect chains of 2–18 runnables (Denzinger, Becker, Ulbrich, "Driverator", arXiv 2605.04837, 2026, preprint with open data doi 10.17877/TUDODATA-2026-MOR12ARE); open Bosch engine-management models (APP4MC/Amalthea, FMTV 2016; Hamann et al., ECRTS 2017); BMW Neue Klasse: 4 central computers + 4 harness zones (BMW press). Not found: whole-vehicle SWC count, functions per zone controller.
- Autoware measurement set up on the cluster (`~/autoware_bench`): Apptainer image `ghcr.io/autowarefoundation/autoware:universe-cuda-jazzy`, sample map + 30 s sample rosbag (TIER IV; 3 lidars, IMU, GNSS, vehicle status; no camera data), ML models from Hugging Face; `measure.sh` launches the logging simulator on an A100 node, replays the bag in a loop and samples per-node CPU/RSS every 2 s plus topic hz/bw of the chain topics; `run.sbatch` submits it. Known limit: no camera topics in the bag, so camera-based nodes idle.
- Chains research (agent, 14 searches / 8 fetches): Autoware sensor-to-command chain (Filter → NDT → EKF → StopFilter → BehaviorPath → BehaviorVelocity → ObstacleAvoidance → ObstacleVelocityLimiter → ObstacleStop → ScenarioSelector → MotionVelocitySmoother → PlanningValidator → Controller → VehicleCmdGate) with measured end-to-end latency on x86: mean 194.7 ms, p99 515.9, max 1437.9 ms, of which 8.1 ms communication and 52.8 ms computation, the rest idle/waiting (Betz et al., arXiv 2404.12683, 2024, preprint); an L4 vehicle with Autoware Universe: perception-to-decision mean 521.9 ms, p99 1036.8 (arXiv 2510.11448, 2025, preprint; split preprocessing 90.8, localization 31.0, perception 280.9, planning 119.2 ms); ASPLOS 2018 limit 100 ms (Lin et al.). Classic functions: airbag inflation ~30 ms (SAE 901136); by-wire bus cycle classes 1–5 / 2–10 / 4–16 ms (JASPAR use cases, IEEE 802.1DG 2020). Not found: FTTI / latency values of brake-/steer-by-wire (SAE 2024-01-2792 paywalled), ESP/ABS, ACC/AEB budgets, WATERS chain statistics, Autoware topic sizes (to come from our own measurement). Consequence: the network share of a chain budget cannot be taken from literature; our own chain measurement (CARET-style, from the rosbag replay) is the source.

### 16.20 Decision: scale up, hybrid search (2026-10-23, user)
- The reviewers' objection was realism (65 clusters, 6 cells, Manhattan, Ethernet only, unsourced parameters), not optimality proofs. The user decided: the method must work at a realistic scale (about 6,000 deployable components as in a premium car's four central computers × ~1,500 runnables each, CARIAD/WATERS; tens of candidate cells). A flat MILP cannot: 6,000 × 50 placement binaries, 15 M activation variables of constraint 1h, exponential branch-and-bound with weak (lazy) LP and massive symmetry; even the lite failure case at growth level 1 stays at 0.27 % gap after 2 h (job 1003).
- Terminology fixed: component = deployable unit = chain element (Autoware: ROS 2 node, 133 measured without / 156 with the camera path; AUTOSAR: SW-C). Chains and links are always at component level; "runnable" is used only when citing a source.
- Chosen approach (hybrid): (1) upper level, architecture search by a multi-objective evolutionary algorithm (NSGA-II class) over placement, standbys, units and network types; the independent checker becomes the evaluator (all rules and costs), the repair heuristic the repair operator; yields the cost–robustness front in one run; scales to thousands of components because evaluation is milliseconds. (2) MILP stays as the proven reference on small instances (the lite cases: GA quality measured against proven optima) and as the lower level inside a unit (partition assignment of the components placed there). Reported honestly: GA results with seeds and repetitions, "within x % of the MILP optimum on the small instances", no global proof at full scale. This matches the proposal (prior work with GA and Z3) and the literature (Lukasiewycz/Glaß/Teich: SAT-based DSE + evolutionary optimisation for this problem class).
- Data at that scale: Autoware measured node by node (this file, 16.19); non-AD components generated from the paper's five domains and the AUTOSAR Application Interfaces function lists with CARIAD distributions (ASIL mix QM 41 / A 7 / B 14 / C 10 / D 28 %, periods, execution times, chain lengths 2–18) and 8-byte CAN signals; cross-domain links from Autoware's vehicle interface (control_cmd, gear/turn/hazard commands, vehicle status). Every value with a `source` field; the generated part is labelled representative.
- Open: FTTI per chain (only airbag ~30 ms and by-wire bus cycles 1–16 ms found); the Pareto axes (16.18); camera-path measurement with the Leo Drive bus bag (3 cameras 720×465 at 16 Hz).

### 16.21 Data package B: the realistic software architecture (2026-10-23, user: all energy here)
- Goal: a source-backed software architecture at real scale (~6,000 deployable components): Autoware measured node by node (156 nodes incl. camera path), sensor/actuator drivers of our suite (8 cameras, 5 lidars, IMU, GNSS, 7 actuators), non-AD domains (body/comfort + occupant safety, powertrain/chassis of a BEV, infotainment/HMI, connectivity) from AUTOSAR Application Interfaces function lists with CARIAD distributions. Output: schema v3 (`swc.json`, `flows.json`, `chains.json`, every value with `source`).
- CARIAD distributions (Denzinger, Becker, Ulbrich, "Shedding Light onto SIL and BSW Constraints ... Driverator", arXiv 2605.04837 / ECRTS 2026, one motion-and-drive ECU on AURIX TC39x; dataset DOI 10.17877/TUDODATA-2026-MOR12ARE, server unreachable 2026-10-23, tables taken from the paper): Table 1 SW-C ASIL share QM 41 / A 7 / B 14 / C 10 / D 28 %, ROM 6–255 kB, RAM 0.5–29 kB per SW-C; Table 2 runnable periods 1 ms 5 %, 5 ms 31 %, 10 ms 25 %, 20 ms 12 %, 50 ms 8 %, 100 ms 10 %, 200 ms 2 %, 500 ms 4 %, 1000 ms 3 %, WCET ranges 15–290 µs (1 ms) up to 1,209–9,200 µs (1000 ms); each SW-C typically one init + one cyclic runnable, more only when needed; Table 3 chains: activation patterns 1 (65 %) / 2 (25 %) / 3 (10 %), runnables per chain 2 (40 %) / 3 (25 %) / 4 (20 %) / 5 (10 %) / 6 (5 %), up to 18; data-age bounds = chain hyperperiod × 1.8–4.9; Table 4: communication mostly between runnables of equal period, otherwise adjacent periods; ~6,000 BSW runnables; engine ECUs a decade ago up to 1,500 runnables.
- Autoware measurement runs so far (jobs 1013–1034): 133/156 nodes, localization initialised from the bag's GNSS pose (/initialpose), kinematic_state 40 Hz, lidar 10 Hz, IMU 28 Hz; per-node processing times recorded (EKF 1.0 / p99 10.6 / max 42 ms; vehicle_cmd_gate 0.23 / 1.7 / 9 ms; map_based_prediction 0.23 / 0.6 / 1.3 ms); planning needs a valid route (goal on a lanelet node ahead; being fixed). Camera path with the Leo Drive bus bag (3 cameras 720×465 bgr8, 15.5 Hz, 1 MB per frame): YOLOX traffic-light containers 265 % CPU and 3.2 GB each, GPU 70–86 % utilised. Node graph (publishers/subscribers per node) dumped by `graph_dump.sh`.
- Non-AD catalogue and generator (2026-10-23): `tools/arch_v3_functions.json` lists 70 function clusters of six non-AD domains, each with its source: Body & Comfort 18 (AUTOSAR_EXP_AIBodyAndComfort R21-11 chapter 4: wiper/washer, mirror, interior light, seat adjustment, central locking, exterior light, ATWS, horn, defrost, terminal clamp, immobilizer, seat climatization, PASE, RKE, body sensors, keypad; HVAC and window control estimated), Occupant Safety 12 (AUTOSAR_EXP_AIOccupantAndPedestrianSafety SWCo 001–401), Chassis 14 (AUTOSAR_EXP_AIChassis 4.2–4.15; brake-by-wire actuation estimated), Powertrain 10 (AUTOSAR_EXP_AIPowertrain 5.1–5.5 reduced to a BEV; traction inverter from Infineon AN1101/AP32540: current loop 50 µs, speed loop 5 ms, FTTI ≤ 60 ms, unintended torque ASIL D; BMS from the NXP HVBMS reference design; charging, thermal, DC/DC estimated), Infotainment 8 (AUTOSAR_CP_EXP_AIHMIMultimediaAndTelematics R25-11; cluster telltales ASIL B; audio IEC 60908 / AVB), Connectivity 8 (TCU, eCall, V2X ETSI, OTA AUTOSAR UCM, central gateway S32G, IdsM, DoIP/UDS, Wi-Fi/BT). The AUTOSAR documents were downloaded (curl, TLS verification off) and read; the three research agents had failed on the PDFs. `tools/build_architecture.py` expands the catalogue with the CARIAD distributions (periods mapped to the nine CARIAD classes, WCET per class, ROM/RAM per ASIL, 1–3 runnables per component, CPU demand = Σ WCET/period × 700 DMIPS of a TC39x-class core, pipeline + period-adjacent links of 8-byte signal frames, chains of Table-3 lengths with bound = hyperperiod × 1.8–4.9) and adds the cross-domain links of Autoware's vehicle interface and the AUTOSAR inter-domain interfaces. Scale 1 = 240 non-AD components (the documents' own decompositions), scale 20 = 4,580 (131k DMIPS); with Autoware's ~156 nodes scale ~25 reaches 6,000. Non-AD ASIL mix at scale 20: QM 55 / A 12 / B 10 / C 9 / D 14 % (CARIAD prior clipped by each function's ASIL). Autoware part: nodes from the topic graph (`ros2 topic info -v`, probe job 1044), processing times / CPU / RSS / topic rates from run 1034; still to be joined.
- First complete build (2026-10-23, scale 25, seed 1): 134 Autoware nodes from the topic graph (458 topics, 372 publisher→subscriber links; probe job 1044) + sensor chains replicated to our suite (5 lidars, 8 cameras) + 5,725 generated non-AD components = about 5,900 components, ~8,900 links, 141 chains (the measured Autoware lidar-to-command chain of 13 nodes with bound 100 ms, plus 140 generated). Non-AD CPU demand 164k DMIPS. Measured per-node processing times / CPU / RSS join from run 1034 once it finishes.
- Run 1034 (sixth measurement) hit the 40 min wall time during the second playback (graph dump via `ros2 node info` took ~10 min); recovered: CPU / RSS table of 60 processes, processing times of 8 nodes (EKF 0.92 / p99 2.3 / max 4.2 ms; vehicle_cmd_gate 0.21 / 1.5 / 2.2; map_based_prediction 0.30 / 1.0 / 1.4; mission_planner 4.3 once; route_selector 6.0 once; evaluators 0.2 / 0.3 / 28 ms). Topic rates / sizes from the earlier run (out2_prev): kinematic_state 40 Hz, lidar 10 Hz, IMU 28 Hz, GNSS 1 Hz, velocity status 29 Hz. Camera run CPU table merged (traffic-light containers). Planning / control processing times still missing: the mission planner rejects the goal ("Failed to plan route", goal lane found but no route); next attempt should take the goal from the lanelet the vehicle is on, a few lanelets downstream via the routing graph, or use the planning simulator's route API.
- `configs/arch_v3/` written (swc.json, flows.json, chains.json; scale 25, seed 1): see counts above; every record with `source`.
- Measurement join (2026-10-23): CPU / RSS resolved per node path from the real-time run (`cpu_by_node.json`: __node:= processes exact, executables matched by name, container loads shared equally among the nodes the launch log loaded into them; camera run merged for the traffic-light nodes): 149 of 151 Autoware nodes have CPU / RSS, total about 2.7 cores and 18 GB RSS without planning / control in motion; GPU during the camera run median/p90 utilisation in `gpu_by_run.json`. Limits: equal container split (CenterPoint's true share unknown: no processing_time topic), planning / control not exercised (no route), 2 nodes unmatched.
- Second independent reference for the non-AD numbers (2026-10-23): Kramer, Ziegenbein, Hamann, "Real World Automotive Benchmarks For Free", WATERS 2015 (Bosch; PDF via the Wayback Machine, original host unreachable): runnables per engine ECU 1,000–1,500, labels 10,000–50,000 (90 % of 1, 2 or 4 bytes; Table I sizes), period mix Table III (10 ms 25 %, 20 ms 25 %, 100 ms 20 %, 1000 ms 4 %, 1 ms 3 %, 50 ms 3 %, 2 ms 2 %, 5 ms 2 %, 200 ms 1 %, angle-synchronous 15 %), ACET per period Table IV (min / avg / max, e.g. 10 ms: 0.21 / 10.09 / 309.87 µs), WCET = ACET × factor Table V (e.g. 10 ms: 1.06–30.03), chains: 70 % one activation pattern, 20 % two, 10 % three; 2–5 runnables per pattern (30 / 40 / 20 / 10 %); end-to-end constraint = sum of the periods of the involved activation patterns. Hamann, Dasari, Kramer, Pressler, Wurst, ECRTS 2017 (open access): the full engine-management model has 1,250 runnables in 21 tasks / ISRs communicating via 10,000 labels on 4 cores at 200 MHz; inter-task communication mostly between equal periods (Table II). The generator now draws runnable times from CARIAD or WATERS (50/50, recorded per runnable), label sizes from WATERS Table I, chain lengths from both, and alternates the two published end-to-end rules (sum of periods / hyperperiod × 1.8–4.9).
- AD timing literature (agent, 14 searches): no per-node planning / control table exists; chain-level: Betz et al. arXiv 2404.12683 (x86: E2E 194.7 / p99 515.9 / max 1437.9 ms, computation 52.8 / 124.3 / 624.0, communication 8.1 / 56.1 / 402.4; RAM ~10 GB), He & Shi arXiv 2510.11448 (Universe on a Xeon vehicle PC: preprocessing 90.8, localization 31.0, perception 280.9, planning 119.2 ms mean; 14.7 cores busy), Autoware Foundation discussion #4612 (real vehicle, VTune: behavior planning ≈ 0.66 core, motion planning 0.24, control 0.58, scenario selector 0.01; whole system 10.7 cores), Abaza et al. arXiv 2311.13333 (Autoware.Auto localization BCET/ACET/WCET, NDT 2.78 / 25.64 / 60.93 ms). These fill the planning / control gap until our own route-enabled run succeeds (jobs 1061 rosbag + AD-API route, 1062 planning simulator).
- Calibration of the generated non-AD numbers against the two real references (2026-10-23): CPU demand now from the average execution time (WATERS ACET, or CARIAD WCET divided by the period class's typical WCET/ACET factor), the worst case kept as `cpu_dmips_worst`; result 3.2 cores of TC39x class per 1,250 runnables (ECRTS 2017 engine ECU: 4 cores at 200 MHz), worst case 33 (WATERS factors 1–30). ROM / RAM per component drawn per SW-C from the CARIAD Table 1 ranges, then scaled (recorded in `source`) so that 1,250 runnables hold the flash / SRAM of one TC39x engine ECU minus the BSW share (16 MB − 4 MB, 6.9 MB − 0.8 MB; Infineon, CARIAD, ECRTS 2017), every value kept inside its ASIL range: the ranges come from CARIAD, the total from the real ECU. Totals at scale 25: 5,725 non-AD components, 9,590 runnables, ~17k DMIPS average / ~177k worst, ROM ~92 MB, RAM ~47 MB.
- FTTI (agent, 14 searches): ISO 26262-1:2018, 3.61: FTTI = "minimum time-span from the occurrence of a fault in a relevant item to a possible occurrence of a hazardous event, if the safety mechanisms are not activated"; FHTI = FDTI + FRTI ≤ FTTI (Infineon AN1001; SAE 2019-01-0110). HARA derivation: kinematics give the time from malfunction to the hazardous event (unintended steering: time to the lane boundary, t ≈ √(2d/a)); SAE 2021-01-0066 ties the FTTI to an acceptable travel distance; AUTOSAR's safety example splits a 500 ms FTT into 200 ms detection + 200 ms reaction + 100 ms buffer. Published values: low beam 500 ms (AUTOSAR EXP_SafetyUseCase, read), switchover ≤ 50 ms (Becker 2014), inverter ≤ 60 ms (Infineon AN1101, unverified), inverter example 200 ms (trade article, low). Not found: steering, braking, BMS, airbag, AEB/ACC, lane keeping (SAE 2024-01-2792 and 2026-01-0801 paywalled; NHTSA DOT HS 812 574 unreadable). The generator writes published values where they exist, kinematic derivations (marked) for EPS 150 / brake 100 / crash 10 / BMS 1,000 ms, and a placeholder 4 × e2e bound for the remaining ASIL ≥ 3 chains (50 published, 88 derived, 252 none of 390 chains).
- Generator refinements (2026-10-23): ACETs per WATERS period class drawn from the Weibull fits of the DTU benchmark fingerprint (Craciunas, Becker, Pop, "Automotive Cause-Effect Chains in the Wild", RTSOPS 2026, github dtu-ese/automotive-cause-effect-chain-benchmark; shape ≈ 1); chain density 4 chains per 100 runnables (same fingerprint; 390 chains now); a shared-vehicle-signal layer (17 source functions: speed, wheel speeds, steering angle, pedals, gear, SoC, clamp / lock / light states, temperatures, crash status, …; each component reads 0–2 of them) giving ~5,600 cross-function links, in the range of published inter-ECU signal counts (≈2,500 luxury car 2008; >7,000 Volvo; IEEE Spectrum 'How software is eating the car'). Scale research (agent): embedded flash >50 MB per vehicle, >1 GB in a high-end car over 100+ ECUs (NXP white paper); ~1 GB software volume expected by 2010 (Pretschner et al., FOSE 2007); DTU ADAS fingerprint 'TrustMotion' (102 tasks, 39 chains) available for the AD side. Totals now: 5,876 components, 14,529 links (419 measured), 390 chains, ~12.6k DMIPS average non-AD demand.
- Planning / control measurement, route problem solved (2026-10-23): both earlier route requests failed for map reasons, not Autoware ones. Rosbag map: the goal node picked along the heading lay in lanelet 66, unreachable from the ego lanelet 2 ("Failed to find a proper route"). Planning-simulator map: the goal yaw came from raw OSM way order (lanelet2 re-orients bounds on load) -> "Goal's footprint exceeds lane". Fix: `~/autoware_bench/lanelet_poses.py` (runs in the container, lanelet2 Python bindings, `loadRobust` because both sample maps carry `road_marking` regulatory elements lanelet2 rejects): `chain` mode picks the longest run of successive road lanelets on the routing graph (planning map: lanelets 102-142-106, 226 m; start at 30 % of the first, goal at 60 % of the last, yaw from the centerline tangent); `goal` mode projects the localized ego pose onto its lanelet (direction-checked) and puts the goal 80 m further along the successors. Autoware's MGRS local frame reproduced with lanelet2's `UtmProjector` at the 100 km grid square's south-west corner (`mgrs_origins.json`; the `mgrs` package returns the centre of the 1 m cell, so the origin is moved back 0.5 m; check against the rosbag map's `local_x/local_y` tags: 0.0 m error over 200 nodes). `measure_psim.sh` and `measure2.sh` now compute the poses at run time. Jobs 1073 (planning simulator) and 1074 (rosbag + perception) submitted with the fix.
- Literature on Autoware planning / control timing (fallback and cross-check for the measured values; agents not used, 12 searches): no paper gives per-node planning / control execution times. Chain level: Betz et al., arXiv 2404.12683 (containerized Autoware, AWSIM, no traffic, AMD EPYC 7313P 16 cores + RTX A6000, ROS 2 Humble) trace the 14-callback chain Filter -> NDT -> EKF -> StopFilter -> BehaviorPathPlanner -> BehaviorVelocityPlanner -> ObstacleAvoidancePlanner -> ObstacleVelocityLimiter -> ObstacleStopPlanner -> ScenarioSelector -> MotionVelocitySmoother -> PlanningValidator -> Controller -> VehicleCmdGate: computation 52.8 ms mean / 624 ms max (bare Linux), end-to-end 194.7 ms mean / 1,438 ms max, of which idle (timer waiting) 133.8 ms; on Ampere Altra (aarch64, 1.5 GHz) computation 135 ms mean / 705 ms max. TIER IV (Autoware discussion #4818): behavior_path_planner + behavior_velocity_planner "can momentarily exceed 100 ms". COLA (arXiv 2305.07147): Autoware pipeline latency 101.7 ms mean / 209.6 ms worst. MMRHP (arXiv 2510.18371): planner processing latency 15.5 ms mean (9-32 ms). Autoware's own temporal-metrics page gives only rates (planning 10 Hz, control 33 Hz, localization 50 Hz, perception 10 Hz), no response-time values. Betz et al., IEEE IV 2023 (DOI 10.1109/iv55152.2023.10186585) and the TUM dissertation (mediatum 1785218) hold per-node tables but are not accessible (paywall / bot wall). Use: if the simulator run gives per-node processing times, those are used (source `measured_psim`); the chain figures above bound the planning-stage total (≈ 50 ms mean, ≈ 100 ms+ peaks with objects) for the sanity check of the sum.
- Generated chain bounds (check 2026-10-23): 390 chains, bound median 75 ms, p10 15 ms, 14 chains ≤ 5 ms (1-ms-period chains, WATERS "sum of the periods" rule; e.g. traction-inverter control 1 ms). Such bounds are only feasible when the chain is co-located, as in the engine ECU they come from; the MILP's intra-cell hop latency must stay below them once the model reads schema v3 (check `sync` in uncertainty.json). Memory after calibration: 15.4 MB ROM per 1,250 runnables (target 12.1; the clamp to the CARIAD per-ASIL ranges stops the scaling), 1.67 runnables per generated component (9,559 runnables in 5,725 components).
- Planning / control measured (job 1073, planning simulator, 2026-10-23): route accepted (lanelets 102-142-106), autonomous mode engaged, vehicle drove 41 s (max 4.3 m/s) of the 165 s recording; `pt_stats.py <bag> moving` restricts the statistics to the driving window (`~/autoware_bench/pt_stats_psim_moving.txt`; whole run in `pt_stats_psim.txt`). Driving-window processing times (mean / p99 / max ms, 10 Hz unless noted): behavior_path_planner 9.0 / 38.5 / 176.8; behavior_velocity_planner 10.5 / 23.3 / 78.9; path_optimizer 12.7 / 27.2 / 48.1; motion_velocity_planner 8.5 / 15.1 / 50.6; elastic_band_smoother 3.6 / 6.8 / 27.2; velocity_smoother 6.7 / 15.9 / 21.2; planning_validator 1.8 / 4.5 / 10.4; controller (lateral MPC + longitudinal PID, 33 Hz) 3.0 / 10.1 / 48; lane_departure_checker 0.9 / 2.8 / 8.5; vehicle_cmd_gate 0.3 / 1.2 / 4.6; map_based_prediction 0.4 / 1.2 / 3.5 (no objects: dummy perception). CPU with a route (`cpu_by_node_psim.json`, from `cpu_by_node_from_ps.py`): behavior_planning container 27.8 % of a core (shared by path + velocity planner), motion_planning container 16.6 % (1.5 GB RSS), control container 18.4 %, velocity_smoother 4.4 %, planning_validator 4.4 %. Comparison with the literature: our chain computation (sum of means, 13 nodes) 31.6 ms vs. Betz et al. 52.8 ms mean for 14 callbacks on an EPYC 7313P at 3.0 GHz (perception is not in their chain), p99 sum 94.9 ms; TIER IV's "behavior planners can exceed 100 ms" matches our maxima (177 / 79 ms). Generator: `load_measurements` reads the simulator table second (rosbag run first), `node_time()` picks a node's total over its module debug topics (largest mean; lateral + longitudinal summed; cyclic-time / pipeline-latency topics excluded), `processing_time_source` records which run; the planning / control CPU entries come from the simulator run (`cpu_run`); chain `AW_lidar_to_command` carries `comp_mean_ms` 31.6, `comp_p99_sum_ms` 94.9, `net_budget_ms` 68.4 (bound 100 ms minus the mean computation). 19 of 151 Autoware nodes now have processing times (was 7).
- Rosbag run with the route (job 1074) was killed from outside: both jobs ran on zits-slurmlogin1 and the planning-simulator script's cleanup (`pkill -f component_container; pkill -f ros2`) killed the rosbag job's nodes 40 s after its route was set (node count 100 -> 41 at 13:42:02; no OOM: 12.4 GB of 64 GB). Both scripts now start the launch with `setsid` and clean up with `pkill -s $LAUNCH` (own session only). Resubmitted alone (job 1075).
- FTTI, published steering value found (2026-10-23): Kaihara, Kimura, Ishihara, Nakamura, "Safety Design of Steering Systems for Autonomous Driving", JTEKT Engineering Journal 1017E (2020), METI projects: for steering failure during automated driving the time from the failure to lane departure (their FTTI, ISO 26262 Part 3) is Tc = 240 ms in the tightest road condition (metropolitan expressway, curve radius 88 m, 100 km/h; expressway conditions give longer times), confirmed on a driving simulator; main -> sub switchover (hot standby) in <= 10 ms. Applied: `SteeringSystemEps` and `SteeringVehicleStabilizing` FTTI 240 ms (`source` cites the paper; replaces the derived 150 ms). Brake-by-wire: SAE 2026-01-0801 (EMB technical safety concept, FTTI-based fault injection) and 2011-01-2357 exist but are paywalled; the derived 100 ms stays marked derived. Agents were not used for this stretch (the standing rule); 14 web searches.
- Non-AD per-domain check (2026-10-23, scale 25): Body & Comfort 1,900 components / 3,733 DMIPS / 35.5 MB ROM (QM 80 %, A 13 %, B 7 %); Occupant Safety 450 / 1,247 DMIPS / 11.0 MB (D 38 %); Chassis 1,000 / 2,525 DMIPS / 22.7 MB (C 29 %, D 30 %); Powertrain 925 / 2,221 DMIPS / 21.7 MB (D 38 %); Infotainment 825 / 1,632 DMIPS / 14.9 MB (QM 79 %); Connectivity 625 / 1,250 DMIPS / 12.1 MB. Limitation at that time (fixed the same day, see the MB-scale memory note below): the Infotainment and Connectivity components used the CARIAD classic-AUTOSAR demand distributions (kB-scale SW-Cs on a TC39x-class core), whereas real head-unit applications (navigation, media, voice) are Linux / Adaptive applications with MB-GB footprints on an application processor; the catalogue functions themselves come from AUTOSAR AI_HMI / AUDIO / GATEWAY / UCM / IdsM / DoIP. For the DSE this affects memory sizing of the infotainment computer only, not the safety-relevant placement.
- FTTI, further searches (2026-10-23, 3 web searches, no agents): no published millisecond FTTI for brake-by-wire (SAE 2026-01-0801 / TRID 2778030 report redundancy handover 'within the FTTI window' without the value; SAE 2019-01-0110 gives the process only), BMS (GB/T 39086-2020 'Functional safety requirements and testing methods for battery management system of electric vehicles' has an informative annex on determining the FTTI, text not accessible) or airbag (patents give ASIL D / 1e-8 per hour targets and a 45 ms deployment time, not an FTTI). The derived values stay marked derived; an industry contact (BMW) quoted 30 ms for the fail-operational switchover, to be confirmed and then applied to `FTTI_FAIL_OPERATIONAL_SWITCHOVER_MS` (now 50 ms, Becker 2014) as 'industry expert, personal communication, 2026'.

Not found: V4H and S32Z2 prices (Orin module / S32G3 prices used as proxies, marked), DRIVE Orin automotive SoC price, LIN counts of TC397 / S32Z2.
- Rosbag run with the route, second attempt (job 1075, 2026-10-23): TIMEOUT at 50 min with only the first 30 s recorded (9 processing-time topics, no planners): the script's `ros2 topic echo --once /localization/kinematic_state` after the mode change had no timeout and the 30 s bag had already ended, so it blocked forever (the earlier runs died for other reasons before reaching it). Fix in `measure2.sh`: the playback loops (`--loop`) so that topics keep flowing while the initial pose, route and autonomous mode are set, every `ros2 topic echo --once` / service call has a `timeout`, and the recording runs 150 s of looped driving with the route before the playback is stopped (no second playback). Resubmitted (`out2_run7_timeout/` keeps the failed run).
