- Rosbag run 7 (job 1075) failed for a script reason: the sample bag is 30 s, the route was set at the end of the playback and a `ros2 topic echo --once /localization/kinematic_state` without timeout then blocked until the 50 min limit (the bag recorded 10 topics before the route; file kept as `pt_stats_run7_partial.txt`, not used). `measure2.sh` fixed: playback at rate 0.5 (60 s wall), goal from `goal_cache.json` (the run-7 goal, lanelets 4 -> 1, route_found), timeouts on every `echo --once`, no second playback (a restart jumps the simulated clock back). Resubmitted; the generator prefers `pt_stats_run7.txt` (rosbag with perception + route) over the planning-simulator values when it exists.
- Rosbag run with the route, third attempt (job 1082, 2026-10-23, looped playback, timeouts): completed in 8 min, route accepted, 220 s recorded, but still only the 9 processing-time topics of run 6 (EKF 1.30 / p99 11.9 / max 35 ms over 8,099 samples; vehicle_cmd_gate 0.27 / 2.1 / 14.7 ms; map_based_prediction 0.36 / 1.3 / 3.3 ms over 228 samples only). Reading: with `--loop` the 30 s bag and `/clock` jump back every pass; the time-synchronised perception (concatenation, tracker, prediction) stops after the first pass (prediction samples end at ~23 s), the behavior planners wait for predicted objects and never plan, so no planning / control processing times come from the rosbag replay. A parallel job (1083, not ours) restarted into the same `out2/` and removed the launch log of 1082; the generator now also reads the kept launch logs (`out2_run7_timeout/`, `out2_prev/`) for the container-to-node mapping. Decision: the planning / control processing times stay from the planning-simulator run (job 1073, route, driving window; source marked), the rosbag runs supply sensing / localization / perception; a rosbag run with planners and real objects would need the route to be set within the first pass of a longer bag (set the goal right after the initial pose, no loop), noted as a later improvement, not pursued now. `pt_stats_run7.txt` now holds the 1082 statistics (same 9 topics, longer samples).
- Build after run 1082 (2026-10-23, scale 25, seed 1): 5,876 components (151 Autoware measured, 5,725 generated), 14,529 links, 390 chains; Autoware CPU / RSS for 150 / 151 nodes (338 % of one EPYC core, 19.2 GB RSS), processing times for 20 nodes (the EKF's debug topic lives in the parent namespace `/localization/pose_twist_fusion_filter/`, now mapped to the node by `NODE_TOPIC_ALIAS`; NDT publishes no processing-time topic in this image, only its CPU share is known). Measured lidar-to-command chain (13 nodes): computation 33.0 ms mean, 107.7 ms p99 sum, network budget 67.0 ms of the 100 ms bound.
- MB-scale memory for the POSIX / Android applications (2026-10-23, user request; 9 web searches / 5 fetches, no agents): 14 of the 16 Infotainment and Connectivity functions carry `mem_mb` in `tools/arch_v3_functions.json` (CentralGateway and IntrusionDetection stay classic AUTOSAR): the ROM / RAM ranges are the memory of the whole function (application or service group), drawn log-uniformly once per function and shared equally by its generated components (`platform: posix`; excluded from the TC39x calibration). Sources: Android 15 CDD 2.5.1 (automotive device: >= 816 / 944 / 1,280 / 1,824 MB RAM per main display by density class, >= 4 GB /data per user) and 3.7 (minimum application memory 80-144 MB per app on large / xlarge screens at 160-280 dpi, up to 384 MB at 480 dpi; 32-96 MB small / normal); navigation: offline map area 500 MB-2 GB, max 2.5 GB per map (Google Maps, 9to5Google 2022 / Daily Dot); voice: on-device ASR models 153-891 MB, 232 MB peak RAM (Parakeet Android SDK), 'a couple hundred MB' per model (Google Speech On-Device); OTA: aktualizr 2-5 MB binary, ~20 MB RAM, 64-256 MB RAM / 128-512 MB storage per primary ECU (HERE OTA Connect guide 2020.7); TCU: whole software in 512 MB RAM / 8 GB eMMC (iWave iW-RainboW-G26, Linux 5.15); V2X: full ITS-G5 / C-V2X stack OBU with 256 MB DDR3 / 512 MB NAND (Unex OBU-352); AGL demo image 2 GB RAM (QEMU / VirtualBox). Not found: per-application figures for cluster HMI (Qt / Kanzi / Populus publish none), DoIP / diagnostics, Wi-Fi / BT stacks, ECall, inter-domain services → marked est. Result (scale 25, seed 1): Infotainment 3,466 MB ROM / 1,649 MB RAM (825 components), Connectivity 1,289 / 467 MB (625), classic domains unchanged (35 / 11 / 23 / 22 MB ROM); non-AD total 4.8 GB ROM / 2.1 GB RAM. The extra random draws shift the seeded sequence: non-AD CPU 12,469 DMIPS (was 12,608), 14526 links, 386 chains, FTTI known for 138.
- Uncertainty-source research (2026-10-24, stopped by the user for cost after ~15 min, 6 agents on the session model; lesson: agents on Sonnet only): two groups completed, four partial. Completed: middleware latency (Betz et al. 2404.12683 Table VI: DDS communication over the 17-callback Autoware chain 8.10 ms mean / 56.05 p99 / 402 max on x86, ≈ 0.5 ms mean per hop; Klüner et al. 2505.02734: Fast DDS 1 kB inter-host over 1 GbE median 0.49 ms, 4 MB 35.4 ms, vSomeIP TCP 7.8 ms; kernel share 0.35 ms; Pannell IEEE 802.1 white paper 2021: store-and-forward bridge 15.7 µs per hop at 100 Mbit/s, 2.0 µs at 1 Gbit/s; Diarra et al. ETFA 2015: gPTP 1 µs over 7 hops) and thermal derating (NVIDIA Jetson Linux r36.2: software throttling at 99 °C, hardware 103 °C, shutdown 105 °C, Tj limit 105 °C; AEC-Q100 grades; NASA/NSWC derating tables: microprocessor frequency derated to 90 % in severe environments; no E/E synthesis paper with location-dependent derating found). Partial transcripts with fetched sources (SaFAD white paper, JTEKT, Becker 1404.6809, KIT, Bakita RTAS 2023, Anderson RTAS 2019, Liu CPT 2024, Ebert & Jones, Charette, McKinsey) are kept under `~/.claude/projects/-hpc-home-okumus/7780ab60-34e8-4bdf-b8a9-85ed9573c796/subagents/workflows/wf_f1fa33e9-bc8/` (journal.jsonl + agent-*.jsonl) for later extraction without new searches. Decision pending with the user: formalisation as Bertsimas-Sim budgeted uncertainty (ρ = share of components at their worst case simultaneously; per-component worst from WATERS/CARIAD/measurement; DTU generator also draws the WATERS WCET factor uniformly, so the generator stays).
- Uncertainty model, final proposal (2026-10-24, awaiting user OK; to be implemented with schema v3, the v2 model stays as is for regression). Principle: every continuous uncertain parameter u = u_nom + ρ·Δu with Δu its sourced reference deviation (Ben-Tal / El Ghaoui / Nemirovski uncertainty level; cost(ρ) = price of robustness, Bertsimas & Sim 2004); ρ = 0 nominal design, ρ = 1 every parameter at its reference deviation; discrete uncertainties are constraints at every ρ. Scaled by ρ: (1) software demand variability, all software alike (no ASIL table, no AI margin): CPU_i(ρ) = avg_i + ρ·(worst_i − avg_i) from the component data (generated: WATERS / CARIAD ACET vs WCET, median worst/avg 8.3; Autoware: measured p99 / mean of the processing time, median 2.9); ρ = 1 is the classic hard real-time assumption (all components at WCET at once); (2) software growth: demand ×(1 + ρ·g_d), g_d = provisioned headroom per domain, level from the NXP / Wards Intelligence OEM survey 2024 (entry vehicles capped at ≤ 50 % headroom, often 30 %; midrange 31-70 %; trade press, low), domain profile from Ebert & Jones 2009 (10-20 % per year); (3) per-hop middleware latency: computed worst-case transmission (fixed) + 0.5 ms + ρ·3.0 ms (Betz et al. 2404.12683 Table VI: DDS communication over 16 hops 8.10 ms mean / 56.05 ms p99 → 0.51 / 3.50 ms per hop; Klüner et al. 2505.02734: Fast DDS 1 kB inter-host median 0.49 ms; both preprints); gPTP sync 1 µs and switch 2 µs per hop at 1 GbE fixed (Diarra et al. ETFA 2015; Pannell IEEE 802.1 2021); σ removed; (4) hardware derating: C·(1 − ρ·ε), ε = 0 cabin, 0.10 elsewhere (NASA NEPP TR04-0600 / NSWC SD-18: microprocessor frequency derated to 90 %), plus a hard placement rule: a unit class only where its temperature grade covers the site (AEC-Q100 grades; Jetson Orin throttles at Tj 99 °C, shutdown 105 °C, not AEC-qualified → cabin only). Constraints at every ρ: single failures (ASIL ≥ C standby; cold only if FTTI ≥ switch-over, 50 ms Becker 2014 / 30 ms industry contact pending / 10 ms hot JTEKT 2020; the ASIL ≥ C rule itself still without source), separation of redundant pairs and runtime monitors (a few components flagged as monitors in the data), accelerator use: proposed as a capacity resource (TOPS demand per component from measurement, capacity per unit class) instead of the unsourced K_max = 2. Removed: ASIL margin table, ai_margin 0.33, network_safety_factor, K_max (if the TOPS capacity is accepted). Still open: FIT values for the expected-loss report (only Julitz et al. 2210.04040 preprint: MCU 1e-4/h, sensor 1e-5/h), source of the ASIL ≥ C fail-operational rule, domain growth profile. Found in the stopped research but not yet used: IEEE Spectrum 2021 (premium cars ≥ 100 M LOC, F-150 150 M, AD ≥ 500 M estimated).
- Uncertainty fixes implemented (2026-10-24, user decisions): (1) accelerator contention limit K = 2 replaced by an accelerator capacity in TOPS, always on like the CPU capacity: HW_ACC is now an integer number of units per cell (hardwares.json: tops 26 = Hailo-8, max_units_per_site 4, assumption), components carry tops_req (rated-equivalent TOPS = GOPs per inference x rate / 0.19; 0.19 = effective / rated TOPS of Jetson AGX Orin in MLPerf Inference v3.1 Edge, ResNet-50 v1.5 offline 6,424 samples/s x 8.2 GOPs = 52.7 of 275 TOPS); constraint 6 = primaries + hot standbys within units x 26 x derating, constraint 1h extended to the TOPS of woken cold standbys; checker: tops_capacity and failure_capacity (TOPS), hardware cost counts units. Old architecture (v2 and lite): CenterPoint 0.84 TOPS (CenterPoint-Pillar ~16 GOPs per sweep at 0.32 m pillars, derived from PillarNeXt CVPR 2023 Table 1 Pillar-B 281 GFLOPs at 0.075 m, 10 Hz), Camera_YOLO 25.39 TOPS (YOLOX-S 26.8 GFLOPs at 640, arXiv 2107.08430, at 960x960 = 60.3 GOPs, 8 cameras at 10 Hz; its DSP requirement replaced by HW_ACC: a DNN runs on the accelerator), TrafficLight 3.58 TOPS (fine detector YOLOX-S 640 + MobileNetV2 classifiers, CVPR 2018, 2 cameras at 10 Hz); all other components no accelerator. Schema v3: lidar_centerpoint 0.84 TOPS and the traffic-light DNN containers (measured in the camera run, 264 % CPU / 3.2 GB each, 1.79 TOPS per camera) added per traffic-light camera (8), inserted between map-based detector and occlusion predictor. (2) Derating only on processing capacity (CPU, accelerator TOPS), no longer on RAM / ROM (optimizer constraints 1d, 2, 1h, repair, pack_partitions, checker); values cabin 1.0, all other areas 0.90 (NASA NEPP TR04-0600, Dowdy & Sharma 2000: frequency of plastic digital microcircuits 100 % protected / 90 % severe; was 0.85-1.0 without source). (3) Critical set (standby after a single failure) = per-component fail_operational flag in the architecture data, ASIL rule only as fallback: no source for "ASIL >= C must be fail-operational" exists; the criterion in the literature is the safe state: a function must be fail-operational when no safe state can be reached by switching it off (ISO 26262:2018 emergency operation / EOTTI; arXiv 2101.07307), which for SAE level 3+ means the driving-task chain, steering, braking, compute and communication (Safety First for Automated Driving 2019; steer-by-wire fail-operational ASIL D, RWTH dissertation 2024). Old architecture: 43 fail-operational (ASIL >= 3 rule gave 47): ADAS chain without monitors, map loaders, mission route, costmap, traffic light; sensor and steering / brake / motor drivers; drivetrain; Sensing preprocessors (ASIL 2) now included, light drivers (ASIL 3) excluded. Schema v3: 68 of 159 Autoware nodes (sensing, localization, object perception, scenario planning, control, MRM; not validators, evaluators, monitors, map, route, traffic light, parking) and 725 generated components (functions SteeringSystemEps, SteeringVehicleStabilizing, BrakeByWireActuation, Esc, VehicleLongitudinalControl, StandStillManager, Epb, DcDcConverter, CentralGateway). (4) Parallel-session repair: a second branch of this session (remote control, 10-23 15:03-15:25 local) had put `cpu_currency` (one DMIPS currency: Autoware host core share x 2.56 x 12,250 DMIPS per A76 core; Arm DMIPS/MHz table) and its own unit classes into partitions.json; my partitions.json writes overwrote that branch's unit classes and the restore removed the currency, while the committed generator (5088b78) read it. Recovered from the transcript into configs/unit_classes.json; the generator now reads it there; unit-class DMIPS aligned to that basis (Orin 229.7 k, V4H 49 k flyer, S32G399A 23.9 k at 2.3 DMIPS/MHz). Regression expectations are no longer valid (critical set, TOPS, derating changed); smoke test job 1099 (lite, --uncertainty, alpha 1, 900 s): 8,833.64 at 0.48 % gap after 160 s (was 7,184.74 proven before the changes).
- Item 6 implemented (2026-10-24): robust crossing latency = computed worst-case transmission and queueing + sync 1 µs + platform overhead, platform = 0.51 ms + rho x (3.50 - 0.51) ms per hop (Betz et al. arXiv 2404.12683, Table VI: DDS over 16 hops 8.10 mean / 56.05 p99 ms; cross-check Klüner et al. 2505.02734: Fast DDS 1 kB over 1 GbE 0.49 ms); rho = 1 for now (per-hop p99), to be bound to the common robustness level; sigma (network_safety_factor) removed from the model and the checker; platform 2.0 ms estimate replaced. The industry 30 ms switch-over is dropped (user): recovery stays 50 ms (Becker 2014). FIT-weighted expected loss: not pursued (user has no FIT data); failures are reported as lost functions per scenario (worst / mean), unweighted. Regression rerun as job 1101 (job 1100 cancelled to avoid mixing the latency models across cases).
- Post-failure chain bound and growth profiles (2026-10-24, user): (a) degraded_bound_ms = the normal bound (net_budget_ms) in chains.json and as the default in the model and the checker; the former 2x had no source and is not justified (the vehicle is still at speed when a failure occurs; emergency operation must itself be safe). (b) Growth profiles: ADAS / Driver / Sensing 1.0 (20 % per year: upper end of Ebert & Jones 2009, ADAS/AD = main driver and almost half of the software market by 2030, McKinsey 2023), all other domains 0.5 (10 % per year: McKinsey 2023 body and energy software 10 % CAGR, total software market > 9 % CAGR); alpha = 1 = ADAS doubled (~3.8 years), others +50 %. Market growth used as the proxy of demand growth. Regression restarted (1101 cancelled, 1102 cancelled after a syntax slip).

The present sw_margin stays until this is decided.

- Robustness level rho implemented (2026-10-24, user decision): uncertainty.json `rho` (levels 0, 0.2, ..., 1.0; growth_years 5; growth_rate_ref 0.20) replaces the alpha sweep. Mapping: growth demand x (1 + 0.20 x profile)^(5 rho) (ADAS / drivers / sensing 20 %/year, others 10 %/year; rho 0.2 = one year; rho 1: ADAS x2.49, others x1.61); sw_margin x (1 + rho x margin) (item 1 still open); robust latency per crossing + 0.51 + rho x 2.99 ms (rho 1 = per-hop p99); derating capacity x (1 - rho x (1 - class value)) (rho 1: 0.90 outside the cabin); failures and separation (redundancy) on for rho > 0, off at rho = 0 (deterministic nominal design); TOPS and CPU capacity always. Objective pair: cost (min) and the robustness radius r(x) = largest rho at which x stays feasible (max), solved by the epsilon-constraint sweep over rho (each level = min cost under U(rho); Bertsimas & Sim price of robustness). The sweep runs whenever any uncertainty block is on (main.py), so single-block runs give the sensitivity curves (e.g. only --robust_latency). solve_info records rho; the checker uses it for the multiplier, derating and latency. Regression cases moved to rho levels (growth [0, 1], failures [0.2], failures_a1 [1.0]); job 1108 gives the new expectations.
- Speed report (2026-10-24, item A.3, for the user; no agents, from the solve logs): size is not the problem on today's instances (lite 4 cells / 50 components with failures: 3,801 rows, 1,824 columns, 1,080 binaries; 10 cells / 65 components: ~8,000 rows, ~4,100 columns, ~3,200 binaries), the weak LP relaxation is: paths / link capacity, chain latency and failure survival are lazy constraints, so most integer candidates are rejected in the callback (lite failures rho 0.2: 238 of 257 candidates; repair fails mostly on 'placement'); B&B explores ~300k nodes per hour on 10 cells and stops at 3.4 % (job 795) / 11.3 % (job 803) gap after 1 h, the lite failure case at alpha 1 needed 2 h for 0.13 % (job a1_long) before the rho changes. At schema-v3 scale (5,876 components, 33 cells) a flat model has ~194k placement binaries and ~0.8M cold-standby activation variables (793 fail-operational components x 33 x 32): not tractable flat. Proposed plan: (1) measure: scaling study 4 / 6 / 10 / 18 / 33 cells x 50 / 65 components at rho 1 with failures (time to first design, to 1 % gap, gap after 1 h) = also experiment D.14; (2) quick wins on the exact model: monotone lower bound across the rho sweep (cost(rho_k+1) >= optimal cost(rho_k), a valid constraint), Gurobi tuning (grbtune on a 10-cell instance; MIPFocus, Cuts, Symmetry, Presolve), stronger lazy cuts (minimal conflicting sets instead of the whole candidate: combinatorial / logic-based Benders cuts, Hooker & Ottosson, Math. Prog. 2003), latency / distance pre-fixing of impossible placements; (3) scale: hierarchical decomposition along the schema-v3 hierarchy (level 1: MILP places ~150 function / Autoware groups on the cells with aggregated demand and traffic; level 2: per cell, components into partitions = small exact packing) followed by MIP-based large neighbourhood search on component level (free one zone or one function with its chain partners, re-solve with Gurobi, keep improvements; RINS, Danna et al. Math. Prog. 2005; local branching, Fischetti & Lodi 2003; LNS, Pisinger & Ropke 2010), every design validated by the checker; quality measured against the flat MILP optimum on the lite / 65-component instances. A multi-objective GA is not needed any more: with the rho sweep every Pareto point is a single-objective (min cost) problem, where LNS on the existing model is the stronger tool; SAT-decoding EAs (Lukasiewycz et al. 2007) remain the reference if the objectives change.

Targets: rho 0 (no failures) proven optimal within 10 min; rho > 0 (failures on) first design within 5 min and gap <= 1 % within 1 h per point; whole rho sweep (6 points) overnight (<= 6 h); lite regression (4 cells) all cases proven within 1 h. 18 / 33 cells: no target, gap after 1 h reported for the scalability plot. - Speed step 1 (2026-10-24, working tree, not committed): two valid model options in configs/buses.json backbone, default false: `upfront_paths` (C-NET-4: for every flow and cell pair, sum of the selected paths over the flow's eligible cable types >= src[u] + dst[v] - 1, the callback's path cut in the model from the start) and `cell_cut_capacity` (C-NET-5: per cell and direction, the volume of the primary flows leaving / entering the cell <= capacity of its cables + bus lines; standby and relayed traffic left out, so a valid relaxation). Observation from the baseline: the 10-cell rho 1 gap is mainly primal (incumbent 10,818 vs. ~9,000 expected from the lite / rho 0.2 optima; bound 8,586), so the variants target early, valid incumbents. Validity check: the proven optima (c10 rho 0.2 = 7,407.87) must not change.

- Plan proposal for C (2026-10-25, to discuss with the user): goal = realistic scale (schema v3: 5,876 components, 33 cells, unit classes) solvable, answering the reviewers (macro-clusters, few ECUs, unrealistic counts). Approach, MILP kept: (C1) loader for schema v3 + checker at component level; (C2) unit classes in the model (per cell: class choice with sourced CPU / TOPS / RAM / ports / cost / ASIL capability instead of identical partitions; the number and kind of ECUs is a result); (C3) deployment groups built from the architecture (function clusters, Autoware subsystems, tight chains; ASIL-pure) as a search decomposition, not a model simplification; (C4) level-A MILP places groups on cells / unit classes with aggregated demand and traffic (the present model with groups as SCs); (C5) level-B per unit: components into partitions / cores (small exact packing); (C6) MIP-based LNS at component level (free a zone or a function with its chain partners, re-solve, keep improvements) so groups can split; every design checked by the checker at component level; (C7) validation: decomposition vs flat MILP optimum on the small instances, scalability 500 / 1,500 / 3,000 / 5,876 components x 10 / 18 / 33 cells, lower bound from the group-level relaxation at full scale; (C8) paper runs: rho sweep on v3, templates central / domain / zonal on the same axes, sensitivity per axis. Estimated 2-3 weeks.
- C plan corrections (2026-10-25, user): (1) no unit classes as decisions: the ECU of a cell is synthesised from the software (partitions, ports, bus lines, hardware features) as in the present model; the archetype (domain / zonal / central) must emerge; configs/unit_classes.json stays reference data only. (2) No grouping that encodes architecture (domain grouping would bias the result toward a domain architecture). (3) No multi-start bias check. Data facts: units unified in schema v3 (CPU DMIPS, RAM / ROM kB, TOPS; Autoware RSS MB -> ram_kb; no MB fields left); open: Autoware ROM not measured (159 nodes), one Autoware node without CPU / RAM measurement. Mandatory co-location by chain budgets is practically absent: network budget per chain min 0.78 ms, p10 12.9 ms, median 73.1 ms; at rho 0 no chain is too tight for one cell crossing (0.51 ms), at rho 1 (3.5 ms per crossing) 3 of 386 chains with 10 of 5,884 components (0.2 %) must stay in one cell -> grouping cannot be justified by the data.
