- Speed step on branch c-scale (2026-10-26): (1) pre-elimination dropped: the chain crossing bound (nominal and per failed cell) is already in the model and cable latencies (µs) cannot exclude any placement; (2) the lazy cuts are already per (flow, path, link / scenario), not whole-candidate no-goods: the weakness is that the LP sees no traffic and learns one combination per rejection (x scenarios); (3) therefore aggregated per-cell inequalities the LP sees at once: `cell_cut_capacity` alone (tag c, jobs 1212-1214; earlier only measured together with upfront_paths) and new `cell_cut_capacity_failures` (C-NET-6: per cell-failure scenario and surviving cell, out / in traffic of surviving primary flows + flows moving to the partner's standby <= capacity of the cables not going to the failed cell; valid relaxation, option in buses.json, default off; tag cf = both options). Benchmark instance c10_rho06 added (hardest sweep point); base run for it = job 1215. All options off in the working tree.
- Seed portfolio (2026-10-26, user: background, low priority): base code + NoRelHeurTime=120 with Seed 1/2/3 on c10_rho06 and c10_rho1, tags seed1-3 (jobs 1231-1236), to quantify the run-to-run variance and the portfolio gain.
- Benders feasibility cuts, version 1 (2026-10-26, branch c-scale, user: implement and see): `benders.py` + option `benders_cuts` in buses.json backbone (default off). For every integer candidate the callback solves a routing LP: path shares f[t, w, p] over the candidate paths of the point-to-point flows (bus-eligible flows left out), link capacity = cables x cap, relay paths need the candidate's switch, chain budgets with transmission + hop times (queueing relaxed). Infeasible -> one Farkas cut lambda^T b(y) >= 0 over comm, hw_use[SWITCH] and the flow activations (linear lower bound src + dst - 1, mu >= 0 keeps it valid) covering all links, flows and chains at once; the per-combination cuts stay. Sign of the certificate verified numerically at the candidate (cut must be violated there). Nominal state only in this version; failure scenarios later. Smoke test: lite rho 0.2 with the checker (job bsmoke).
- Benders v1 validated on the lite instance (2026-10-26): the first version cut off the optimum (7,426 instead of 7,414.53) because of three errors: capacity applied to both directions together (the model is full duplex: per direction), standby-flow activation without the hot condition, and an ambiguous Farkas sign. Rewritten as a phase-1 LP (demand slacks minimised) whose optimal duals give the cut with definite signs (demand rows >= 0, capacity / relay / chain rows <= 0). Rerun: 7,414.59 at gap 0.0039 % (within Gurobi's tolerance of the proven 7,414.5255), cable 60.915 as the regression, checker OK, 91 Benders cuts in 341 candidates. Full benchmark with the option = tag b.
- Findings 2026-10-26 evening: (1) tag cf "proved" lite rho 1 at 8,988.17, above the proven 8,978.25 (pc, regression 1195): the per-failure-scenario cut-set (C-NET-6) is invalid, because a primary flow relayed through the failed cell is lost in the callback's scenario (not rerouted) and carries no load while the inequality counted it; option removed from the model and buses.json. (2) The nominal cell cut-set alone (tag c) is valid (pc contains it and reproduces the optimum) but slower on 10 cells: rho 0.2 proven in 2,747 s instead of 349 s, rho 1 gap 17.7 % and first design after 48 min; it stays off. (3) The first Benders benchmark (jobs 1246-1249) failed at once: the snapshot copied tracked files only and benders.py was untracked; speed_bench now snapshots new, not ignored files as well; resubmitted as tag b.
- Fix-and-optimize improvement loop ("LNS", 2026-10-26, user: try it in parallel): configs/solver.json `lns` block (enabled false by default; time_share 0.5, iter_s 120, free_share 0.3, stop_gap 0.01, seed) read by main.py and handed to optimize(); inside the lexicographic solve, after phase 1 (which then gets (1 - time_share) of the time) and only when the gap is above stop_gap: iterations fix the placement x of a random 70 % of the components to the incumbent (bounds), keep every lazy constraint and cut, re-solve for iter_s, accept improvements; at the end the model is left in the state of the best design; the phase-1 bound is kept for the reported gap. Random neighbourhoods only. Gurobi heuristic variant submitted in parallel: tag heur (NoRelHeurTime=120,RINS=20,Heuristics=0.2). Smoke test of the loop: lite rho 1, 900 s (job lsmoke).
- LNS smoke (lite rho 1, 900 s, phase 1 = 450 s) found no design at all (398 candidates, all rejected), so the loop never started. Cause: with NoRelHeurTime=120 the lite rho 1 instance finds its first design late and erratically (nr tag 554 s, b 195 s, c 364 s, cf 550 s) while without NoRel it takes 7 s (base, mf1): the no-relaxation heuristic helps the 10-cell incumbent search but delays small instances. Noted for the default (maybe size-dependent). LNS measured directly on the benchmark (tag lns, 10 cells + lite, 1 h).
### 16.22 Method survey for part C (2026-10-26, user request; 21 web searches, 1 fetch, no agents)
Exact / OR methods. (1) MILP with lazy constraints = ours; its weakness is the traffic-blind relaxation. (2) Logic-based Benders (Hooker 2007 OR; Lombardi et al. 2010, Benini et al. 2011 Ann OR: MIP allocation + CP scheduling with no-goods for MPSoCs; Karlsson & Rönnberg 2022 avionics: MIP master + CP subproblem, instances up to 54,731 tasks): the principled form of our callback; our Benders v1 (routing LP, Farkas / phase-1 duals) is this. (3) Classical Benders for network design (Magnanti & Wong 1981 Pareto-optimal cuts; hop-constrained survivable design, Botton et al. 2010; degeneracy of flow subproblems, Naoum-Sawaya & Elhedhli 2013): cut strengthening is the known lever. (4) SAT / SMT decoding DSE (Schlichter, Lukasiewycz, Haubelt, Teich 2005-2008; Glaß et al. 2017 handbook chapter; Eder et al. SoSyM 2020 and TUM dissertation 2022: SMT-based E/E topology exploration with a Continental case; CLEO-CoDe 2021: SAT decoding beats plain metaheuristics as constraints grow): feasibility by solver, optimisation by EA; no optimality proof; this is the line the proposal cites. (5) CP-SAT: RunSoC 2.0 (arXiv 2609.01614, automotive tasks to MPSoC partitions, CP-SAT best, feasible to 500 tasks where CBC and a GA fail; memory the binding constraint) and planning / scheduling hybrids: CP suits the partition / core packing subproblem. (6) Column generation / branch-and-price (Althaus et al. 2012 real-time networks: tasks to ECUs with bus and deadlines, lower bounds early; Hoffmann dissertation 2014): exact, but our coupling (latency, failures across cells) makes pricing hard. (7) Matheuristics: MIP-based LNS (Pisinger & Ropke; ACS parallel LNS, Munguía et al. 2018; RINS Danna 2005) = our fix-and-optimize loop; learned LNS neighbourhoods (Sonnerat et al. 2021: 2-37.8x better primal gap on Google MIPs; Addanki et al. 2020; La Rocca et al. 2025). (8) E/E-specific recent: Zaheri & Reuss IV 2025 (ILP, ASIL decomposition, cost vs critical chain time, one case study, no scale data); Näke et al. ITEC-AP 2025 / ARANEA TU Dresden (zonal partitioning as p-median ILP + harness routing as multicommodity-flow ILP; more zones -> less wiring); Stuttgart 2023 (k-means + Dijkstra); a 2024 MILP + discrete-event-simulation loop for off-road E/E (Sensors = MDPI, not citable by our rule); Graf et al. DATE 2014 multi-variant DSE; Chalmers NSGA-II thesis 2017 (250 SWCs x 25 ECUs: premature convergence).
Learning methods. (9) GNN predict-and-search (Han et al. ICLR 2023: GNN predicts variable marginals, MILP searches a trust region around them; primal gap -51 % SCIP / -10 % Gurobi) and neural diving (Nair et al. 2020): exactness kept inside the region, learns from solved instances; the most credible learning route for us because we own an instance generator. (10) RL / GNN for placement and assignment: AlphaChip (Mirhoseini et al. Nature 2021) with the 2023-2024 reproduction dispute (Kahng ISPD 2023, Markov; rebuttal Goldie et al. arXiv 2411.10053) = large compute, contested gains; facility-location RL (SIGSPATIAL 2024: 95 % of solver quality, 1000x faster; UniFL ICML 2026 with size-generalisation guarantees for the uniform case); Joshi et al. 2021: learned policies do not generalise to larger instances without care; Bengio, Lodi, Prouvost EJOR 2021 (survey): ML as a component inside exact methods, not a replacement. (11) RL for HW/SW co-design of accelerators (Chiplet-Gym, SCORCH, Jiang et al. TCAD 2020, CORE 2025; ArchGym 2023: proxy cost models 2,000x faster): parameter-space DSE with simulators, not constrained assignment. (12) LLM-driven DSE (AgentDSE, Agentic Architect, gem5 Co-Pilot 2025, MAHL chiplets 2025, SECDA-DSE): LLM proposes configurations, a simulator or model scores them; and LLM-evolved heuristics (FunSearch 2023, EoH ICML 2024, LLaMEA 2024, G-LNS 2026: co-evolved destroy / repair operators for LNS): a possible way to design our LNS neighbourhoods without hand bias, evaluation burden high.
Assessment for us (scalability, proof, bias): exact at full scale = only via decomposition (Benders / LBBD with CP packing) and strong cuts; learning-augmented exact search (predict-and-search / learned neighbourhoods trained on our generator, validated by the checker, ground truth = proven optima on small instances) is the novelty candidate with a defensible story; end-to-end RL / GA are not (no proof, contested scaling, operator bias). LLM-evolved operators are an optional add-on for the LNS.
- Benchmark round 2 results (2026-10-26, 1 h per instance, 32 cores; all checker OK unless noted). 10 cells rho 1: base 20.3 % / 10,818; nr 1.63 % / 9,091; Benders (b) 7.81 % / 9,174 (first design 215 s, 741/771 candidates still rejected); heur (RINS=20, Heuristics=0.2) 5.59 % / 8,986 (best incumbent of all runs, bound weak); seeds 1/2/3 with nr: 1.52 % / 9,058, 20.3 % / 10,016, 0.59 % / 8,998 -> the seed portfolio (best of 3) meets the 1 % target, single runs do not reliably. 10 cells rho 0.6: every variant 10.5-12 % (base 8,674, b 8,645, heur 8,647, seeds 8,656-8,685, lns 8,658): the bound (~7,735-7,746) is the problem, not the incumbent; no primal method moves it. 10 cells rho 0.2: all proven 7,407.87 (base 349 s, lns 549 s, b 586 s, nr 657 s, heur 1,010 s). Lite rho 1: heur 0.22 %, b 0.35 %, base 0.48 %, lns 0.53 % / 8,988.23, nr 0.76 %. LNS loop itself: rho 1 phase 1 (30 min) ended at 13,529 (a bad incumbent), 15 iterations improved it to 11,729 (8 improvements, 120 s each) but far from the 9,000 others reach: fixing 70 % of a bad placement keeps it bad; rho 0.6: 8,674 -> 8,658 in 15 iterations. Conclusion: Benders v1 (nominal only) helps the first design and the rho 1 gap vs base but less than the no-relaxation heuristic; LNS with random 30 % neighbourhoods is too weak; rho 0.6 needs a stronger bound (failure-scenario Benders cuts or model strengthening), not more search.
- Benders v2 and the seed portfolio (2026-10-26, user: both in parallel). (a) `tools/portfolio.py`: one Slurm job per rho level and seed (configs/solver.json portfolio.seeds = 1, 2, 3) plus a dependent collect job that keeps the cheapest checked design per level (results/<out>/best, cost_vs_rho_portfolio.dat); started on the 10-cell sweep (results/portfolio_c10, jobs 1287-1305, 1 h + 5 min per job). (b) benders.py `failure_lp_cut` + option `benders_failure_cuts`: per candidate and active cell c, a phase-1 LP routes the required post-failure traffic (base flows between two fail-operational components, the candidate's alive variant: primary / standby endpoints not at c, paths and relays not touching c, post-failure chain budgets); infeasible -> dual cut with the base flow's configuration indicator x[i,u_i] + x[k,u_k] + r[i,v_i] + r[k,v_k] - 3 (vacuous for any other configuration, hence valid). Validity smoke: lite rho 0.2 must reproduce 7,414.53 (job bsmoke2); then benchmark tag b2.

### 16.23 Scale evidence: who solves placement + network at ~6,000 components (2026-10-26, user request after rereading the ITSC reviews; 22 web searches, 3 fetches, no agents)
- Automotive / avionics allocation with a network: no published work at thousands of components that also synthesises the network, standbys and chains. Largest exact allocation + scheduling by LBBD in the literature: 10 processors, 129 tasks (survey in Karlsson & Rönnberg 2022, Linköping/Saab, optimization-online 8921). Avionics IMA multi-objective ILP: one real example with 536 tasks (Annighöfer, DASC 2018; I/O restrictions raise the time x10). TUM E/E synthesis MIP (Askaripoor et al. ITSC 2021, IEEE T-IV 2024 "E/E Designer"): time linear in applications, exponential in nodes. SMT DSE with a Continental case (Eder et al. SoSyM 2020) and SAT decoding + EA (Glaß et al. DAC 2009): sizes not in the abstracts. Saab LBBD solves 54,731 tasks / 2,530 messages / 21 modules, but every task is pre-assigned to its module (scheduling only, no placement).
- Methods that reach this scale elsewhere: heuristic co-synthesis with task clustering (COSYN, Dave, Lakshminarayana, Jha, IEEE TVLSI 1999: task graphs with over 1,000 tasks, optimal on the small literature examples); RL device placement of dataflow graphs (Mirhoseini et al. ICLR 2018, grouper + placer: over 80,000 operations; Placeto, NeurIPS 2019: transfers to unseen graphs), objective = measured runtime, no hard constraints of our kind; learned neighbourhood search for million-variable ILPs trained on small instances (Ye et al. ICML 2023; Light-MILPopt ICLR 2024); Benders for location problems (hub location exact up to 500 nodes, Contreras, Cordeau, Laporte OR 2011; Fischetti, Ljubić, Sinnl Mgmt Sci 2017).
- Consequence: nobody proves optimality at this scale; large-scale results are heuristic or matheuristic, with quality shown against exact solutions on small instances and bounds where available. Placement + network + failures + chains at full-vehicle scale is an open gap.

### 16.24 Counterexample-guided loops (LLM / solver proposes, verifier refutes) (2026-10-26, user request; 12 web searches, no agents)
- Pattern = CEGIS: a proposer suggests a candidate, a verifier (Z3, Batfish, MILP, our checker) returns a counterexample, the proposer retries. LLM as proposer: Jha et al. IEEE MILCOM 2023 (GPT-4 + Z3, blocks-world planning, small); Verified Prompt Programming, Mondal et al. HotNets 2023 (GPT-4 + Batfish router configs, 6-10x fewer human prompts); LLM-Modulo position, Kambhampati et al. ICML 2024 (LLMs cannot plan or self-verify, external verifiers must close the loop); Lemur ICLR 2024 (LLM + automated reasoners for program verification, sound calculus); NAACL 2025 (LLMs write Z3 models for planning incl. task allocation, fail on some allocation cases). LLM writes the formal model, solver solves: SatLM NeurIPS 2023, OptiMUS ICML 2024 (MILP with solver feedback). Solver-only: CEGIO, Araújo et al. Sci. Comput. Program. 2018 (SMT counterexamples narrow the search; small problems). LLM evolves code scored by evaluators: FunSearch, EoH, AlphaEvolve 2025 (Borg scheduling heuristic in production). Learning inside Benders: cut classification, Jia & Shen INFORMS J. Optimization 2021.
- For us: the MILP with lazy constraints already is this loop (MILP proposes, callback checker refutes with a cut = logic-based Benders). An LLM proposing whole designs does not scale to ~6,000 components (published cases are small; one counterexample per iteration). Useful forms: (1) the learned proposer of 16.23 item 3 (predict-and-search / learned LNS) with the checker's violations fed back as cuts or penalties; (2) minimal conflict sets (e.g. an unsat core from Z3 over the violated rule) as stronger no-good cuts; (3) LLM-evolved neighbourhood / repair operators scored by the checker (cost: many LLM calls).

### 16.25 Learned search: RL version of 16.23 item 3 (2026-10-26, user decisions; branch learned-lns)
- Decision: the method is the RL form of learned neighbourhood search (Song et al. NeurIPS 2020, Wu et al. NeurIPS 2021): a policy chooses each iteration which components to free (destroy), the exact MILP with its lazy constraints re-solves them with the rest fixed (repair), reward = cost decrease. Correctness never depends on the policy: every design comes out of the MILP and passes tools/check_solution.py. Model: HGT-style heterogeneous attention (type- and relation-specific, with edge features) as the local layer plus global attention over all nodes of a graph (GPS with HGT inside; user concern: a plain GNN stays local). Domain and zone labels are not inputs. Pretraining by imitation (free the components that sit elsewhere than in a good design) from the label data, then PPO with MILP rewards. Initial design of an episode: a short unrestricted MILP run (no hand-written heuristic).
- Implemented so far (supervised predictor, kept as pretraining / start): learn/instances.py (variants: 4-12 cells, rho, chains and components left out, demands +-20 %), learn/gen_data.py (labels: exact model 450 s, checked; Slurm array, at most 8 concurrent Gurobi sessions), learn/features.py, learn/model.py, learn/train.py, learn/search.py, optimizer.py guide (phase 1 within predicted cells, status GUIDED without a bound, guided LNS), main.py hook via configs/solver.json learn.search (off by default). Smoke: lite rho 0.2 guided run reproduced the proven optimum 7,414.53, checker OK. Label data: results/learn/data_v1 (job 1334, 480 variants), keep running (user: also use synthetic data if the user finds some).
- Gurobi licence: academic WLS, nominal 2 concurrent sessions; about 9 ran 1 h without error. RL throughput is bound by concurrent MILP solves, not by GPUs; a higher session limit (Gurobi request or a site licence) would cut training from days to hours.
- Finding (speed logs, 2026-10-26): on 10 cells rho 1 the plain MILP with MIPFocus=1 has its first design after 66 s and 9,020 after 300 s (best known 8,986, +0.4 %), final 8,995.74 after 1 h with gap 15.3 %. The incumbent is easy on 65/10; the bound is the hard part. A primal method (learned or not) cannot show a dramatic gain on 65/10; the gate test is therefore 65 components x 20 and 33 cells at rho 1 against the MILP with default settings and with MIPFocus=1 (training variants have 4-12 cells: also tests generalisation to more cells).
- RL implementation (2026-10-26 evening): learn/features.py (InstanceGraph: static graph once per instance, dynamic state features of the current design added per iteration, vectorised), learn/model.py (PolicyNet: GPS layers with an HGT layer with edge features + global attention; heads placement, standby, destroy, value; Plackett-Luce log probability of the freed set), learn/train.py (pretraining: placement + destroy head as detector of components moved away from a good design), learn/search.py (RLPolicy; learn.search mode 'rl': start design from the placement head as a MIP start, nothing excluded, then the policy picks the freed components), learn/rl.py (episodes as processes, at most 8 Gurobi sessions; PPO with GAE on the GPU), learn/gate.py (gate runs in one job, report: best cost at 5/10/20/30 min, checker). optimizer.py: pick(rng, free_share, design) and observe(before, after) in the improvement loop, init_s / max_iters / final_s, phase 1 without a design continues only until the first design (SolutionLimit 1). Smoke: pretraining on 50 variants (validation: top-1 0.945, moved precision 0.93 on 5 variants); one RL episode on a 12-cell rho 0.8 variant: 10,870 -> 8,786 in 2 of 8 iterations (label of the 450 s exact solve: 8,799); PPO update ran on the GPU.
- Job chain (2026-10-26 ~19:45): labels 1334 (480 variants, throttle 7) -> pretraining 1350 (results/learn/policy_pre.pt) and gate baselines 1351 (milp, milp_mf1 on 20 and 33 cells, 30 min each) and regression 1352 (default path unchanged?) -> RL 1353 (60 rounds x 16 episodes, results/learn/rl_v1) -> gate of the learned search 1354 and report (results/learn/gate_v1/report.txt).

### 16.26 Learned-search findings, base line and targets (2026-10-27, user decisions; branches learned-lns / c-scale)

- Learned search, findings of 2026-10-27 (closes the open items of 16.25; code committed on learned-lns as c4ec5ce, pushed by the user):
  - Steiner bus lengths: the Dreyfus-Wagner recursion is exponential (36 terminals: 27.5 TiB, crash in the callback); above `STEINER_DP_MAX` = 9 terminals `optimizer_utils._steiner_flow` solves an exact directed multi-commodity flow ILP (scipy HiGHS) after the bottleneck-Steiner-distance edge reduction (Duin & Volgenant 1989); identical lengths on 164 terminal sets (max diff 1e-14). The checker has its own flow version (`SolutionCheck.steiner_flow`, > 8 terminals) and tolerates 1e-5 x members on the bus length (IntFeasTol). Regression: all cases pass.
  - The forced `Method=6` (PDHG, set in Feb 2026) made the root relaxation never finish at 20 / 33 cells; removed. Default method: root 9 s (20 cells) / 50 s (33 cells), 65 SWCs, rho 1.
  - Gate (65 SWCs, rho 1, 30 min, Threads 8, results/learn/gate_v2 .. v4): Gurobi alone with MIPFocus=1 reaches 9,002 (20 cells) / 9,029 (33 cells); default parameters 11,486 at 33 cells. Neighbourhood rounds (learned policy, random, whole cells, BFS clusters; `learn/search.py`) do not beat the plain run at 65 SWCs: expected, the rounds are meant for sizes the flat model cannot run. Partition cost is 80 % of the total (7,200 of 9,029): single-component neighbourhoods cannot empty a partition, only whole-cell neighbourhoods can.
  - Start step from the locked network guess (placement fixed, standbys free, repaired for capacity / redundancy / chain gathering / failure balance): infeasible every time. IIS (`start_iis_*.ilp`): four positioning chains with 10-15 ms budgets sit in one cell; after that cell fails, `chain_lb_*_fail_S29` allows one crossing each (3.52 ms <= 4.23 ms left), so all their standbys would have to share one cell, impossible under site_max_partitions 4 / max_per_asil 3. Not solved; the fallback frees the least confident quarter per round, then the full model finds the first design.
  - Accuracy: the 83 % validation top-1 of 16.25 was measured with a perturbed Gurobi design in the input (unperturbed_share 0.3), i.e. the answer was partly in the input. Without a design: 41 % top-1, 81 % top-3, 0 of 44 validation cars fully right; retraining without designs (configs learn.train unperturbed_share 1.0, moved_weight 0; results/learn/policy_nodesign.pt): 41.5 %, no improvement. The network learned the majority cell (1.4 cells per car vs 2.2 in the labels; co-location pairs 96.5 % recall / 58.8 % precision). User: heads C (destroy) and D (value) are dropped; RL stays aside. Discussed, not decided: RL with a cheap rule check as reward (computable from the layout in ms: capacities, redundant pairs apart, chain co-location, failure balance, static chain timing = the model's `chain_lb`, partition + hardware cost = 80-95 % of the cost; not computable without routes: queueing time, bus lines, exact cable cost). Alternative without learning: Gurobi first design piece by piece (chains in groups of 65-100, the rest fixed), rounds free whole cells, no bound at 1,500 / 5,884.
- User decisions (2026-10-27 evening):
  - Order of work: (1) the plain exact model on the 10-cell / 65-SWC base: what v1.1 solves, then the changes made after v1.1 added one at a time; (2) cell count (recommended: 33 for the paper = all candidate sites, 20 for development; not fixed yet); (3) 65 SWCs at that count; (4) grow the components slowly. The schema-v3 loader is built only when a step passes 65 components, not before.
  - Target definition (proposed, to be confirmed): "optimal" = within 1 % of the optimum certified by the solver bound. "Fast": 65 / 10 cells <= 1 % within 1 h per rho level (today 1.63 %, best seed 0.59 %); 65 / 33 cells <= 1 % within a few hours (to measure); 500-5,884 components a checked design with a reported gap overnight (<= 12 h).
  - Consequence: at rho >= 0.6 the gap is a bound problem (the chain-timing, failure-survival and routing rules are lazy and absent from the relaxation: 16.21 benchmark round 2, rho 0.6 all variants 10.5-12 %, three seeds at 8,651-8,652, bound ~7,740 barely above the rho 0.4 optimum 7,408), so 1 % at larger sizes needs model strengthening / cuts, not hours. Two work items: the piece-by-piece builder (designs at scale) and a stronger bound (the certificate).
  - Test level: rho 1 (reference scenario; v1.1 row exists; seed variance shows speed-ups). rho 0.6 only as an extra data point. Cores: 64 per run (Gurobi gains little beyond 32-64; nodes have 128 physical cores).
- Branch state: `master` = v1.1 (2f3b11d). `c-scale` = v1.1 + 65bcc8a (Benders cuts and fix-and-optimize, both off by default; portfolio; v3 units) + the three exact-model fixes applied uncommitted on 2026-10-27 (Method 6 line removed in optimizer.py; optimizer_utils.py and tools/check_solution.py taken from learned-lns = Steiner flow + checker tolerance); learn/ is not on this branch. `learned-lns` = c-scale + learned search WIP (c4ec5ce, on GitHub). `v2`: one old README commit unpushed (ba67f4c). c-scale is not on GitHub yet (no credentials on the node; `git push -u origin c-scale` from the user's terminal).
- Base-line measurements (results/speed/<tag>/c10_rho*.txt, `tools/speed_bench.py report <tags>`; 10 cells, 65 SWCs, failures on, 3,600 s + phase 2 60 s, checker):
  - rho 0.6, 32 cores, jobs 1505 / 1506: `v11_r06` (v1.1 as tagged): root 96.6 s, first design 359 s, 1 h gap 12.0 %, cost 8,662.61, checker OK. `fix_r06` (v1.1 + the three fixes): root 4.2 s, first design 132 s, gap 10.9 %, cost 8,681.89, OK. Reading: root 23x and first design 2.7x faster (Method 6 removal), cost within seed noise (best known 8,650.5, portfolio), gap bound-limited as before.
  - rho 1, 64 cores (`Threads=64`), jobs 1507 `v11_r1` (v1.1, NoRelHeurTime=120), 1508 `fix_r1` (v1.1 + fixes), 1509 `fixmf1_r1` (+ MIPFocus=1): results (1 h, checker OK for all three): `v11_r1` root 93 s, first design 445 s, gap 19.45 %, cost 10,238.13; `fix_r1` root 2.8 s, first design 141 s, gap 15 / 30 / 60 min 0.63 / 0.60 / 0.58 %, cost 8,998.62 (meets the 1 % target); `fixmf1_r1` root 6.2 s, first design 191 s, gap 17.58 %, cost 9,177.41. Winner: v1.1 + fixes with the default parameters; MIPFocus=1 hurts at 10 cells. Single runs (seed 0): the root time is systematic, the gap still needs a second seed (v1.1 seeds ranged 0.59-20 % in 16.21). Reference row at 32 cores: tag `nr` (= v1.1 settings) first design 293 s, gap 1.63 %, 9,091.
  - 65 SWCs at 20 and 33 cells, rho 1, winner code, 64 cores, 1 h (jobs 1510 `fix_c20_r1`, 1511 `fix_c33_r1`), checker OK: 20 cells root 10 s, first design 671 s, gap at 30 min 17.7 %, final cost 9,184.53, bound 7,804, gap 15.0330 %; 33 cells root 70 s, first design 2007 s, gap at 30 min - %, final cost 9,265.00, bound 7,559, gap 18.4113 %. Both below the gate rows of 2026-10-27 with MIPFocus=1 (9,002 / 9,029 in 30 min, 8 threads): at 20 / 33 cells MIPFocus=1 helps (finding a design is the problem), at 10 cells it hurts. Step 0 of the piece plan: 20 / 33 cells rerun with MIPFocus=1 (and NoRelHeurTime=600 at 33 cells).
- Next: (a) the winner becomes the default on c-scale (commit when the user says); (b) 65 SWCs at 20 and 33 cells, rho 1, 1 h; (c) grow 150 -> 300 -> 500 ... until no first design within the hour = start of the piece-by-piece method and of the v3 loader.
- Step 0 results (2026-10-28, 65 SWCs, rho 1, 1 h, 64 cores, winner code; results/speed/fixmf1_*): 20 cells MIPFocus=1: first design 205 s, cost 9,014.12, gap 16.1 % (defaults: 671 s, 9,184.53, 15.0 %); 33 cells MIPFocus=1: first design 911 s, 9,407.76, gap 19.7 % (defaults: 2,007 s, 9,265.00, 18.4 %); 33 cells MIPFocus=1 + NoRelHeurTime=600: first design only at 3,247 s, 9,171.61, 17.8 %. Reading: MIPFocus=1 brings the first design 2-3x sooner at 20 / 33 cells, the 1-h cost is mixed (better at 20, worse at 33); the long no-relaxation phase delays the first design to 54 min, not useful. At 20 / 33 cells no setting comes near 1 %: the bound stays at 7,560-7,800 while the designs cost 9,000-9,400 (the bound problem of 16.21).
- Pieces v1 (pieces.py, optimize(fixed), main.py branch, configs/solver.json pieces; 2026-10-28; user: implement, explain every decision). Groups: chains sharing a component + redundant pairs, ordered tightest budget / fail-operational first, split chain by chain above max_group, unchained components attached to the group of their strongest partner (cascading) else grouped along their messages, small groups merged; every step = the exact model of the sub-architecture (placed + new group) with the placed components locked by bounds, 300 s or 1 % gap; a rejected step unlocks the previous group(s). Results (results/pieces/): lite 50 components / 4 cells, groups of <= 12: 5 steps, 189 s, 8,997.94, checker OK = +0.22 % above the proven optimum 8,978.25 (better than most flat 1-h runs). 65 / 10 cells, rho 1: groups of <= 60 (59 + 6): step 1 did not converge in 300 s (gap 48.7 %, cost 14,675, 6 cells) and was locked; step 2 (the 6 heavy-traffic unchained components: media player, telematics, map loaders) infeasible in 121 s (paths and capacity cuts), the backtrack with everything free found no design in 600 s -> stopped. Groups of <= 20 (18, 20, 18, 9): every step below 1 % gap, 1,017 s in total, 9,173.26, checker OK = +1.9 % vs the flat 8,998.62 (1 h) in 17 min. Groups of <= 12 (12, 12, 7, 10, 12, 12): every step below 1 % gap, 1,298 s, 9,169.80, checker OK (= groups of 20: the group size does not matter at this size, the locking does). Lessons: lock only converged steps (small groups); heavy-traffic components must not come last; the quality price at 65 is about 2 %, the method is for sizes the flat model cannot do.
- Flat vs pieces, like for like (2026-10-28; 65 SWCs, 10 cells, rho 1, all blocks, NoRelHeurTime=120, Threads=64, same code): flat 1 h (fix_r1) cost 8,998.62, bound 8,945, gap 0.58 % (0.63 % already at 15 min, so about 9,001 at the 17 min the pieces build needed); pieces groups of <= 20: 9,173.26 after 1,017 s, no bound (at most 2.5 % above the optimum by the flat bound). Both designs: 3 ECUs, 9 partitions, 43 standbys, partition + site cost 7,410 identical; different cells (flat S30 34 / S01 27 / S04 4 components, pieces S20 41 / S27 19 / S10 5), no component in the same cell, 60 % of the component pairs with the same together / apart decision. The +174.6 is hardware +169 (a feature bought twice: the first groups fixed their cells before the later groups' hardware needs were known), cables and ports +5. Conclusion: at this size pieces is not faster at equal quality (the flat model is better at every time budget); its value is only where the flat model delivers nothing. Improvement rounds (free one cell, re-solve) are the intended fix for the hardware duplication.
- Schema-v3 loader (2026-10-28, user: "fill the gaps with reasonable numbers"): `tools/arch_v3_prepare.py` writes configs/arch_v3/prepared/<name>/ (arch.json in the loader's fields + per-pair flows + meta.assumptions; cfg/ = the run's config files: partitions.json with v3 capacities, sensors.json / actuators.json with the suite's and the generated devices, chains.json with derived budgets, the rest copied). architecture_loader.py and tools/check_solution.py read the device lists (sensors / actuators per driver) and the flows list when present; the v2 files are read as before (regression unchanged). Assumptions, all printed and stored in arch.json: (1) units DMIPS / kB; partition = one R-Car V4H-class block (49,000 DMIPS, 16 GB RAM, 64 GB flash), cell = 4 blocks (configs/arch_v3/prepared/*/cfg/partitions.json; costs unchanged); (2) the 8 traffic_light_node_container nodes (83,000 DMIPS each = the CPU side of GPU inference measured on the A100 host, 86 % of the Autoware CPU) capped at one target core, 12,250 DMIPS; their inference load stays as HW_ACC / 1.79 TOPS; (3) the 159 Autoware nodes without ASIL: fail-operational -> D, driving-chain nodes -> B, else QM; (4) the 38 generated devices get the bus of their function as interface ('direct' -> UART), one 8-byte frame per period (audio streams as sourced) and a position by their place in the car (table in the tool); Autoware drivers mapped to the suite by name (lidar top/left/right/rear = suite front/left/right/rear, clone lidar_top = LIDAR_Top; traffic-light map-based detector per camera; imu_corrector; gnss_poser); (4b) the suite's seven actuators (steering, motor, brakes, HVAC, lights), which no v3 component drove, are driven by the last component of the matching function (EPS, brake-by-wire actuation, traction inverter, climate control, exterior light; user 2026-10-28: 'the car had actuators'), and the chains driving them are in every subset; (5) 390 Autoware topics without rate / size: medians 40 Hz / 737 B; several topics of a pair = one flow (summed volume, largest message); (6) chain network budget = end-to-end bound minus processing times (reproduces the 0.78 / 12.9 / 72.7 ms of 16.21); (7) subsets = the whole Autoware part + random whole non-AD chains (seed) + strongest partners. Prepared: full 5,884 components (14,321 flows, 386 chains, 41 sensors, 12 actuators; demand 218k DMIPS = 4.5 blocks, 45.8 GB RAM, 15.2 TOPS; the Autoware part is 93 % of the CPU), n150_s1 = the 159 Autoware nodes, n300_s1, n500_s1. Smoke (job 1547): pieces on n150 (159 Autoware nodes) at 10 cells, groups of <= 20, 120 s per step: 9 steps, 1,693 s, cost 13,856.54, 5 cells, checker OK (1 warning) - the v3 data goes through the model and the checker. Scale series submitted 2026-10-28 (jobs 1554-1559, after the actuator fix; the first submission was cancelled after a minute): flat (1 h, MIPFocus=1, the setting with the earliest first design at 33 cells) vs pieces (groups of <= 20, 300 s per step) on n150 (167 components: 159 Autoware + the actuator chains; 15 sensors, 8 actuators) / n300 (19 / 9) / n500 (22 / 9) at 33 cells, rho 1, 64 cores (results/scale/). Full car after the fix: 41 sensors, 19 actuators. First attempt (jobs 1554-1559) infeasible at once on every instance: the brake-by-wire chain (network budget 5.4 ms) now ended at the CAN brake actuators, whose single bus crossing (frame + 2 ms hop + 3.5 ms robust platform term = 5.77 ms) exceeds the budget. Fix in the prepare tool (assumption 4c): devices of control loops whose budget cannot pay one bus crossing are wired directly to their unit (SPI: no bus crossing, point-to-point cable), as in the engine / chassis ECUs they come from; the tool now checks every chain's end devices against its budget. Direct-wired in the full car: ACT_Brake_Front (CAN -> SPI, chain budget 5.388 ms); ACT_Brake_Rear (CAN -> SPI, chain budget 5.388 ms). Resubmitted as jobs 1560-1565. Second infeasibility (n300 / n500 flat, found with an IIS on the written LP: one constraint, bus_device_motor_position_sensor): with failures on a bus device of critical software sits on two lines of its type (two lanes), and buses.json offers one FlexRay line; the v2 car had no critical FlexRay device. Fix (assumption 4d): the v3 instances' cfg/buses.json offers CAN 4, LIN 6, FlexRay 2 lines (at least two where a critical device needs two lanes); the model decides how many are used. n300 / n500 resubmitted (jobs 1566-1569): flat again infeasible (IIS: steering_torque_sensor high-ASIL critical takes both FlexRay lines as its two lanes, brake_modulator low-ASIL has none left): the rule is two high lines plus one low line per type where both kinds exist; FlexRay 3 was still infeasible (IIS rows printed: brake_modulator's driver Chassis_Esc_149 is fail-operational at ASIL < 3, so it is a critical device of the low group with two lanes; with the high critical steering sensors four FlexRay lines are needed, and the symmetry rule (sorted devices open lines in order) makes that exact). Final rule in the tool: per bus type and ASIL group two lines where a critical device exists, else one; defaults CAN 4, LIN 6, FlexRay 4; the 300 and 500 models verified feasible with a 20-s write-LP run before submitting jobs 1576-1579. n150 (jobs 1560 / 1561) unaffected and running.
- Scale series, 167 components (Autoware + actuator chains), 33 cells, rho 1, 64 cores (2026-10-28, results/scale/): flat (MIPFocus=1): root 18 s, first design 56 s, phase 1 stopped at the 1-h limit with 15,270.86 and gap 14.6 % (bound 13,040); the design failed the partition packing (14 ASIL-2 components at S30 in 3 partitions), the packing cut restarted phase 1 with the full time limit and the job hit its 1 h 40 min wall limit: no checked design. Pieces (10 groups of <= 20, 300 s per step, every step < 1 % gap, two steps with partition re-solves): 15,504.93 in 77 min, checker OK (1 warning: cable phase at its time limit), 5 ECUs (S31 70, S29 30, S01 26, S03 23, S33 18 components), 18 partitions, 73 standbys, cable 70.2 m. Reading: at 167 the flat model still finds the cheaper phase-1 design within the hour but cannot deliver a checked one in 1 h 40 min; pieces delivers a checked design in 77 min at about +1.5 %. The flat model's packing-cut loop (a full re-solve per cut) is a weakness at this size; pieces packs per step.
- Scale series, 500 components, 33 cells: flat (MIPFocus=1, 1 h): root LP 39 min, no design within the hour ("time limit without any feasible solution"). 300 components: flat root LP 35 min, first design at 37 min, phase 1 ended at the hour with 48,766 (gap 68 %), the packing re-solve hit the wall limit: no checked design. Pieces without the convergence rule: locked step 12 at 82 % gap, stopped at step 15 after 107 min with no design. Pieces with the rule at lock_gap 0.05: step 2 stayed at 9 % after 300 + 600 s (the Autoware sub-model's bound is weak there, the same 9 % the old run had and built on fine), so 0.05 would spend 20 min and split a step that is in fact fine; lock_gap set to 0.10 (the failure case was 82 %) and the pieces2 runs restarted (jobs 1590 / 1591).
- Pieces, convergence rule (2026-10-28): the 300-component pieces run locked step 12 (20 new components) at the 300-s limit with an 82 % gap and 10 cells (cost 9,629 -> 55,565); step 13 inherited it (23 % gap, 14 cells), step 14 found no design and backtracked. Rule added to pieces.py (configs/solver.json pieces.lock_gap 0.05, piece_time_max_s 1200): a step is locked only below lock_gap; above it the step is re-solved with doubled time from its own design up to piece_time_max_s, then its group is split in two halves (the first half solved again). Smoke on the lite instance (20-s steps, lock_gap 0.02, max 60 s, results/pieces/smoke_lock): step 2 at 12.9 % gap was re-solved with 40 s to 0.29 %, no split needed, 9,002.04 in 117 s, checker OK; the 300 / 500 pieces runs restarted with the rule as pieces2_* (the old 300 run continues to its end for comparison).
- Pieces at 300 / 500 with the 10 % lock rule (jobs 1590 / 1591, 2026-10-28): 300 stalled at step 16 (the lidar / localisation block: 19 fail-operational ASIL-D nodes), 500 at step 22 (perception / planning block, 11 fail-operational): every candidate rejected by the lazy cable-capacity rules ('paths and capacity' nominal, 'capacity after failure' = link capacity after a cell failure; the heavy lidar / camera streams must fit the 1-Gbit links from their drivers), 111-244 candidates per solve all rejected, no design in 300 / 600 / 900 s with 1-2 groups unlocked. Cause: hard blocks arrive when the cells are full, and the model does not see cable capacity up front. Changes (user: 'do 1, 2 then start the test'): (1) groups ordered hard first (fail-operational count, message traffic; pieces.py hardness), so the lidar block is now step 1 of 16 on n300; (2) every step runs with the valid static cable-capacity rules upfront_paths + cell_cut_capacity on (pieces.static_capacity; the flat model keeps them off); (3) per-step dump of the partial design (pieces.dump_dir) to reproduce a failing step alone. Smoke on the lite instance (results/pieces/smoke_hard): 5 steps, 110 s, 9,150.71, checker OK (the old order gave 8,998-9,002 there: hard-first costs about 1.7 % on the small car, where the order of the easy groups mattered little before). 300 test started as job 1608 (results/scale/pieces3_n300_s1_c33, dump on).
- Results of the 10 %-lock runs without the later changes (pieces2, 2026-10-28): 300 components stopped at step 17 after 206 min (steps 16 / 17 found no design with up to 3 groups unlocked), 500 stopped at step 26; no design from either. The finalised method (pieces3: hard groups first, static cable-capacity rules per step, 10 % lock) on 300: step 1 converged (2,559, 2 cells); step 2 stayed at 17-20 % gap through 300 / 600 / 1,200 s and was split (10 + 10, first half locked at 9.2 %); step 3 converged; step 4 again above 24 % after 600 s (re-solving with 1,200 s at 78 min). Observation: the sub-model gap of the Autoware blocks is a weak bound, not a bad design, so the re-solve rule costs 20-35 min per hard step; The run stopped at step 9 after 151 min: steps 1-8 converged (150 components placed, 12,111, 4 cells; 5 re-solves, 1 split), step 9 (14 components) found no design with 1, 2 and 3 groups unlocked (300 / 600 / 900 s). Per-step dumps in results/scale/pieces3_n300_s1_c33/pieces_step_*.json for a standalone reproduction of step 9. Step 9 = 19 Autoware platform / API nodes (autoware_state, mrm handlers, fail_safe, diagnostics; 6 fail-operational ASIL D, 8k DMIPS, 0.55 GB, one UART device, no chain); the partial design after step 8 uses 4 cells at 12-21k DMIPS and 1.7-6.8 GB each (far below the caps) with 91 locked standbys. Rejections of the step-9 candidates: failure survival 214 of 219, capacity after failure 161, paths and capacity 96 (thousands of cuts, no accepted candidate in 300 / 600 / 900 s). Hypothesis: locking the standby cells of the placed components (91 of them) leaves the failure-survival rule no room for the new fail-operational nodes; Lab result (tools/pieces_step_lab.py, jobs 1634 / 1635, 32 threads, 600 s): step 9 standalone found a design with the standbys locked (12,095, gap 0.99 %, after 330 s) and with them free (12,092 after 630 s). Hypothesis refuted: the standbys are not the blocker; the step simply needs more than 300 s, and the fallback made it worse by unlocking groups (more free variables, more lazy rejections: 36 / 56 free found nothing in 600 / 900 s). Fallback changed in pieces.py: a step without a design first gets doubled time up to piece_time_max_s (1,200 s), only then are previous groups unlocked, each unlock with the full 1,200 s.
- Fast build + polish + build report (2026-10-29, user's proposal, rated 8/10): (a) fast build = pieces.lock_gap 1.0: every group's design is accepted at piece_time_s (the time fallback stays for groups without a design); (b) polish rounds (pieces.polish_rounds, polish_time_s, polish_partners): after the build the whole car is in the model, everything fixed except one group (weakest build gap first) plus its strongest communication partners, re-solved from the current design for polish_time_s, kept if cheaper (like the GNN method's 'free the least confident', with the group gap as the signal; also to try: most expensive groups, whole cells); (c) build report per solve (group's fail-operational count, traffic, devices, chains, CPU, RAM; time, gap, cost, cells, result) printed and written to dump_dir/pieces_report.txt. Overnight jobs 2026-10-28/29: flat 300 and 500 at 33 cells with 8 h search (1638 / 1639), pieces 500 (1640, queued), pieces 300 with the new fallback (1637); smoke of report + polish on the lite instance (1643), then 300 fast build + 6 polish rounds of 600 s (pieces5, auto-submitted when the smoke passes).
- 300 with the new fallback (pieces4, job 1637, 2026-10-29 night): stopped at step 8 after 181 min. Steps 1-7 built 120 components (11,241, 4 cells; step 2 split, steps 2 / 3 re-solved, step 6 needed 600 s). Step 8 = 17 components (5 EPB, 8 central-gateway, powertrain coordinator, 3 Autoware point-cloud filters; 16 fail-operational, ASIL C / D, 3.4k DMIPS, 2.2 GB, no devices): no design in 300 / 600 / 1,200 s and with 1 / 2 groups unlocked at 1,200 s each; every candidate rejected by 'paths and capacity' (100 %), 'failure survival' (~80 %), 'bus length' (~70 %), 'capacity after failure', 'degraded latency' - although the static cable rules were on. Lab on the step-7 dump (jobs 1645-1647, 900 s, 32 threads): control no design (107 candidates, all rejected), standbys free no design (8 candidates), static cable rules OFF: design 15,410 at 6 % gap after 922 s (570 candidates, 1 accepted). Conclusion: the static cable rules (upfront_paths + cell_cut_capacity) make the step model so much slower that the lazy loop sees 5x fewer candidates; they are off by default again (pieces.static_capacity false). The 300 fast-build + polish run and the 500 run were restarted with the rules off (pieces6_n300 / pieces6_n500: fast build, 6 polish rounds of 600 s, dump + report).
- Fast build + polish without the static cable rules (pieces6, jobs 1648 / 1649, 2026-10-29): 300 components: complete design 19,836.30 after 113 min build (16 groups, every step accepted at ~300 s, one step 1,836 s for packing re-solves), 6 polish rounds (68 min) no gain, checker OK; 6 cells. 500 components: complete design 20,880.84 after 140 min (27 groups), polish rounds 1-3 improved to 20,862.83 (-0.09 %), checker 1 ERROR: FLEXRAY2 cable 2.422 m shorter than the Steiner tree of its members 2.584 m (investigated 2026-10-29: not a missing cut but a checker bug. Members S15, S30, brake_modulator; the optimiser's tree 2.4219 runs S30 -> ACT_Steering -> brake_modulator -> S15 through the steering actuator as a Steiner point; the checker's Dreyfus-Wagner found 2.584 because the cable lengths are not a metric when channel lengths (tabled points) and Manhattan lengths (the 38 generated devices, not attached to the channel graph) mix: S30-brake_modulator direct 1.482 m but 1.32 m via ACT_Steering. Dreyfus-Wagner is exact only on a metric; the optimiser's DP happened to find the shorter tree through its final relaxation, the checker's terminal-rooted DP did not. Fix: shortest-path closure (Floyd-Warshall) of the distances before every Steiner computation, in optimizer_utils.steiner_tree and in the checker (_metric, also for its flow model and pairs); the up-front bus-length bounds of the model (pair and Euler-tour bounds) use the closure too. The 65-SWC car is unchanged (0 pairs shortened: its table is a metric, all its devices are tabled), so the regression is unaffected; on the v3 instances 536 of 2,016 pairs get shorter (up to 2.46 m). Re-check with the fixed checker: 500 design OK, 300 / 167 / 65 designs OK. Proper data fix still open: attach the generated devices to the channel graph (tools/vehicle_layout/generate_layout.py) so all lengths come from the harness graph.) Cost driver in both: the group with the 8 traffic-light containers (108k DMIPS, 26 GB) arrives at step 10 / 13 and adds ~5,200-7,200 (2-3 new cells); the hard-first order ranks by fail-operational count and traffic, not by resource size. Polish order by build gap is uninformative (all ~1 %). Flat overnight (job 1638, 8 h limit): 300 components at 5.5 h: 18,998 at 18.3 % gap (bound 15,523), i.e. 4 % cheaper than the pieces design after 3 h; 500 components (job 1639): no design after 6.8 h.
- Endpoints of the v3 car (2026-10-29, user: merge duplicates, drop uncommon devices, only real sensors / actuators, place them sensibly in the car, through the scripts): the 38 generated devices became 16 endpoints in tools/arch_v3_prepare.py (ENDPOINT_OF, ENDPOINTS). Merged into the suite: inverter gate drivers -> ACT_Motor_Front, ESC brake modulator -> ACT_Brake_Front, the three EPS sensors -> ACT_Steering, defrost switch + cabin temperature -> ACT_HVAC_Cabin. Dropped: pedestrian contact sensors, active-hood lifters, AWD coupling, ride-height sensors, accelerometers, chassis sensor pool, parking buzzer. Kept (parts of one unit merged): sensors accelerator pedal, gear selector (+ drive mode), start button (+ key status), keyless receiver (key remote + RF), door lock switches, window switches (+ pinch), seat controls (buttons + position), TPMS receiver, parking sensors, microphones, charge port (+ pilot); actuators horn, mirror motors left / right, on-board charger (+ contactors), amplifier (+ speakers). Positions from the blocks of vehicle.json, left-hand drive (driver at x < 0): pedal in the driver footwell, selector and start button right of the steering column (IONIQ 5 column selector), door / window switches in the driver door, seat controls at the driver seat base, mirrors at the front upper door corners, keyless receiver in the rear headliner, TPMS receiver under the rear seat, microphones in the overhead console, charge port in the rear right quarter panel, horn behind the front bumper, parking sensors in the rear bumper, on-board charger in the ICCU block, amplifier under the rear floor covering (IONIQ 5 N Bose amplifier, BRS installation manual). A merged endpoint carries the sum of its members' frames and is driven by all of their drivers. The instance script now runs tools/vehicle_layout/generate_layout.py on every instance (voxel routing space): all endpoints attached (drops 2-30 cm), the 33 cells and every existing distance unchanged (max change 0.0), and it stops if an endpoint has no cable distance. Full car: 26 sensors + 12 actuators = 38 endpoints (was 60). Renders (headless Blender, tools/vehicle_viz/build_vehicle.py, labels by name): results/vehicle_layout/endpoints_v3/{iso,top,side}.png, checked visually. Results of runs before this change (pieces6, night_flat) used the old devices.
- Reproducibility incident (2026-10-29, user asked whether the group-by-group designs are really valid): a fresh check reported 17 / 19 errors for the 300 / 500 designs that had passed in the morning. Cause: those runs referenced configs/arch_v3/prepared/n300_s1 and n500_s1/arch.json, which were regenerated at 08:46 for the new endpoints, after the runs; the checker compared yesterday's designs with today's device lists (steering sensors merged into ACT_Steering, etc.). Each run's original instance was reconstructed (device lists before the endpoint change; devices then match the run's config exactly: 28 / 31 / 23) and stored as architecture_used.json in the run directory; against it 167 / 300 / 500 are OK (2,972 / 5,516 / 7,217 checks), as are the 65-component designs. Fix: main.py copies the architecture file into every run directory (architecture_used.json, run_info architecture_copy) and the checker prefers that copy over the path the run read from. Reporting rule (user): only checker-passed designs count as results; flat-model incumbents before packing / checking are reported as 'no valid design after T'.
- Target and step time (2026-10-29): target scale 1,000-1,500 components (BMW Neue Klasse press release T0448372EN: 'well over 1,000 software modules', four high-performance computers plus zonal controllers, four wiring zones). Time per group, measured on the 300 build (pieces6, 16 steps of ~300 s): phase 1 (placement) 130-300 s, phase 2 (cable length of the intermediate design) 5-153 s = ~40 % of the build, because phase 2 got the rest of piece_time_s, not piece_phase2_s; the no-relaxation heuristic ran its full 120 s in 6 of 16 steps; the step model grows 35k -> 153k variables (locked components stay variables). Changes: optimize(phase2_cap) and pieces caps phase 2 of intermediate steps and polish rounds at piece_phase2_s; pieces.step_gurobi_params (default NoRelHeurTime=20) appended to the run's parameters for steps and polish; resource-heavy groups first (> 10 % of the car's CPU or RAM; the traffic-light group moves from step 10 to step 3 on n300); cell polish (pieces.polish_mode 'cell'): each round frees one whole cell (every component with its primary or standby there), fewest hosted components first, so a cell can be closed. Test on n300 with the 40 endpoints (pieces7, auto-submitted after a lite smoke).
- Flat MILP 300 components, 33 cells, rho 1 (job 1638, 2026-10-28/29, MIPFocus=1, 8-h search limit per solve, 22-h job limit, old devices): **no valid design after 14 h**; stopped by the user's decision. Course: root relaxation 35 min, first incumbent at 29 min of the search (51,406), phase 1 ended at 8 h with an incumbent of 18,998 (gap 18.3 %) that failed the partition packing; the packing cut started a second solve, whose incumbent was 18,705 (gap 17.3 %) at 5.8 h when stopped. Group by group on the same instance: 19,836, valid, 3 h (pieces6).
- Group by group with capped cable stage, heavy-first order and cell polish on n300 with the 40 endpoints (pieces7, job 1671): build 49 min (old settings 113 min on the old instance), build cost 20,628.85 before polish (not comparable with 19,836: different instance). Easy groups 90-140 s instead of ~300 s; the traffic-light group (step 3) needed 4 solves (3 packing cuts) and opened 6 cells at once (at rho 1 each container grows x2.5 to ~30k DMIPS, one per partition). Comparison run with the old order on the same instance (pieces8, heavy_first false, otherwise identical; started in parallel on the user's request). Result pieces7: **19,787.68, valid** (checker OK, 5,631 checks), 102 min in total = build 49 min (20,628.85) + 6 cell-polish rounds 53 min (S26 closed -49, S29 no gain, S24 closed -660, S25 -92, S03 -3, S19 -38; polish -4.1 %). Each round but one used its full 600 s.
- Noted for later (user 2026-10-29, not implemented): certificate for the group-by-group designs. Gap of a design = (its checked cost - a lower bound of the whole problem) / its cost. The flat model's bound is valid but loose because the lazy rules (paths / link capacity, chain timing with queueing, failure survival) are not in its relaxation, so the certificate is pessimistic (old 300 instance: bound 15,522, barely moving in 8 h; vs 19,836 = at most 22 %). Idea: a separate bound model with the rules written up front, used only for its relaxation (LP / root with cuts), never for designs; existing pieces: buses.json upfront_paths and cell_cut_capacity (on the lite instance both on proved the rho 1 failure case within 1 h, i.e. a much tighter bound), the chain bound, Benders feasibility / failure cuts (benders.py). Hard parts: failure survival per cell-failure scenario and queueing delay only as valid linear relaxations, model size (scenarios x flows x paths). Decide after the 300 / 500 results.
- Order comparison on n300 with the 40-endpoint devices (2026-10-29, identical settings except the group order; both checker OK): heavy-first (pieces7) 19,787.68, 6 ECUs, build 49 min + polish 53 min = 102 min; old order (pieces8) 20,126.31, build 39 min + polish 22 min = 62 min (polish -86, one cell closed). Heavy-first is 1.7 % cheaper and 40 min slower (its polish closed two cells, -841). Second instance-copy hole: main.py wrote architecture_used.json at the end of a run, so pieces8 (ended 11:54) copied the n300 instance regenerated at 11:46 (all endpoints in every subset) and its checker / diagram crashed; restored from pieces7 (same instance) -> OK, 6,313 checks. main.py now copies the instance at the start of the run. Switch in every ECU: the cost model prices an Ethernet port at 17 USD and a switch (SJA1110, 16 ports) at 23 USD, so from two Ethernet connections a switch is cheaper; every ECU of the design has 3-4 backbone links plus up to 5 Ethernet sensors. Read as an Ethernet switch chip inside the ECU (as in real zone controllers); port price (low confidence) and switch size go into the sensitivity study. Subsets: every endpoint's driving components and their chains are now in every subset (187 components fixed); n300 / n500 / n1000 / n1500 rebuilt with 28 sensors + 12 actuators each.
- Decision (user 2026-10-29): stay with the settings of the 300 run pieces7 (now the defaults of configs/solver.json pieces, enabled stays false). Noted for later, if time permits: the order comparison is a single run each (heavy-first better only after polish: build 20,629 vs 20,213, final 19,788 vs 20,126); repeat with several seeds per order or at 500 before claiming an effect.
- Ethernet port cost (user decision 2026-10-29): configs/buses.json ETH port_cost 17.0 -> 4.25 USD (a quarter), so that a switch (22.87 USD) pays off only from about 6 Ethernet ports on one ECU instead of from 2. Reason: in the 300 design every ECU had a switch at 3-9 Ethernet ports (average 5.5): the switch replaced up to 16 port costs. Research (no agents): gigabit PHY 2.16 (NXP TJA1120 list) / 9.02 at 1k / 11.71 at 250 (TI DP83TG720); SJA1110 22.87 USD (DigiKey), up to 6 integrated 100BASE-T1 PHYs, gigabit ports need external PHYs; no reliable connector price found; processors have 2 (Jetson AGX Orin) to 4 (S32G2) Ethernet interfaces. All v3 instances rebuilt with the new cost. The 65-component regression expectations change with it (configs/buses.json) and must be re-measured before the next regression run.
- Parallel cell polish (2026-10-29, user): pieces._polish_cells_parallel (pieces.polish_parallel, default 3): each batch frees that many cells of the current design, each in its own spawned process with its own Gurobi session and an equal share of the threads (separate processes because the lazy rule checks run in Python), for polish_time_s; the cheapest improvement is kept, a cell that improved but was not taken stays a candidate; polish_rounds cells in total. tools/pieces_polish.py polishes a finished build again: it rebuilds the design from the run's last dump (all primary and standby cells fixed; partitions, hardware, cables re-decided) with the run's own instance, config and Gurobi parameters, then polishes and checks. A/B on the same 300 design (old instance, old port price): sequential polish 53 min, 20,628.85 -> 19,787.68; parallel polish 21 min (+79 s rebuild), rebuilt start 20,756.67 (the rebuild re-decides cables / hardware without the build's variable values: +128) -> 19,831.67, valid (6,499 checks). Same cells closed (S26, S24); 2.5x faster, final cost +0.2 %. In both batches S03 improved but was not taken (-92 / -169): a third batch would likely gain more; candidate rule: batches until a batch brings no improvement, instead of a fixed number of cells.
- Open (user 2026-10-29, to solve later): the parallel polish keeps only the cheapest of a batch's copies; improvements found in the other cells of the same batch are thrown away (300: S24 -130 and S03 -93 in batch 1, S03 -169 in batch 2, ~390 USD; every batch measured so far had >= 2 improving cells: 2 of 2 at 300, 4 of 4 on the lite instance). Candidate fix: after each batch one extra solve that frees all improving cells together, starting from their results.
- Parallel build, first look at 500 (job 1716, cancelled after 12 min for the redesign below): batch 1 = steps 1-2 (2 groups, 327 s, combined check fit, 12 s), batch 2 = steps 3-4 (2 groups, 77 s, fit, 33 s); the lite smoke (4 cells) had a clash. Batches are formed from groups that exchange no message and share no chain; the first (heavy, fail-operational) groups are mostly linked, so only 2 per batch there.
- Parallel build with own cells (user 2026-10-29: parallel groups must not touch each other): optimize(fixed['allow']) restricts a component's placement and standby to given cells; pieces._own_cells splits the cells per batch: a cell is cheap for a group when it is close (cable route) to the devices the group drives and to the cells of its already placed message partners (weighted 1 + message volume); cells in use are shared out first, each group gets a fair share (ceil(cells / groups), at least 2), groups without any pull get the least loaded remaining cells; the log prints every group's cells. Lite smoke (4 cells, 2 groups): batch 2 split S09/S25 vs S01/S17 and fit (17 s); batch 1: step 4 found no design in its 2 cells and also none sequentially with steps 1-3 locked (proven infeasible), it needed one previous group unlocked. Fix: optimize sets last_status; a step proven infeasible skips the longer-time retries (pointless) and unlocks at once (the smoke had spent 7 retries of 30-1,200 s limits that each ended immediately).
- Parallel build at 500 with own cells (job 1779, 4 groups max, 128 CPUs; stopped by the user's decision after 74 min): every batch had only 2 independent groups (steps 1-2, 3-4, 5-6, 9-10, 12-13; steps 7 and 8 alone); the combined checks (12-47 s each) all passed and caught nothing; batch 5: group 12 found no design in its own 16 cells and then none with all cells in 300 / 600 s (a hard group on this placement path, not caused by the split). First 11 groups: 32 min of steps (+ ~5 min lost in batch 5) vs 24 min one group at a time. Conclusion: at 500 the groups are too linked (only pairs independent), the check overhead and the different placement path eat the gain; the parallel build stays implemented but off (pieces.build_parallel 1). Decision (user): build one group at a time + parallel polish for 1,000 / 1,500.
- Runs started 2026-10-29 evening: 1,000 (job 1914) and 1,500 (job 1915), 33 cells, rho 1, 40 endpoints, ETH port 4.25, one group at a time, heavy-first, parallel polish (3 x 2 batches), 64 CPUs each, 24-h limit.
- LLM-in-the-loop, noted for a later discussion (2026-10-28, user: "note it, we talk later"). Question: people design complex PCBs / CAD with GPT / Claude under many constraints, why not us? Research (10 sources, no agents): (1) the LLM never solves the constraints; it writes code for a deterministic tool (KiCad via its Python / IPC API, CadQuery for CAD) and the tool's checker is the judge in a propose -> DRC / ERC -> keep-only-if-not-worse loop (CopperLoop github.com/PaoloJN/copperloop; Agnet_kicad; Claude-Code RF workflow arXiv 2608.31006; CAD-Coder NeurIPS 2025, CadQuery code with a geometric reward); (2) the hard combinatorial part stays a solver or RL: Quilter (RL on millions of synthetic boards against physics checks), DeepPCB (RL), PCBWorld arXiv 2607.05915 (RL on synthetic boards approaches rule-based routers; LLM agents only beat open-loop LLM baselines); Text2CAD-Bench arXiv 2605.18430: text-to-CAD "largely unsolved beyond basic geometry", assembly constraints future work; chip-design agents (ChatEDA arXiv 2308.10204, ORFS-agent arXiv 2506.08332) orchestrate OpenROAD / tune parameters; (3) nobody claims optimality: "passes every check" is the goal. Structural difference: their checks are local (clearance, width, per-net length / impedance) and take milliseconds; ours are global (chain timing with queueing on shared buses, failure scenarios) and take minutes, so propose / repair rounds need the cheap placement check (capacity, chains apart / together, failure balance, static timing) as the counterexample source. Assessment: an LLM could replace only the start heuristic (first design / grouping) in the counterexample loop of 16.24 (LLM proposes, cheap check rejects with reasons, Gurobi repairs through optimize(fixed)); confidence: ~60 % that it yields a valid design on the 65-SWC car after a few rounds, ~25 % that it beats the rule-based pieces (within ~2 % of flat, 17 min) in quality or time at 65-500, at 5,884 only per group (= pieces with an LLM choosing each group's cell), ~30 % gain over the fixed rule; cost 1-2 days plus API calls; it never contributes to the certified gap. If the certified gap is not required, the pieces + rounds pipeline with the checker as judge is already the PCB-style tool; what the PCB tools add is staging with cheap proxies, a proposal policy trained on synthetic instances, and engineering years. To be discussed after the scale series.
- Note on the no-relaxation heuristic: NoRelHeurTime=120 is the v1.1 default and was on in every run of the series and in every pieces step, except the two MIPFocus=1 runs at 20 / 33 cells of step 0 (parameter string replaces the default; same as the gate rows of 2026-10-27); the third step-0 run had NoRelHeurTime=600.
- Drawing fixes (user 2026-10-29: cables too thick and on top of each other, no cable to the GPS, none into the doors): (1) tools/vehicle_layout/solution_paths.py did not draw directly wired devices (SPI / UART: GPS, IMU, front and rear brake) because it looked their software up by an old naming rule; it now takes them from the checker's reading of the run's architecture (one cable per reading component, plus one for the standby of a critical one, as costed): 10 more cables. (2) Every cable now includes the straight drop between the unit / device and its nearest channel point (part of distances.json, so of the cost); the door switches sit 12 / 30 cm from the nearest channel (the routing space has no channel inside a door), so their cables used to stop at the sill. The routed total now equals the costed cable length (500 design: 79 cables, 144.88 m drawn vs 144.90 m costed, rounding). (3) tools/vehicle_viz/build_vehicle.py: cables 1 cm thick (was 2.8 cm); cables sharing a piece of channel lie side by side in a flat band (1.4 cm apart, fixed order per piece, tilted so the band is open in the top, side and 3/4 views); picture only, the optimiser routes each cable on its own shortest path.
- Switches in the 500 design (user 2026-10-29: "10 switches in the car, is this normal?"): 8 switches: 4 inside ECUs (S08 with 13 Ethernet ports, S15 7, S23 7, S27 5) and 4 stand-alone boxes without software (S06, S07, S11, S28) that only relay one or two connections each (S02-S08, S02-S15, S02-S27; S19-S23 and S19-S27). Hand-made what-if with the same software placement: the boxes replaced by direct cables and S27 without a switch (5 ports at 4.25 are cheaper than a switch): checker OK (8,874 checks), cost 20,701.30 instead of 20,784.19 (-82.89), 3 switches (S08, S15, S23), backbone 33.6 m instead of 29.2 m. Causes: (a) a stand-alone switch pays only the chip (23 USD); the unit cost (site fixed_cost 70: housing, mounting, power) is charged only to cells with software; (b) the whole network is about 1 % of the cost and every solve stops at a 1 % gap (about 208 USD), so leftovers of 83 USD are never removed, neither by the build nor by the polish. Industry: zonal cars are projected with 4-6 zones, each with an aggregation switch, plus central switches (Marvell); the switches sit inside zone controllers and central computers. Proposed fix, awaiting the user's decision: (1) a stand-alone switch pays the unit cost like an ECU (cost-based, no rule); (2) a final network clean-up: one solve with every placement fixed and only the network free, to 0 % gap. Not to be switched on while jobs 1914 / 1915 run (their polish workers load the current optimizer.py).
- Reporting slip found on the way (not fixed yet): the optimiser's failure report and its repair heuristic name a cable scenario by the end cells of a route; for a relayed route (S02-S06-S08) the report lists "cable S02-S08" with no loss and leaves out the physical cables S02-S06 and S06-S08. Validity is not affected: the lazy check and the checker test the physical cables.
- Installation-space drawing fix (user 2026-10-30: "the cells of my ECUs are not green, and there are small green cubes in odd places"): the data were right, the drawing was not. All 33 candidate cells (the 10 used ones included) are green voxels (free radius >= 0.10 m), but above every used cell lie 3-7 grey voxels, and where grey met green both shells drew a face in the same place (535 faces); the renderer shows one of two coincident faces, mostly the grey, so the green under the used cells disappeared and green showed mainly where nothing lay on top of it. tools/vehicle_viz/build_vehicle.py: the grey shell now leaves out the faces it shares with the green one (voxel_surface, solid mask); layout.blend of the 500 design rebuilt (the PNGs are unchanged: they do not show the space). The small green spots away from the ECUs are real: 16 of the 25 green pockets (27 of 219 voxels, 1-5 voxels each, along the cabin sides and in the upper trunk) hold no candidate cell, since the cells are taken on a 12 cm lattice (every second voxel). Note: green tests a free ball of radius 0.10 m (half the middle side of the 0.30 x 0.20 x 0.06 m box), so a drawn box can reach beyond the green; the boxes are not yet checked against the car body.

- Candidate cells redone as real mounting places (user 2026-10-30 night, after the installation-space pictures: the scanned lattice cells sat in the front and rear bumpers, behind the rear headrests (the shelf cells S10-S24), asymmetric left / right, as cubes instead of unit-shaped slots, with green 'unit fits' pockets under the B-pillar that were no cells; 'look on the internet where ECUs really sit, B- and C-pillar slots upright'). Web evidence (secondary sources, no OEM drawing of the IONIQ 5 found; service manuals, parts guides, owner forums, teardown summaries): Tesla Model 3 front body controller under the cowl screen between the strut towers (Tesla service manual), left / right body controllers behind the lower dash of the footwells (parts guides), Autopilot computer behind the glovebox; body control modules at the driver-side kick panel / lower dash; Bosch EDC engine ECUs under the windscreen scuttle, Hyundai Accent ECU under the dash at the A-pillar (actronics); IONIQ 5 IBU in the instrument panel (parts listing) and ICCU under the rear seat (owner forum); Audi A8 zFAS in the luggage compartment, Audi gateway J533 under the rear bench or behind the glovebox, telematics units under the front seats (Audi Q5 / Q7; parts guides); zonal controllers at the corners of the body (onsemi, Promwad). Not supported by any source: units in the bumpers (radars and sensors sit there) and on the parcel shelf. Implemented: configs/vehicle.json ecu_zones rewritten (enabled = true, 30 zones, each one slot box of installation_space.ecu_size 300 x 200 x 60 mm with an orientation: flat, flat_y, wall_x = upright on a side wall, wall_y = on the bulkhead; every slot checked against fixed_blocks by hand, symmetric pairs): BAY_L / BAY_R (strut-tower brackets), COWL_L / COWL_R (scuttle bulkhead), BAY_C (above the front drive unit), DASH_L (instrument panel), DASH_R (glovebox), KICK_L / KICK_R (footwell kick panels, upright), TUNNEL (under the console), SEAT_F_L / SEAT_F_R (under the front seats), SEAT_R_L / SEAT_R_R (under the rear seat), BPIL_L / BPIL_R and CPIL_L / CPIL_R (pillars, upright), TRUNK_L / TRUNK_R (trunk side walls above the wheel arches, upright), UFLOOR_L / UFLOOR_R (below the trunk floor beside the charger), ROOF_F / ROOF_R (headliner behind the mirror / at the rear), DOOR_F_L/R and DOOR_R_L/R (inside the doors, upright); plus APIL_L / APIL_R (A-pillar base above the kick panels, upright; user: 30 cells) = 30 cells; thermal classes engine_bay 5, cabin 17, trunk 4, underbody 2, roof 2. tools/vehicle_layout/generate_layout.py: with ecu_zones.enabled the sites come from these zones (id, name, orient, bbox kept in sites.json) instead of the scan; regenerated configs/sites.json (30), channels.json, distances.json against the unchanged cable space (drops 0.03-0.23 m, the front doors 0.23 m and BAY_C 0.21 m the longest; site-site cable length 0.19-4.29 m, no unreachable pair); the old 33-cell sites.json kept in the scratchpad only. tools/vehicle_viz/build_vehicle.py: cells drawn as their slot boxes (space view and the ECU boxes of a result), the scanned 'unit fits' region not drawn when the cells are curated; legend table shows the cell names. Rendered: results/vehicle_layout/space2/{iso,top,side,rear,front}.png, layout.blend, legend. Loader check: load_architecture_from_json with --num_locs 33 now gives all 30 cells. Consequence: every number so far (flat vs pieces table, the 500 polish ablation, 1,000 / 1,500) is on the old 33 scanned cells; the paper's runs must be redone on the 30 cells. Note on config sets: configs/ carries the v2 endpoint set (15 sensors, 7 actuators, 10 chains of v2 components); the v3 scale runs use per-run config copies (prepared by tools/arch_v3_prepare.py: v3 sensors / actuators / chains / partitions, then generate_layout.py on the copy; later copies inherit the switch fix and the 4.25 port cost), so a new run config = the last working copy (netfix_n500_s1_c33_cfg) + the new vehicle.json + generate_layout.py on it (30 cells, 70 entities, 75 chains, no unreachable pair). First attempt (job 2970) was built from configs/ by mistake and crashed at step 1: the 10 v2 chains have no component in the v3 instance, pieces.sub_architecture kept them (all() over an empty filter) and optimizer.optimize indexed an empty chain; both now guarded (a chain without any component of the instance is left out). Flow fixed for the paper runs (user 2026-10-30 night): build (groups <= 20, heavy first, 300 s per step, every step accepted, 5-s cable stage) -> cell polish (fewest first, 6 x 600 s, cells above 200 free skipped) -> level polish (fewest first, one round per level, 600 s) -> the pair again if the pass improved, at most 2 passes (pass 3 gained 13 of 1,616 in stages_cl) -> network clean-up 1,800 s to 0 % -> checker. Started: final30_n500_s1_c30 (job 2971, 300 GB, 12 h; results/scale/final30_n500_s1_c30, config copy _cfg; main.py --num_locs 30, checker appended to the job). Expected about 6.5 h.
- Why the switch fix needs the clean-up solve (2) and not only the box cost (1): build and polish re-decide the network in every solve, but each solve ends at 1 % of the total (about 208 USD) or on its time limit; the last solves of the 500 run ended on the time limit (final status TIME_LIMIT, 7.8 % from the bound). With (1) alone one or two boxes (93-186 USD) still fit inside the 1 % margin, and a time-limited solve keeps what it starts from; (2) solves only the network of the final placement, to 0 %, so it must find at least the -82.89 of the hand-made what-if.
- Switch fix implemented (user 2026-10-30), both parts behind JSON keys, so the running 1,500 job (own config copy, keys absent) is unchanged: (1) configs/partitions.json site.switch_node_cost (70): a cell with a switch and no software pays the unit cost like an ECU; optimizer.py adds node_j = SWITCH_j and not y_j (three linear constraints, exact for binaries) to the site cost, optimizer_utils.py reports it in site_cost, tools/check_solution.py recomputes it (absent = 0 = the model before). (2) configs/solver.json pieces.final_network_s (1800) / final_network_gap (0): pieces._final_network after the polish: one solve with every primary and standby cell fixed and everything else free, from the current design, kept if cheaper (or as cheap with shorter cables); also run by tools/pieces_polish.py after its polish. The sequential polish now leaves the kept design's values in opt.last_values (the clean-up's start), as the parallel one did. Smoke (lite, 50 components, 4 cells, results/pieces/smoke_netfix): build 9,150.48, polish 9,146.68, final network 9,138.45 (OPTIMAL in 9 s), checker OK. Test on the 500 design: results/scale/netfix_n500_s1_c33 (the final placements of pieces10_n500 as pieces_step_28.json in netfix_n500_s1_c33_src, switch_node_cost 70, polish off, rebuild then clean-up of 1800 s), job 2877.
- 1,000 components (job 1914): checker OK (27,207 checks), 21,809.68, 7 ECUs, 2 switches (S23, S27, both inside ECUs, no switch boxes), 9 h 38 min: build 8.6 h (56 groups, 16 failed tries = 2.2 h; the last group, 2 components, opened an 8th cell, +1,090), polish 2 batches, 59 min (S04 closed, -914; batch 2 found three savings, S22 -72, S23 -77, S32 -92, and kept only S32). 500 (job 1697): 3 h 11 min: build 2.2 h (27 groups, 2 failed tries = 15 min), polish 59 min. Twice the components, three times the time: twice the groups, each 1.9x slower on average (4.9 -> 9.3 min; 4.3 -> 6.9 min without the failed tries) since every group's model holds all components placed so far; a polish solve is 600 s of search plus ~19 min of model set-up at both sizes (1.76 M rows at 500, 2.03 M at 1,000).
- 1,500 components (job 1915): on track until group 60 (21,821, 7 cells, 1,090 components); group 61 (19 new components) was locked at its 300-s limit with a 28.5 % gap: 9 new cells, +8,135 (the fast build accepts every group); since then most groups need 2-3 failed tries and an unlock (16 of its 18 failed tries came after group 61), 38-63 min per group; 16 cells, 29,983 at group 68; build expected to end 11:00-15:00 on 2026-10-30, job limit 16:00 (a time-out in the polish would leave only the placement dumps). Proposed, awaiting the user: keep the fast build but retry a group that ends above ~10 % gap (lock_gap 0.10; here groups 61 and 62).
