Rebuild of the network (300-s limit, 1 % gap): 20,666.05 in 952 s (most of it model set-up), switches S08, S15, S23 (all inside ECUs). Final network clean-up: 20,627.80, OPTIMAL (0 % gap, proven best network for this placement) in 451 s, checker OK (7,575 checks; the only warning is the 5-s cable-length phase). Before -> after: 8 switches (4 boxes S06, S07, S11, S28 + S08, S15, S23, S27) -> 2 (S15, S27, both inside ECUs); 16 -> 8 Ethernet links (a dual star: S02, S19, S23 linked to both S15 and S27, S08 to S27), no relayed route; 12 -> 7 bus lines; hardware 860 -> 722, ports 173.5 -> 166.5, cables 130.69 -> 119.29 USD, 144.9 -> 127.0 m; total 20,784.19 -> 20,627.80 (-156.39; the hand-made what-if was 20,701.30). Rendered in results/scale/netfix_n500_s1_c33/vehicle_layout (75 cables, 126.96 m drawn = costed).
- Gap of a stored design (2026-10-30, user: the % gap of the final output; tools/design_bound.py): the build reports no bound, so the tool computes a valid lower bound of the full model from a counting relaxation on the checker's demands (partitions per ASIL level from the nominal load over the partition caps, at least 2 where a critical component exists; units from partitions / max_partitions, the unit caps, 2 with a critical or redundant component; hardware from the TOPS demand and the standby cells; one port per device of a point-to-point interface; cables, bus lines, switch nodes 0). Result: 500 (pieces10) 20,784.19 vs bound 16,102.56 = gap 22.5 %; 500 with the switch fix 20,627.80 = 21.9 %; 1,000 21,809.68 = 26.2 %; 1,500 35,789.20 = 55.0 %. The slack sits in the partition count (500: 24 vs 19): the bound counts capacity only, while the designs have every cell at its 4-partition limit, because a partition is per cell and ASIL level and the chains and standbys put most ASIL levels on most cells. The flat full model of the 500 (night_flat_n500, 8 h, configs of 2026-10-28) found no design and ended with a best bound of 15,537.92 (root LP 15,371.63, 21 nodes): the model's own LP bound is weaker than the counting bound, so branch and bound cannot certify the gap at this size. Options, not started: (a) a relaxed exact model (placement, partitions, standbys, failure capacity, chain colocation from the latency budgets, no network) for a tighter bound, about a day of work plus hours of solving, outcome uncertain; (b) validate the group-by-group method against the exact optimum on instances the flat solver proves (65-150 components) and report that loss next to the bound-based gap at scale.
- Safety-level polish (2026-10-30, user: even a 10 % distance is too much, attack the partitions): pieces.polish_mode 'level' shares the cell-polish code (pieces._free_sets gives the candidates: cells, or ASIL levels with every component of the level, primary and standby; pieces.polish_fn picks the sequential or the parallel version, also for tools/pieces_polish.py): each round frees one level across the whole car, the rest fixed, re-solved from the current design for polish_time_s, kept if cheaper; fewest components first, levels above polish_max_free skipped; with polish_parallel the levels run at the same time and the cheapest improvement is kept, the others stay candidates (polish_rounds = tries in total); the report line gives the level's partitions and the total before and after (pieces._polish_note). Motivation: a partition belongs to one cell and one level, the cell polish cannot empty a level's partition on a cell that stays open (ASIL 3 of the 500 design: 4 partitions, 3,200 USD, for 1 % of one partition's load; ASIL 0: 3 for 39 %). Smoke test (results/pieces/smoke_levels, lite instance, 4 cells): sequential rounds ran, ASIL 3 improved 9,146.93 -> 9,000.63 (no partition change), clean-up and checker OK.
- Model reuse, "write once" (2026-10-30, user: a polish log of the 1,000 run showed where a 1,750-s polish solve goes: about 19 min of translating the instance into the Gurobi model in Python before Gurobi starts, then 71 s presolve, 472 s root relaxation, and almost no search left of the 600 s; the translation was repeated by every solve of the build, the polish and the clean-up because optimize() builds, solves and discards its model): optimize(reuse=True) keeps the translated model in the optimizer (self._session, keyed by the component and cell ids) and the next optimize(reuse=True) on the same instance skips the three build calls and the chain bounds (the chain block moved up into the translation, before the fixings), restores the placement bounds changed by an earlier fixed / allow, and applies only the fixings, the start and the solver settings of the call; the phase-2 bound constraint is removed at the end of every solve, the Steiner and packing cuts stay (valid for every design). Used by the sequential polish (_polish_cells, also in level mode), _final_network and the rebuild of tools/pieces_polish.py, so a polish run translates once (rebuild) and every later round and the clean-up start solving at once; the build steps and the parallel polish workers (own processes) are unchanged. The level polish therefore runs sequentially (polish_parallel 1): each level starts from the design improved by the previous one, every improvement is kept, one solve uses all threads. Smoke tests (lite instance): tools/pieces_polish.py translated once and reused the model in 6 solves (5 level rounds, clean-up), main.py reused it from polish round 2 on; both checker OK. Real run started 2026-10-30 (job 2902, results/scale/levels_n500_s1_c33: netfix placements, level polish 5 x 600 s sequential, clean-up 1800 s, 64 cores, 96 GB).
- Ablation plan (user 2026-10-30: every modelling and method decision must be shown step by step in the paper's ablation study, from the flat model with everything in it to the group-by-group method). Ladder, in the order the method evolved, each row measured the same way (time to the first checker-valid design, its cost, model rows / columns, bound and gap where one exists; 65 components / 10 cells and 167 / 300 / 500 at 33 cells, rho 1, 64 cores): (1) cable rules static in the model (buses.json upfront_paths + cell_cut_capacity) vs lazy in the callback (default); have: 10 cells 2.3x larger model and no design in 1 h with static, lite instance proved with static, step lab 107 vs 570 candidates; need: flat static vs flat lazy at 65 and 167 with the current code. (2) partition packing: exact per-slot assignment (not implemented; expected ~3x placement decisions) vs pooled capacity + check after the solve with a restart (current) vs pooled + check inside the search (to implement: the packing check in the lazy callback, the cut added there, no restart); have: flat 167 and 300 lost their designs at the restart; need: the in-search variant and flat 167 / 300 with it. (3) flat vs group by group: have the table of 2026-10-30 (65: flat proven 0.6 %, pieces +1.9 % in 17 min; 167: flat no valid design in 1 h 40, pieces valid in 77 min; 300: flat none in 14 h, pieces valid in 3 h; 500: flat none in 8 h, pieces valid in 3.2 h). (4) inside the build: lock rule (accept every step, lock_gap 1.0, vs retry above 10 %; have: 300 / 500 stalled with the rule, fast build delivered, 1,500 lost 14,000 at group 61 without it; need: 1,500 with the rule), static cable rules inside the steps (have: pieces4 vs pieces6), group order heavy-first vs fail-operational-first and group size <= 20 vs <= 60 (need runs, 3 h each at 500). (6) network clean-up + switch housing cost (have: 500 -0.75 %, 8 -> 2 switches). (7) model reuse (speed only; have: 19 min per solve at 1,000 from the logs). Speed-up analysis for the 1,000 (user 2026-10-30 night, from the logs): 500 build (pieces10) 27 steps / 29 solves / 2.2 h: 23 steps ended at the 1 % gap (mean 154 s), 4 at the 300-s limit, 2 no-design retries = 0.6 h; 1,000 build 56 steps / 72 solves / 8.6 h: 29 at the gap (mean 196 s), 27 at the limit (median gap there 1.6 %), 16 no-design solves = 2.7 h (31 % of the build) wasted on doubled retries. Polish rounds are time-starved, not stalled: in stages_cl the improved rounds found their last better design at 550-600 s of 600 (five rounds), so shorter rounds or a stall rule would lose gains; in passes 2-3 of stages_cl 11 of 18 rounds were no-gain repeats of a cell or level nothing had moved in. Levers, in the proposed order: (1) a start design for every build step (the previous design plus the new group placed next to its partners, standby on the second-best cell) so no step starts without a design: removes the retries (2.7 h at 1,000) and should also fix the 1,500 build; with piece_gap 2 % for intermediate steps the limit steps end earlier; (2) skip rule in the polish: a cell / level is re-tried only if a component in it or a partner moved since its last no-gain try (about -1.8 h of 4.5 h at 500); (3) build_parallel 3 (exists, untested at scale): independent groups at once, up to 2x on the step part, one 500 test = an ablation row; (4) levels above polish_max_free split into two cell halves (ASIL 0 at 1,000 has about 400). Estimate at 1,000: today's flow about 14 h (8.6 h build + 2 passes of polish + clean-up) -> about 5 h with (1)-(3). Translation is no longer a lever (once per polish, 15-19 min). [Corrected 2026-11-01: the translation of the 500-component whole-car model takes under a minute (final30 log: polish round 1 built the model, solved 600 s and extracted the design in 653 s; build steps 18-26 carried 13-25 s outside Gurobi); the 10-15 min figures were erratic slowdowns of the Python-side work (step 27 first attempt 785 s outside Gurobi, its retry 110 s for the same model; the polish-only rebuilds 650-940 s), cause not yet pinned down.] Nothing implemented yet. The expectation 'same end, different round' did not hold. Decision: polish_order stays 'fewest' (ablation row: cell order most first 19,813.34 vs fewest first 19,011.58, level order irrelevant). The merge the fewest-first run found is also the move the gap discussion names as the main leftover (ASIL 0 3 -> 2 is done there; the remaining 2,400 are ASIL 2 / 3 / 4).

- Gap discussion for the paper (user 2026-10-30 night; numbers of the best checked 500 design on the old 33 cells, stages_cl job 2935, tools/design_bound.py): design 19,011.58, counting lower bound 16,102.56, gap 15.30 % = 2,909.02. Decomposition of the gap: partitions 22 vs 19 = 2,400.00 (83 %), units 6 vs 5 = 70, hardware 6 vs 3 accelerators = 215, ports 36 vs 21 = 99.94, network 124.08 vs 0 (the bound has no network). Partitions per level (bound = the level's nominal load over the partition caps, rounded up, at least 2 where a critical component exists; design = partitions used, cells they sit in): ASIL 0: 2 vs 2 in 2 cells; ASIL 1: 2 vs 2 in 2 cells; ASIL 2: 9 vs 10 in 5 cells (load 8.69 partitions); ASIL 3: 2 vs 3 in 3 cells (load 0.01); ASIL 4: 4 vs 5 in 5 cells (load 3.39); every used cell holds its maximum of 4 partitions (S02, S15, S19, S23, S27), S30 holds 2. Plain explanation (agreed wording): the bound treats each level as one pile and counts the fewest partitions the pile fills; in the car a partition lives in one cell and holds one level, a cell holds at most 4 partitions and there are 5 levels, so the levels share the cells like a jigsaw and a level is cut into pieces, one per cell it touches; every piece is a whole partition even when nearly empty. What cuts a level: (1) a backup must sit in another cell than its original (the bound knows this one: hence >= 2 partitions per level with a critical component), (2) chain time budgets keep a component near partners of other levels that live in other cells, (3) the 4-partitions-per-cell limit; (2) and (3) are unknown to the bound because it has no cells, and putting cells into it would be the placement problem itself. Judgement (not a proof): two of the three extra partitions look forced, one might be saved, so the design is probably within 4-5 % of the true optimum and the bound 10 % or more below it; Gurobi's own bound of the flat model after 8 h (15,538) is weaker than the counting bound. Paper narrative in three steps: (a) report the counting bound as a valid lower bound and the gap as an upper limit on the distance to the optimum; (b) decompose the gap and state that 83 % is partitions the bound cannot count (no cell-and-level tie), i.e. the bound is loose by construction; (c) anchor the method's quality on the largest instance the flat exact solve proves: 65 components, flat optimum proven to 0.6 % (8,999), group-by-group 1.9 % above it (9,173, plain build, no polish). Planned for the sentence 'exact up to N components, our design within x % of it; beyond that the counting-bound gap' (all on the 30 cells, after the 500 run): the 65 with the full flow (build -> cell -> level -> clean-up), the flat solve at 65 to 0 %, then flat solves at 100 and 150 with 24 h each once the partition-packing check runs inside the callback (the flat 167 lost its designs at the packing restart). Target wording only if the runs support it: measured gap about 10-15 % to a loose bound, exact solution reached up to N, difference < 1 % there. On the 30 curated cells (final30, job 2971, below) the same picture: design 19,774.53, bound 16,102.56 (the counting bound does not depend on the cell set beyond the best derating, 1.0 in both), gap 18.57 % = 3,671.96, of which partitions 23 vs 19 = 3,200 (87 %), hardware 215, network 98, ports 89, units 70; per level bound / design: ASIL 0 2 / 3, ASIL 1 2 / 2, ASIL 2 9 / 10, ASIL 3 2 / 3, ASIL 4 4 / 5. The ASIL 0 merge 3 -> 2 that the 33-cell run found is the one move still missing here (see the final30 bullet).

Checker: the job's own check printed 6 'report' errors, all of them the key order of comm_link_peak_load for three links (SEAT_R_L|DOOR_R_L, SEAT_R_L|DOOR_R_R, KICK_L|DOOR_R_L: optimizer_utils wrote the pair in cell-index order, the checker recomputes it in name order, tools/check_solution._pk; on the S-numbered cells the two orders were the same, so the slip never showed), no design rule failed; sacct shows the job as FAILED only because the checker's exit code ends the sbatch --wrap. Fix 2026-10-31: optimizer_utils writes the key by name order; the checker canonicalises reported keys with _pk (either spelling accepted, the larger value if both occur); the design file untouched; re-check OK (7,262 checks in 23 rules, 1 warning = the cable phase status), stages_cl re-checked OK too. The ASIL 0 merge is not reachable by moving ASIL 0 alone from this design (the pass-2 ASIL 0 round proved no gain in 153 s; every ECU except DOOR_R_R is at the 4-partition limit), it needs a cell move first; the 33-cell run found it in its pass 2 after a different pass-2 cell stage. - Speed-up for 1,000, decisions (user 2026-11-01): measured split of the 1,000 build (8.6 h): 7.1 h inside Gurobi (2.7 h of it in 'no design' attempts thrown away by the retry ladder), 1.5 h outside (translation and reports; 30-60 s per step with spikes of 5-20 min in steps 48, 52, 55, 56); the late steps fail because every candidate breaks a cable rule (bus length, path capacity) and the repair fails; the cpu partition allows 10 days, the 12 h was our choice. Proposals: (1) continue the step's model instead of rebuilding and restarting it on 'no design' (expected -1.5 h at 1,000); (2) memory print per step and round, then free the old model or reduce threads depending on what it shows; (3) skip rule in the polish: dropped by the user (its saving was at most the 1 h 20 of no-gain rounds at 500, likely a quarter to a half); (4) levels above polish_max_free cut into parts by cell: done (pieces._free_sets: ceil(n / max_free) parts, whole cells spread evenly, a part still above the limit cut by id; key 'ASIL a part i/n', _polish_note parses the level from the second word; test on the 500 design with max_free 100: ASIL 0 198 -> parts by cell [115, 83] -> [58, 57, 83], ASIL 4 152 -> [74, 78]); (5) parallel build later, for 1,500 only. Decisions on (1) and (2) pending. Corrections (2026-11-01, user asked whether every step translates): the polish and the clean-up translate once (verified in the final30 log: round 1 built the whole-car model, rounds 2-22 and the clean-up reused it); the build translates once per step by design (the step's model holds only the components placed so far plus the new group), 20-50 s per step at 500, about 15 min over the whole build, not the problem; the big overheads (final30 step 27: 785 s before the solve on the first attempt, 110 s on the retry of the same model; 1,000 run steps 48 / 52 / 55 / 56: 5-20 min) are erratic and not translation. The 295 GB MaxRSS belonged to job 2959 = order_most (a 500 polish-only run), not to the 1,000 run (job 1914, sc_pieces_n1000, which ran on the login node zits-slurmlogin1 alongside the 1,500 run 1915 and other users' work; worker nodes have 256 CPUs and 2 TB, the login node 256 CPUs and 980 GB). MaxRSS of the 1,000 run (1914) 66 GB, of the 1,500 run (1915) 71 GB: memory is not a problem at these sizes; proposal (2) is left with only the unexplained spikes to chase (about 50 min at 1,000).
- Item 1 implemented (2026-11-01, user go): optimizer.optimize(resume=True) solves the kept model of the previous optimize(reuse=True) on for time_limit more seconds when that call ended at its phase-1 time limit without a design (self._resumable); nothing of the model is touched (no bounds reset, no fixings, no start, no objective set again), so Gurobi continues its search where it stopped and keeps the lazy constraints of the earlier attempt; pieces._build_level calls every step with reuse=True and, on 'no design', solves on with the extra time doubling the total (300 -> +300 -> +600, piece_time_max_s) before unlocking the previous group (the 'retrying with N s' rebuild is gone; report rows of the extra attempts carry resumed=True); an INFEASIBLE step still unlocks at once. Side effect: the last build step's model (the whole car) is the session the polish's first round reuses, so the polish translates nothing. tools/pieces_step_lab.py --resume S tests it on one dumped step. Measurement for the speed-up discussion (1,000 log): the Python lazy callback took 3.0 h of the 7.0 h of phase-1 solve time over the build (43 %; 60-73 % in many steps, e.g. step 40: 186 of 254 s). Lab jobs 2026-11-01: 3155 = step 27 of final30 with 300 s + resume 300 s (results/scale/lab27_resume.txt); 3156 = the same step under cProfile for the callback hot spots (results/scale/lab27_profile.txt). Config set for the 500 on the corrected cells prepared: results/scale/final30b_n500_s1_c30_cfg (final30 cfg + new vehicle.json, layout regenerated: 30 cells, 70 entities, no unreachable pair, no overlap warning; dump_dir set to the final30b output dir); the run waits for the lab result.
- Lab results 2026-11-01 (jobs 3155 / 3156, step 27 of final30 replayed standalone, 300 s, NoRelHeurTime=20, 64 threads): the step found a design at 300 s this time (cost 21,883.97, gap 1.74 %; in the run the first 300-s attempt had none), so the resume path was not exercised; job 3176 repeats it with 100 s + 200 s. Wall 1,032 s for a 305-s solve: the cProfile run (3156) shows where the time outside Gurobi goes: optimizer._add_constraints 877 s, of which tree_bound 869 s = 1.23 million steiner_tree calls (optimizer_utils, 648 s own time): the upfront bus-line length bounds (device groups of up to 4 members, each with every cell and every pair of cells: with 10 CAN devices and 30 cells about 385 groups x 466 unit choices per bus type) are computed when their devices first appear in a step's sub-architecture; the module cache _STEINER_CACHE makes later calls in the same process free (that is why the main run's polish round 1 built the whole-car model in under a minute while the polish-only tool needs 11-15 min: a fresh process recomputes the trees). This explains the 'erratic' spikes: they are the steps where new devices join (1,000 run steps 48 / 52 / 55 / 56 with 33-40 devices). Inside the solve, the callback's time is in scenario_states (24 candidates x 2.7 s): for every single-failure scenario (27 at 500, 51 at 1,000) and every flow (about 3,000) flow_alive calls ends_of(t), a scan of the flow's endpoint variables, again and again (ends_of 5.4 million calls, the value lambda 170 million calls, 46 s); the endpoints of a candidate do not change between scenarios. Proposed engineering fixes (no change of the model, the bounds or the checks): (A) Steiner trees cached on disk per config set (key: point ids + distances) and the first computation parallelised, so every run and tool pays once or nothing; (B) in the callback, the endpoints, silent flags and positions of a candidate computed once per candidate and reused over the scenarios. Expected at 1,000: (A) removes most of the 1.5 h outside Gurobi, (B) gives the search back a large part of the 3.0 h the callback took (43 % of the solve time).

Candidate new default: 'pairs' (fastest to the target) or 'one_unit' (best design at 284 s); one step of evidence each, the polish rounds (600 s, whole car, the same 1.46 M rows today) not yet measured. Ablation row: bus-length bounds full vs pairs. Decision pending (user).

- Fixes 2026-11-01 (all in, nothing committed): (1) memory guard in optimizer.optimize: SoftMemLimit = memory.soft_share (0.75) and MemLimit = memory.hard_share (0.9) of the job's memory (SLURM_MEM_PER_NODE, else the cgroup limit; no limit without one) less what the process holds outside Gurobi (configs/solver.json 'memory', new section; main.py and tools/pieces_polish.py pass it to the optimizer). Gurobi checks MemLimit at every allocation and SoftMemLimit only where a solve can stop gracefully. The soft limit ends the solve with its incumbent (status MEM_LIMIT handled like a time limit; phase 2 skipped, it would start the same root solve again); the hard limit raises inside Gurobi and is caught: no design from that solve, the model dropped and rebuilt by the next solve. (2) The model of a previous instance is disposed before a new one is translated (since item 1 two models were alive during every build step). (3) Memory line after every solve, build step and polish round: process RSS now / peak and Gurobi's peak (item 2 of the speed-up list, now with a purpose: the lines show how close a run comes). (4) Checkpoint pieces_polish.json in dump_dir after every improving polish round (the step-dump format plus 'polish'); tools/pieces_polish.py starts from it when it exists (--step K = a build step instead). run_info.json of final30b written by hand (the run never got to write one) so the tools can use its dumps. Tests: T1 (job 3249, results/scale/memtest_T1.txt, script results/scale/memtest_run/memtest.py): the step-27 design of final30b rebuilt with 'pairs' bounds under MemLimit 0.5 GB (expected: caught, no design, model dropped), then normally (rebuilt), then SoftMemLimit 0.5 GB on the kept model (graceful stop), then normally again on the kept model. T2 (job 3247, results/scale/memtest_T2.txt): tools/pieces_polish.py on the final30b build with 'pairs' bounds, 3 cell rounds of 120 s, no clean-up (results/scale/memtest_cfg, memtest_run, memtest_out): expected pieces_polish.json after the first improving round and a checker line at the end. The approved run resubmitted at once with the guard (job 3248, final30c_n500_s1_c30, config results/scale/final30c_n500_s1_c30_cfg = final30b's plus the memory section; full bounds, 600 GB, 12 h, 64 threads), to be cancelled and resubmitted if a test fails. Recommendation unchanged, now stronger: 'pairs' bounds make the model 5.5x smaller and the root LP 15x smaller (the barrier is the memory hog), so they remove the risk at its source and speed the polish rounds up; the twin run still waits for the user's go.

- Fix B done 2026-11-01 (user: implement it; question 'what if the placement changed?': it cannot inside one callback call, the candidate is one snapshot vals taken at the top of the call, the scenarios differ only in the failed element, and the memo dicts are created in the call and die with it; the repair keeps the placement and only re-routes). optimizer.lazy_cb: per call, ends_of_t / ends_of (one function now) scan a flow's endpoint variables once and keep them in _ends; silent(t) keeps its flag in _silent; the route configuration of a flow (w_if, p, path, cfg) in a routes object is scanned once by _flow_cfg_scan and kept in _fcfg keyed by (id(routes_), t) with the object itself stored against id reuse; flow_alive returns a fresh copy of cfg as before; pos_x / pos_r were already once per call. Rules, cuts and checks unchanged. New: _cb_stats['time_s'] = seconds inside the callback (printed in the periodic and the after-phase lines); DSE_CB_VERIFY=1 (environment, test runs only) re-scans every memoised value the old way at the end of every call and raises on any difference, counted in _cb_stats['verified']. Test on step 27 of final30, 'pairs' bounds, 300 s, NoRelHeurTime=20, 64 threads (tools/pieces_step_lab.py results/scale/lab27_pairs 27): B0 = old logic + timer (job 3304, results/scale/lab27_B0.txt): phase 1 at the limit (301.9 s, 4,086 nodes), 89 candidates, callback 141.5 s = 1.59 s per candidate = 47 % of the solve; both phases 92 candidates, 144.8 s; design 21,767.55, gap 1.23 %. B1 = fix B + self-check (job 3305, lab27_B1.txt): phase 1 converged at 127.7 s (215 nodes), 49 candidates, callback 39.0 s = 0.80 s per candidate; both phases 144 candidates, 96.9 s = 0.67 s per candidate with the self-check still on; verified 144 of 144 calls, no difference; design 21,659.27, gap 0.99 %. Per candidate the callback is 2.3x faster (1.57 -> 0.67 s). The search path is not comparable between runs (NoRelHeurTime is wall-clock and the threads race: job 3178 converged at 119 s with the old code, B0 did not in 300 s), so the gain is read per candidate, not from the end times. The running 1,000 (job 3263) loaded the old code at its start and does not profit; every run started from now on does. Fix A (Steiner trees cached on disk) is not implemented: with 'pairs' bounds no trees are computed upfront, only those of the proposed designs' buses, so there is little left to save.
- Fix B, plain mode (job 3306, results/scale/lab27_B2.txt, 60 s, 16 threads, self-check off): ran through normally, 22 candidates, callback 24.5 s, no design in the 60 s (expected for this step at 16 threads); no error. Fix B is in for every run started from now on.

- Polish changes A / B / C (user 2026-11-02 13:45, go; after the 1,000 fix B result: 78 % of its gap is 3 partitions above the counting bound, the cell stage skipped the two biggest cells and the level stage never reached its big parts). pieces.py, three config keys in solver.json pieces, defaults = the old behaviour: (A) polish_split_cells (false): in the cell stage a cell above polish_max_free is cut into parts by ASIL level, the same way a level is cut by cell (key '<cell> part i/n', each part its own round; _polish_note reads the cell name before ' part ' for the closed-cell note); (B) polish_order 'partitions': levels and parts ordered by the level's partitions in the design, most first, then fewest components (ASIL 2 with 9 first at 1,000; the older 'waste' order = partitions beyond the raw load, untested, is the alternative), and polish_all (false): every candidate of a stage gets a round in a pass, polish_rounds no longer caps it; (C) polish_min_gain (0.0): in _polish_stages a pass gaining less than this share of the cost ends the polish, used with polish_passes 4 instead of the fixed 2. Dry test on the 1,000 fix B design: cell stage old = 3 candidates (SEAT_R_R 50, DASH_R 82, DOOR_F_L 99), with A = 11 (KICK_R 413 -> 4 parts, BAY_L 355 -> 2, DOOR_R_R 240 -> 2); level order with B: ASIL 2, ASIL 4 parts, ASIL 0 parts and ASIL 3, ASIL 1. - Gap decomposition of the two decided designs (user 2026-11-02 14:40; tools/design_bound.py, counting bound 16,102.56 for both sizes; 'hardware' in the tool = accelerators and switches together, 169 per accelerator, 23 per switch). 500 pairs + fix B (final30p, 18,197.13, gap 11.51 % = 2,094.57): partitions 21 vs 19 = 1,600 (76 % of the gap; per ASIL 0/1/2/3/4 design 2/2/9/3/5, bound 2/2/9/2/4); ECUs 6 vs 5 = 70; accelerators 4 vs 3 and switches 2 vs 0 = 215; ports 35 vs 21 = 97; cabling and links (cable 70.78 + comm 41.36, 12 links, 111.5 m) vs 0 = 112. 1,000 pairs + fix B (final30pB, 19,165.82, gap 15.98 % = 3,063.26): partitions 22 vs 19 = 2,400 (78 %; design 3/2/9/3/5, bound 2/2/9/2/4); ECUs 6 vs 5 = 70; accelerators 5 vs 3 and switches 2 vs 0 = 384; ports 38 vs 21 = 108; cabling and links (cable 74.93 + comm 26.39, 11 links, 110.1 m) vs 0 = 101. In points of the gap at 1,000: switches 0.2, cabling 0.5, ports 0.6, everything else (partitions, accelerators, ECU) 14.7 of the 15.98; at 500 the three small rows give 1.2 of the 11.51. The bound has no cabling, links or switches at all (it cannot prove a minimum without placements; 0 is the safe value), so those rows are entirely bound weakness, about 1 point. Reading per level: ASIL 2 sits on its bound (9 = 9, load 8.7 partitions); ASIL 0 is 3 at 1,000 but 2 at 500 (the 500 polish merged it), so likely a design slack; ASIL 3 (3 vs 2 at both sizes) and ASIL 4 (5 vs 4 at both) are the suspects for bound weakness: the counting bound ignores the single-failure capacity rule (the standbys of a failed cell's critical components must fit in the surviving partitions; ASIL 4 has 148 critical of 279 at 1,000 and its 4 partitions would sit at 85 % load) and the separation of a primary from its standby, which spread a level over more cells than the load needs; the same for accelerators (5 vs 3: accelerators are per ECU, the bound ignores standby copies and placement). Possible bound improvements, not done: (1) partitions per level with the single-failure capacity rule; (2) a cable minimum from every device's distance to its nearest candidate cell.
- Result figure for the paper, literature check (user 2026-11-02 15:20: the current render shows nothing a reader can validate; wanted: which hardware sits where, partitions, buses, utilisation, as a picture). Three figure families in the literature: (a) mapping / specification-graph view of the DSE community (application graph of tasks and messages, architecture graph of ECUs and buses, mapping edges; Graf, Glass, Teich, Lauer, DATE 2014 'Multi-variant-based design space exploration for automotive embedded systems' and the SystemCoDesigner line): structure only, unreadable above ~50 tasks; (b) topology / deployment schematic of the software-architecture community and the tools (Bucaioni, Pelliccione, ICSA 2020 'Technical architectures for automotive systems'; Kugele, Hettler, Peter, ICSA 2018 'Data-centric communication and containerization for future automotive software architectures'; Vector PREEvision network diagram with ECU-load and bus-load reports; fortiss AutoFOCUS3 deployment synthesis; UML deployment diagrams): ECUs as boxes, buses as lines with drops, backbone links, software inside the boxes, numbers as bars or tables; (c) physical / harness view of the zonal papers (ITEC Asia-Pacific 2025, Stuttgart IFTE / TU Dresden Aranea: location covering + multi-commodity-flow routing; TU Dresden 'Efficient architecture evaluation and generation of automotive wiring harnesses'): car top view with zone controllers and routed harness, metrics (length, weight, cost against the number of zones) as bar charts. Excluded as venues: MDPI Energies 2023 (Maier, Reuss, k-means + Dijkstra zonal design), Electronics 2022 (Askaripoor et al. synthesis survey), Sensors 2024; IEEE Access 2025 (Hossain, Quddus, 12-zone clustering) borderline, not used. Our render is family (c) only and overloaded (46 device badges + every cable); none of the validating numbers is visible. Proposal (not built yet): one composite figure per design (500 and 1,000): panel 1 = the top view with only the used ECUs as boxes (accelerator and switch glyphs on the box), the 6 bus lines as one coloured polyline each, backbone links thick, peripheral cables thin grey, no device badges; panel 2 = deployment schematic, one column per ECU with its partitions stacked and coloured by ASIL, each with a CPU and a RAM fill bar (utilisation %) and its component count, ports as small squares by interface type under the ECU, bus lines across the bottom with type, device count, length against the limit, backbone links with peak load against capacity; strip = partitions per ASIL and units / accelerators, design against the counting bound. Everything is in solution_1.json (assignment, partition_counts, standby, buses with length, devices and units, comm_links, comm_link_peak_load, hw_features, interfaces) plus the component loads of the architecture; tool to write: tools/vehicle_viz/design_figure.py (matplotlib, PDF for the paper), panel 1 over the existing top_raw.png.

Panels: A = the top-view render cropped to the car with only the used ECUs labelled (name + GPU / switch words), cables of the render by type; B = partitions per ASIL level and units / accelerators / switches / ports, design (filled) against the counting bound (outline), plus the headline numbers (cost, bound, gap, cost split, cable split, standbys); C = deployment schematic: one column per ECU, partitions stacked (fill = ASIL level, blue ramp light to dark), each with CPU and RAM utilisation bars (nominal load = primaries + hot standbys over the derated partition cap, the checker's partition_capacity rule) and 'N comp +h hot +c cold', ports as squares by type, backbone links as arcs with 'IFACE load %' (comm_link_peak_load over the cable type's capacity), shared bus lines (CAN / LIN) under the columns with devices, ECUs, length and device load / capacity. - Result figure, second version (user 2026-11-02 16:20: no bound in this figure, it shows one Pareto solution and its emergent archetype; panel B dropped; the car more transparent, the ECUs prominent, cables kept; ECUs typed and coloured by an emergent type). tools/vehicle_viz/design_figure.py rewritten: panel A = the render faded (alpha 0.32) as context, on it the routed cables of <solution>_cables.json as vector lines by type (ECU-to-ECU links thicker), the devices (circle sensor / square actuator) and the used ECUs as boxes in the type colour with name and component count (box size with the count); car metres -> image pixels by an affine map fitted on the render's anchors (top_anchors.json: sites and devices; outliers = badges moved off their marker dropped, mean error 0.005 of the width); summary box = ECU types with members, partitions per ASIL, accelerators, switches, ports, links, bus lines, cable split, components / critical / standbys, cost, and the type rule; panel B = the deployment schematic as before, headers in the type colour, columns ordered by type, 'ports · sensors, actuators' under each. ECU type rule (constants COMPUTE_CPU 0.40, IO_DEVICES 6, IO_BUSES 3): compute-heavy = accelerator on the unit or CPU load >= 40 % of the unit cap; I/O-heavy = >= 6 devices attached or >= 3 shared bus lines; both = zonal compute, compute only = central compute, I/O only = zonal I/O, neither = small controller. Colours: types = dataviz palette slots 2-5 (orange, aqua, yellow, magenta; node is not on the cluster, the validator could not run, the slots are the pre-validated order), ASIL = blue ramp, cables = the render's. On the 1,000 fix B design: central compute DASH_R (57 components, CPU 73 % of the unit, 13 TOPS, switch) and KICK_R (330, 41 %, software consolidation, 1 device); zonal compute BAY_L (298, 63 %, 17 devices, 5 bus lines), DOOR_F_L (82, 99 %, 19 TOPS, 10 sensors: the perception node), DOOR_R_R (184, 47 %, switch, 6 rear cameras); zonal I/O SEAT_R_R (49, 0.1 %, 5 devices, 3 bus lines). Output results/vehicle_layout/final30pB_n1000/design_figure.pdf / .png.
- Result figure, third version (user 2026-11-02 17:00: no summary box, descriptions go to the caption; the car only as a silhouette, no interior or installation space, ECUs / sensors / actuators / cables must stand out; ECU labels E1..En; the ECU types must be discussed and grounded in the literature; the backbone arcs in panel B looked bad). tools/vehicle_viz/design_figure.py: panel A = the car's silhouette (the render's non-background pixels closed, hole-filled, largest piece; light grey fill, thin outline) with the routed cables, devices and the ECU boxes labelled E1..En (order = role, then component count; the E -> cell mapping is printed for the caption); panel B = the deployment columns with 'E1 KICK_R' headers, the backbone links as an upper-triangle matrix (interface, peak load / capacity, grey fill by load) instead of arcs, the bus lines below; one row, A left, B right (18 x 7.4 in). ECU roles now the five node roles of the E/E literature on three measured axes (constants COMPUTE_CPU 0.40, IO_DEVICES 6, IO_BUSES 3, DOMAIN_SHARE 0.50; ADAS + Sensing count as one domain 'automated driving', Platform left out): compute & domain -> domain controller; compute & I/O -> zone controller + compute; compute alone -> central compute; I/O alone -> zone controller; neither -> edge ECU. Literature behind the roles: Bosch (vehicle computers across domains + zone ECUs that connect the computing layer to sensors and actuators; the 2015 serial roadmap distributed -> domain -> cross-domain -> vehicle-centralised / zone-oriented), Wang, Guo, Cao et al., Automotive Innovation 7(1) 2024 'Review of E/E architectures for autonomous vehicles: topologies, networking and simulators' (domain-oriented = functional concentration, zone-oriented = location concentration, hybrid, parallel roadmap), Bucaioni and Pelliccione ICSA 2020 (feature model and three reference architectures), Bucaioni et al., Advanced Engineering Informatics 59, 2024 'Modelling centralised automotive E/E software architectures', Jiang, SAE 2026-01-0071 'Evolutionary paths toward vehicle zonal E/E architecture'; vendor definitions (NXP, Infineon: zone controller = edge aggregation of sensors / actuators + gateway to the central compute; domain controller = one per functional area; HPC = the compute-intensive workloads). On the 1,000 fix B design: E1 KICK_R central compute (330 components, CPU 41 %, largest domain 26 %), E2 DOOR_F_L domain controller automated driving (82, CPU 99 %, 10 sensors, 54 % ADAS + Sensing), E3 DASH_R domain controller chassis (57, CPU 73 %, switch, 52 % Chassis), E4 BAY_L zone controller + compute (298, 70 %, 17 devices, 5 bus lines), E5 DOOR_R_R zone controller + compute (184, 47 %, switch, 6 cameras), E6 SEAT_R_R zone controller (49, CPU 0 %, 5 devices, 3 bus lines) = a hybrid architecture. Thresholds are a proposal for the user to settle.

- ECU role discussion, device locality (user 2026-11-03: a zone controller must take the I/O of its own area, otherwise it is no zone): checked on polishABC_n1000 with the routed cables (40 cabled devices). In the model a device is always cabled to the unit that hosts its reading / driving component (point-to-point) or to a bus line that reaches that unit; there is no zone gateway that terminates a local device and forwards over the backbone. Result: only 7 of the 40 devices are hosted on their nearest used ECU; straight-line device-to-host distance 59.6 m in total against 25.6 m to the nearest ECU; device cables 62.1 m of the 118.0 m; cable cost 72 of 17,510 = 0.4 %, so locality is barely rewarded. Per ECU (hosted devices / of them local / devices whose nearest ECU it is): E1 3/0/4, E2 15/4/13, E3 7/1/8, E4 1/0/5, E5 7/0/2, E6 4/0/2, E7 3/2/6. Consequence: the current I/O axis (hosted devices, bus lines) does not measure zonal aggregation; 'zone controller' is not justified by location for E2–E5. Options given to the user, nothing changed: (1) define the I/O axis by local devices (the unit is the nearest used ECU of the device) plus a hosting-share criterion for central compute, which would read this design as several compute units plus edge ECUs, not zonal; (2) make locality matter in the model (higher cable cost weight or a zone-gateway rule), a modelling decision = ablation row and new runs.
- Zone rule fixed in the role taxonomy (user 2026-11-03: the groups stay, but 'zone controller' is wrong unless the unit connects to the devices near it). tools/vehicle_viz/design_figure.py: the I/O axis is now 'connected to at least IO_LOCAL = 4 nearby devices', nearby = the used ECU nearest to the device (straight line in the top view), connected = the unit hosts the device's software or sits on the device's bus line; hosted devices from elsewhere and bus-line counts no longer make a unit zonal (IO_DEVICES / IO_BUSES removed). New function local_devices(); the figure's device row is now 'nearby devices it connects to' with the grey track = the devices in its zone, the fill = the connected ones, text 'k of n', the hosted-device count as the small line, threshold tick at 4; the bus-line row stays as information without a tick. Roles with the fixed rule: polishABC_n1000: E1 DOOR_F_L central compute (3 of 9 nearby), E2 BAY_L central compute (3 of 5), E3 DOOR_F_R central compute (0 of 3), E4 SEAT_R_R zone controller + compute (6 of 13, the only zonal unit), E5 KICK_R edge ECU (323 components, CPU 28 %, 0 of 2), E6 DOOR_R_R and E7 DASH_R edge ECUs. final30pB_n1000: E1 KICK_R and E2 DOOR_R_R central compute, E3 DOOR_F_L and E4 DASH_R domain controllers, E5 BAY_L zone controller + compute (5 of 5 nearby, 14 hosted), E6 SEAT_R_R edge ECU (3 of 13). - Two questions on the A+B+C 1,000 design (user 2026-11-03), answered from the config and the solution. (1) Why are the device connections scattered instead of local: prices (buses.json / partitions.json): cable ETH 1.5 USD/m, CAN / LIN / UART 0.5 USD/m; partition 800; unit 70; port 4–4.25. The device cables are 85.5 m (52 USD); wiring every device to its nearest ECU would save about 34 m straight-line, i.e. 20–50 USD = 0.1–0.3 % of 17,510, while one partition is worth 530–1,600 m of cable; so placement is decided by partition packing (ASIL, caps, max 3 partitions per ASIL per unit) and the cables follow; there is also no gateway rule (a device is always wired to the unit hosting its software). The modelled harness (40 device cables + backbone, 118 m) is a small fraction of a real harness, so locality is under-weighted in the objective. Options, none started: cable-cost sensitivity (x10, x50; full runs), or a zone-gateway rule in the model (device terminated at the nearest unit, data forwarded over the backbone) = the zonal model, an ablation row. (2) Why 4 accelerators: not the TOPS (15.2 of 26 per module); the 8 traffic-light containers (one per camera 6–13, ASIL 2) each need 89 % CPU and 69 % RAM of a partition, so each fills its own ASIL-2 partition; max_per_asil_per_site 3 limits a unit to 3 of them; so they spread over 4 units (3 + 2 + 2 + 1), and every unit hosting one needs an accelerator (169 USD, cheaper than any alternative). Calibration point: the container's CPU share (cpu_mean_pct measured on the A100 host, host-to-target scaled) may include GPU-wait time; a lower CPU demand per container would cut the partitions and the accelerators.
- To do (user 2026-11-03): cable-cost sensitivity runs (cable cost x10 and x50 on the 1,000, A+B+C polish) to see at which weight zonal wiring appears; not started.
- Paper material, harness and locality (user 2026-11-03: keep for the paper). (a) Why the model's cable total is only 118 m: a real harness holds every wire of the car, power supply and ground to every unit and device plus signal wires for hundreds of switches, lamps, motors and sensors, 1,500 to 3,000 wires, kilometres of copper, 30 to 60 kg, one of the most expensive parts of the car; the model routes only what the placement decision changes: the backbone links between the ECUs (32.5 m in the 1,000 design) and one cable per device (40 devices, 85.5 m); no power, no ground, no per-signal wires; the device count is small because the architecture samples functions from the catalogue, each with a handful of sensors and actuators. (b) The device cables total 85.5 m, about 52 USD; wiring every device to its nearest ECU would save about 34 m, 20 to 50 USD, 0.1 to 0.3 % of the cost; one partition (800 USD) is worth 500 to 1,600 m of cable; so the solver places a sensor's software wherever an ASIL partition has room and the cables follow. (c) Why it matters: in reality the harness is the main reason OEMs go zonal; in our objective the harness is 72 USD of 17,510 (0.4 %); the model cannot feel that pressure, so the exact optimum does not go zonal; not a solver bug, the model's harness is a small slice of the real one. (d) Decision (user 2026-11-03): add a zone-gateway rule to the model (a device may be wired to any used unit, which forwards its data over the backbone to the host); test it on the 500 first, then the 1,000; the modelling note comes before the code.
- CORRECTION (2026-11-03) to the 'no gateway rule' statement above: the model already has a gateway mechanism for Ethernet devices (optimizer.py attach_s / attach_a: an ETH sensor or actuator attaches to any active unit, the data is routed over the backbone to the host; constraint 9-ATT). In polishABC_n1000 all 8 cameras, the rear lidar and the amplifier attach to SEAT_R_R (the switch unit), the lidars front / left pillar to BAY_L, right pillar / top to KICK_R, while their hosts are KICK_R, DOOR_F_R, DOOR_F_L: the attachment is chosen by port and switch economics (a 16th of a switch is cheaper than an ECU port) and link capacity, not by distance, because cable is cheap. What has no gateway: the bus devices (CAN / LIN, 21 of the 40 devices: the bus line must reach the host unit, so a local unit cannot terminate the line and forward over the backbone) and the point-to-point SPI / UART devices (4, wired to the host). Counting by the physical cable end, 12 of the 40 devices end at their nearest ECU (7 by host). design_figure.py now labels every device with the unit its cable ends at (cable_end(): ETH attachment, otherwise the host) and counts 'nearby devices connected' by it. Consequence for the gateway decision: the missing mechanism is a CAN / LIN gateway (a zone unit terminates a bus line and forwards its signals over the backbone); the ETH side needs no new mechanism, only a realistic harness weight.
- Modelling note, bus gateway (2026-11-03, user go; config key configs/buses.json backbone.bus_gateway, default false = the old rule, so it is an ablation row). Old rule (constraint 11): a bus device (CAN / LIN / FlexRay) sits on its lane lines together with the unit of every component that drives it and, with failures on, that component's standby unit; its data crosses the bus once. New rule with bus_gateway true: every lane of a device (line b that carries d) has exactly one gateway unit g(d, b), a member unit of that line; the device's data is forwarded between the gateway and the unit of every user instance over the backbone, as a data flow per lane, user and instance (types BUS_SC for sensors, SC_BUS for actuators; volume and kbit as the device's); the user's unit no longer has to be on the line (when the gateway is the user's own unit the flow stays inside the unit and costs nothing). Any active unit on the line may be the gateway, no switch needed; it has the bus port by membership. Variables g[d, b, j] with g <= bus_unit[b, j], g <= bus_dev[b, d], sum_j g[d, b, j] = bus_dev[b, d]. Failures: the lanes of a device are alternatives (variants of one base flow per device and user, keyed 'a<k>' per line k): an instance is served if at least one lane's flow is alive; a lane's flow dies with its line, with its gateway cell, or with its backbone path as any flow; the gateway's failure is in scope (unlike the Ethernet attachment, which stays out of scope), because the two lanes of a critical device exist for exactly that; a single-lane device of non-critical software is lost with its gateway (degraded mode). Latency: the device's own bus crossing stays (constant per chain end) plus the forwarding flow's backbone path, which enters the chain's flow list through the device's base flow; the nominal and degraded checks pick the first active lane variant (an inactive lane has no endpoints). Capacity: the forwarding flows load the backbone like any flow (nominal: every active lane; standby variants after failures), and the cell-cut / upfront-path options see them through flow_ends. Ports: unchanged (one bus port per line membership). Costs: nothing new; the forwarding may need Ethernet links and ports where none were; the saving is that units no longer join the lines of their devices' software (shorter lines, fewer bus ports). Reporting: solution key bus_gateways {device: {line: unit}}; flow_routes entries of the new types with src_inst / dst_inst 'a<k>'. Checker: parses bus_gateways; with gateways present the users-on-the-line rule is replaced by one gateway per lane on the line; BUS_SC / SC_BUS base flows with lane variants; a lane variant's endpoint 'a<k>' is the gateway of lane k; a line failure kills the lane variant of that line. Figure: a bus device's cable end = its first lane's gateway. Untouched: pieces.py (the gateway lives inside every solve), the bound tool (cables 0), solution_paths.py (line trees as before).
- Bus gateway implemented (2026-11-03, per the modelling note above; the files before the change are kept only in the session scratchpad). optimizer.py: bus['gateway'] / bus['gw'] variables (after the bus-line variables), lane flows BUS_SC / SC_BUS with lane variants 'a<k>' and their standby variants (base = the device-user pair), flow endpoints from the gateway variables, constraint 11's users-on-the-line rule skipped under the gateway and replaced by bus_gw_member / bus_gw_lane / bus_gw_one, chain ends include the lane flows (flow_into / flow_from), the nominal chain check takes the active lane (active_lane()), instance_served treats the lanes as alternatives, flow_alive kills a lane with its own line, every open line is a failure scenario under the gateway, VARIANT_ORDER gets the lane keys, the callback watches the gateway variables. optimizer_utils.py: failure_report order and act_outputs for the new types, flow_routes entries with src / dst = device / component and lane_line, solution key bus_gateways. tools/check_solution.py: parses bus_gateways and bus_lines, builds BUS_SC / SC_BUS base flows with lane variants, ends() resolves 'a<k>' to the gateway of line k, alive() kills a lane with its line, variant order per instance (self.variant_order), the users-on-the-line rule replaced by one gateway per lane on the line (+ no gateway on a line the device is not on), line scenarios include the lanes' lines, chain ends include the lane flows, nominal_variant() mirrors active_lane(). configs/buses.json backbone.bus_gateway false (+ comment); tools/vehicle_viz/design_figure.py cable_end() uses the first lane's gateway. Smoke test: configs/full_architecture_v2_lite.json, 4 cells, rho 1 with failures, 900 s, gateway off (job 3509) and on (job 3510), results/gateway_test/lite_gw_{off,on}; then the 500 pair: results/scale/gw30_n500_s1_c30 (gateway on) and nogw30_n500_s1_c30 (off), both with the A+B+C polish, configs prepared with their own dump_dir.
- Gateway smoke tests on the lite instance (4 cells, rho 1 with failures, 900 s cost phase): gateway off (job 3509) 8,975.60, gap 0.36 %, checker OK (the patched code reproduces the old model; the regression value 8,978.25 of 2026-10-24 predates the port-cost decision of 10-29). Gateway on, first attempt (job 3510): no design in 900 s, all 659 candidates rejected (failure survival 627, chain latency 528, degraded latency 571): the nominal chain check took the first lane with a backbone crossing although the lanes are redundant channels and the function uses the best one, so every design that keeps the standby reachable (second gateway at another cell) looked over its chain budget. Fix: optimizer_utils.nominal_lane() / lane_keys(): the lane whose gateway is the user's own unit (no crossing) comes first, then the first lane with both ends placed; used by the callback's nominal chain check and scenario picks, by the failure and chain reports, and mirrored in the checker (variant_keys(), nominal_variant()). Second attempt: job 3511.
- Polish test A+C ended (job 3480, 2026-11-03, 2 h 41): final cost **17,390.82**, gap 7.41 %, checker OK (cable phase of the clean-up at TIME_LIMIT as usual), 6 ECUs, 20 partitions (2/2/9/2/5), 4 accelerators, 2 switches, cable 109.5 m; passes 20,729.58 → 19,234.59 → 18,221.31 → 17,399.49 → no gain in pass 4 (47 + 51 + 24 + 33 min); clean-up OPTIMAL in 153 s. Better than A+B+C (17,510.22, 6 h 01) in cost and time: B (partition order + polish_all) hurts. Decision by the agreed rule: A+C is the default from now on: pieces.py DEFAULTS polish_split_cells true, polish_passes 4, polish_min_gain 0.001 (polish_order 'fewest', polish_all false); the 500 gateway pair configs switched to A+C. The best checked 1,000 design is polishAC_n1000 (17,390.82); its render and figure follow (results/vehicle_layout/polishAC_n1000).

(b) rho 1 with failures: the second attempt (job 3511, lane preference fix) still rejected every candidate (846); the gateway debug print (DSE_GW_DEBUG=1) showed arbitrary gateways because the relaxation saw no price for a remote gateway; two LP-level additions: the chain lower-bound crossing variable of a bus device's flow is now cross >= x[user, j] - sum of the device's gateways at j (a crossing only when no lane's gateway sits at the user's unit), and the two lanes of a critical device end at different units (bus_gw_apart: both gateways in one cell would die with it); jobs 3514 (cross rule only) and 3517 (both) running with 1,800 s.

Repair change before the start: under the gateway rule the repair re-places every lane's gateway (first lane at the unit of the software, second at the standby's unit when on the line; memos cleared); the flat lite tests with failures (jobs 3517, 3524, 3525) still had no design after 10-16 min (placement-driven chain latency violations in the repairs); their outcome is recorded when they end.
- Cable-cost sensitivity, plan (user 2026-11-03: measure it; which multiplier is realistic). Two anchors for a defensible multiplier: (1) installed cost of a device connection: the model prices one signal pair (0.5 USD/m bus, 1.5 USD/m Ethernet, wire price x 2 harness factor); a real device connection adds the power and ground cores (an actuator's 2 x 2.5 mm2 FLRY cores cost about as much again as the signal pair per metre), connectors, protection and routing labour: about x3 to x5 per metre; (2) harness share: the modelled cables are 0.4 % of the design cost, a real harness is of the order of 5-10 % of the E/E cost: x12 to x25. Proposal: a sweep x1 (the pair above), x3, x10, x30 on the 500 with the gateway on (locality can only pay with a gateway for the bus devices), 3 extra runs of 5-6 h each, in parallel after the 500 pair; a config-only change (configs cable_types.*.cost_per_meter scaled), bound tool unaffected (cables 0); the result row for the paper: the multiplier at which zonal wiring (devices at their nearest unit) emerges. Not started (compute held by the 500 pair).
- Flat lite tests with failures and the gateway, final: jobs 3511 (900 s), 3517 (1,800 s, relaxation rule + lanes apart), 3524 (600 s), 3525 (1,200 s, + repair re-placement) all ended without a design (945 candidates in 20 min, every repair failed on chain or degraded latency of placement origin); diagnostic only: the flat search with everything free converges slowly with the gateway freedom. The group-by-group runs produce designs step by step: gw30_n500 step 4 in 33 s with 180 gateway variables in step 5 (bus devices present), nogw30_n500 step 4 in 36 s; gw30_n150 at step 3.
- Cable model: power and ground (user 2026-11-03, go: 'every cable x3 to model power and ground, then the sweep'). Implemented as configs/buses.json backbone.device_cable_factor = 3.0: the per-metre cost of every device-connected cable (direct SPI / UART device cables, Ethernet device attachments, bus lines) is multiplied by the factor in the optimiser's cable expression (calculate_cable_expressions), in the reported cable_cost (calculate_sensor_actuator_cable_costs) and in the checker's cost rule; the backbone between units keeps the signal price (no power on it). Lengths are unchanged. Every result before this date is the signal-only model (factor 1.0 = ablation row 'cable model'). The running 500 pair (jobs 3527 / 3528) and the 150 started before the change and keep the factor 1 (their config copies have no key). The sweep (next): x1, x3, x10 on top of the power-ground factor, 500 with the gateway, after the pair.

- Cable reach (user 2026-11-03, default; replaces the proposed ban of SPI / UART as backbone, which the user rejected as unprincipled): configs/buses.json cable_types.*.max_length_m with sources: ETH 15 m (1000BASE-T1 / 100BASE-T1 link segment), CAN 40 m (ISO 11898-2 at 1 Mbit/s), LIN 40 m, FlexRay 24 m, UART 5 m at 115.2 kbit/s (EIA-232 capacitance limit, low confidence), SPI 1 m (board-level clocked bus, 1 m the upper end with buffered lines at a reduced clock, low confidence; the suite's SPI devices sit 0.4-1.0 m from their nearest cell on the channel graph, so 0.5 m would leave the IMU and the front brake without a feasible cell). Model (optimizer.py, models.Interface.max_length_m, config_reader): a component with a directly wired device (SPI, UART) may only be placed within the device's reach (infeasible_ij), Ethernet attachments and backbone cables out of reach are not created / fixed to 0, candidate paths use hops within reach, a bus line's length variable is bounded by the reach; the checker's new rule 'reach' verifies every cable (direct, attachment, bus line, backbone) against the limit. Channel-graph distances as in the cost. Physics: SPI's usable clock falls with cable length (capacitance, reflections), so a 3 m SPI link at 9 Mbit/s is not a real option; with the reach, SPI between two ECUs remains possible only within 1 m (47 of the 435 cell pairs are within 1 m, 2 within 0.5 m). The load capacity per cable (ETH 1,000, SPI 9, CAN 0.5, LIN 0.0192, UART 0.1152 Mbit/s) was already enforced per cable count and per bus line. Runs started before this entry (r1 pair, r2 pair, r2 sweep) have no reach limit; their SPI backbone links are the 'no reach' ablation row.
- Reach, first test: the lite regression instance on its 4 cells is infeasible with the reach (the IMU's SPI driver has no cell within 1 m: 'SC_13_Driver_IMU_Top_Center has no feasible locations'); on the 30 cells the IMU has SEAT_F_L (0.96 m) and SEAT_F_R (0.97 m) within reach, so the primary and the standby of its driver are forced onto those two cells. tools/regression_test.py (4 cells) therefore needs a relaxed SPI reach in its case configs or 30 cells; not done yet. The reach test moved to the lite architecture on 30 cells (rho 0). A comment entry inside cable_types broke the config reader (string instead of a dict); the comment moved to the top level and the readers skip non-dict entries.
- Reach test on 30 cells (lite architecture, rho 0, job 3590): 28 placements and 388 backbone cable options excluded by the reach, optimum 4,689.06 in 2 min 46 (4,607.28 without the reach and with the power-ground factor 1: not comparable), checker OK with the new 'reach' rule (228 checks in 18 rules); the IMU driver lands at SEAT_F_R within its 1 m, the design uses ROOF_F and SEAT_F_R with one Ethernet link. The reach model is consistent end to end.
- r1 500 pair, first result (job 3528 nogw30_n500_s1_c30, gateway off, A+C polish, signal-only cables, no reach, 3 h 02): final cost 18,259.57, gap 11.81 % to the counting bound, checker OK, 6 ECUs, 21 partitions (2/2/9/3/5), 4 accelerators, 3 switches, 100.4 m cable, 12 links (2 SPI); the polish stopped after pass 2 by the 0.1 % rule (19,709 -> 18,274 -> 18,261), so at 500 the A+C polish ends slightly above the old two-pass polish (18,197.13): the stop rule can end a polish one pass early where the old setting went on. The gateway twin (job 3527) is in polish pass 2.
- Polish infeasibility in the r1 150 gateway run, root cause (2026-11-03): not the gateway and not the reach. The pooled (site, ASIL) capacity constraint is a relaxation of the bin packing; after every solve pack_partitions checks it (first-fit, then an exact MILP) and the optimiser adds a valid cut per unpackable group and solves again, each time with the full time limit (cell polish round 6: 3,031 s). After MAX_PACKING_CUTS = 10 cuts it KEPT the unpackable design ('WARNING ... keeping the solution', round 6, cost 15,562.07, cell closed); the next level rounds, with that placement fixed, were infeasible at presolve (the packing cuts stay in the reused model and contradict the fixed placement), until a round that freed the ASIL 2 components moved them (round 3: 14,911.55). The packing failures come from the r1 architecture's eight 89 % traffic-light containers (3 per site at most, the rest of a site's ASIL 2 software does not fit beside them): the r1 gateway 500 run had 20 packing re-solves without a warning, the r2 runs none. Fixes: (1) big-item strengthening in the model: a component or hot standby above half a partition cap in any resource needs a partition of its own (P >= their number per site and ASIL), valid for every design, closes most of the gap; (2) MAX_PACKING_CUTS 40, and an unpackable design after that is no design (optimize returns None, status UNPACKABLE) instead of being kept. Verified on the lite 30-cell instance (job lite_pack). The r1 150 run's final design will be checked for the partition rule when it ends.
- Checker fix (2026-11-03): under the gateway the checker filed a bus actuator's lane flows (SC_BUS) under the device instead of the component in its lost-function rule, so a component whose actuator lost its gateway was not counted as lost and the report comparison failed ('[report] lost functions reported ... recomputed ...', 8-9 errors on the two first r2 gateway results); fixed (act_out for SC_ACT and SC_BUS); the failure report's alive() also got the lane-line rule of the callback. Both results re-checked: OK (24 rules).
- r2 results, first two (500, corrected architecture, gateway on, A+C polish, no reach): r2gw (device_cable_factor 3, job 3537, 2 h 54): 12,243.75, counting bound 10,216.50 (gap 16.6 %), 5 ECUs, 14 partitions (2/2/2/3/5), 2 accelerators, 2 switches, 66.5 m cable (devices 52.4, backbone 14.2), 9 links (1 SPI), 6 bus lines; 21 bus devices carry gateways, 16 of them forwarded (the gateway is not the host). r2gwc1 (factor 1 = signal only, job 3539, 3 h 08): 11,312.66 (gap 9.7 %), 5 ECUs, 13 partitions (2/2/2/2/5), 2 accelerators, 1 switch, 66.7 m (60.5 / 6.2), 7 links (2 SPI), 6 bus lines, 16 of 21 forwarded. The one traffic-light container cut the ASIL 2 partitions from 9 to 2 and the accelerators from 4 to 2 (r1 500: 18,260, 21 partitions, 4 accelerators).
- r1 500 pair complete (2026-11-03): gateway on (job 3527, 4 h 55): 19,829.62, gap 18.6 % (bound 16,135.56), checker OK (24 rules), switches BPIL_R / COWL_R; gateway off (job 3528, 3 h 02): 18,259.57, gap 11.8 %. On r1 the gateway run ends 8.6 % above its twin: the builds end close (19,987 vs 19,709; the gateway build had 20 packing re-solves against 0, four steps at the wall each) but the polish differs: pass 1 took the twin from 19,709 to 18,274, the gateway run only from 19,987 to 19,846, and the 0.1 % rule ended both after pass 2. The r1 comparison is polluted by the packing failures of the eight 89 % containers (see the root-cause entry above); the clean gateway comparison is the r2 pair at factor 3. After the checker, main.py's architecture diagram (tools/architecture_diagram.py) fails for r1 runs with KeyError camera8: it labels partitions from the regenerated configs/arch_v3/swc.json, which no longer holds the r1 camera chains; results unaffected, to be fixed by reading the run's architecture_used.json.
- r1 150 gateway run ended (job 3526, 6 h): final cost 14,676.35 (no baseline), checker 2 errors '[peripheral] Ethernet device microphones / amplifier is not attached': an artefact of the test config, which was copied from the 500's cfg and lists the 500's devices while the 150 subset has no user for those two; the optimiser attaches only devices with users, the checker expects every listed Ethernet device attached. The run served as the early indicator only (every build step produced a design); it also carried the packing problem (one unpackable design kept in cell round 6, see the root-cause entry).

The costs of different factors are not comparable (the factor is in the cost); the structure is.
- r2 sweep complete (job 3541 r2gwc30, factor 30, 5 h 07): 12,285.01 (cable cost 851 = 6.9 % of the cost, the realistic weight), gap 16.8 %, checker OK, 5 ECUs (BAY_L, DASH_R, DOOR_R_R, SEAT_F_L, SEAT_F_R), 13 partitions (2/2/2/2/5), 2 accelerators, 2 switches, 67.9 m cable (devices 44.6 = the shortest of the sweep, backbone 23.3), 12 links, 10 bus lines (more, shorter lines), 17 of 21 bus devices forwarded, 19 of 33 devices at their nearest ECU. Sweep result (devices at their nearest ECU of 33): factor 1: 15, 3: 14, 9: 21, 30: 19; device cable 60.5 / 52.4 / 45.9 / 44.6 m. Reading for the paper: a heavier harness shortens the device cabling by a quarter and makes roughly 60 % of the devices local (from 45 %), but on the 500 it does not add units or change the ECU count: the remaining non-local devices are the Ethernet sensors drawn to the switch units and the critical bus devices whose two lanes must end at two units. Nothing is running after this entry. Next round needed for the final rows: the same five 500 rows with the reach rule and the packing fix (r3), then the 1,000 on r2 + reach.
- Figure of the r2 gateway 500 (r2gw_n500_s1_c30, render job 3609, results/vehicle_layout/r2gw_n500/design_figure.pdf/.png, 52 cables 66.5 m): E1 SEAT_F_L central compute (194 components = 39 %, accelerator, switch, DSP, HSM, CPU 43 %), E2 DASH_R central compute (176 = 35 %, accelerator, DSP, HSM, CPU 25 %), E3 DASH_L zone controller (91, HSM, 6 of 10 nearby devices), E4 SEAT_R_R zone controller (17 components, one ASIL 4 partition, automated driving 71 %, 4 of 9 nearby), E5 BPIL_R edge ECU with a switch (22 components, one ASIL 4 partition at 84 %, automated driving 77 %). First figure with DSP and security-module tabs; one camera. The 'edge ECU' label on E5 (one busy ASIL 4 partition, unit CPU 21 %) is the compute-threshold question again.
- Figures of the sweep rows (2026-11-03): r2gwc9 (factor 9, results/vehicle_layout/r2gwc9_n500) and r2gwc30 (factor 30, results/vehicle_layout/r2gwc30_n500). The factor-30 design has a switch-only relay node at ROOF_F (a cell with an Ethernet switch and no software, site.switch_node_cost); design_figure.py now draws such nodes as small grey boxes labelled S1.. (switch_nodes_of), lists them in the design table and resolves their labels in the link table (it crashed on them before). Role observation: in the factor-9 design SEAT_F_R hosts 250 components (50 % of the software) with DSP, HSM and a switch at unit CPU 26 % and is labelled 'edge ECU' by the 40 % rule; the strongest case yet for the hosting rule or the 25 % threshold.
- Paper limitation, cable weight (user 2026-11-03, keep for the paper): the factors 3, 9 and 30 rest on two rough arguments, the installed cost of a device connection (signal pair plus power and ground cores, connectors, routing labour: about x3 to x5 per metre) and the harness share of a car (the modelled cables are 0.4 % of the design cost, a real harness of the order of 5-10 % of the E/E cost: x12 to x25). Neither is a measured price. So the paper states them as assumptions and reports the sweep rather than one 'true' value; the power distribution network is not represented by any factor (it goes to every unit whatever runs there). The container CPU demand (0.3 core, 512 MB) is the other stated assumption until measured.
- Commit (user 2026-11-03): all changes of the series committed on c-scale as 2bf7ce3 (115 files; the prepared architectures without their routing_space.npz copies, now ignored). The push to origin failed: no credential helper, no gh, GitHub rejects the host's SSH key; HTTPS reads work. The user pushes with their token (`git push origin c-scale`) or adds an SSH key to GitHub. tools/regression_test.py: the 4-cell cases relax the SPI reach to 3 m (the roof IMU is out of reach of all 4 cells); the regression runs as job 3613 (results/regression/run_2026-11-03.txt); its CASES values predate the device_cable_factor 3 and will need updating from the new proven values.
- Container CPU measured per thread (2026-11-03, user request; job 3614 aw_cam2 on zits-slurmworker4, 4 min 46; autoware_bench/measure_cam2.sh = measure_cam.sh + `ps -eLo pid,tid,pcpu,cputime,comm,args` every second for the traffic-light containers and `nvidia-smi pmon`; analysis autoware_bench/analysis_threads.py, data out_cam2). Each container (camera6 / camera7) has 273 threads and 244 % of a host core in total: one work thread at 59.5 % (max 78.5 %), the CUDA event handler at 5.5 %, and about 270 threads at 0.7 % each = 190 % together, which are the multi-threaded executor's polling threads on the 256-CPU host (one per CPU), not work of the function; no thread sits at 100 %, so there is no separate spin thread: the GPU wait is inside the work thread. Camera rate 15.8 Hz. Result: per 10 Hz stream (59.5 + 5.5) x 10 / 15.8 = 41 % of a host core x 2.56 x 12,250 = 12,860 DMIPS = 26 % of a partition cap before the robustness multipliers (an upper bound). So the earlier assumption of 0.3 core (3,675) was too low and the original one-core cap (12,250) was about right by accident; the old 82,756 (264 % x currency) was the polling artefact. The generator now carries DNN_CPU_DMIPS = 12,860 with this source; RAM stays the 512 MB assumption. Architectures regenerated (revision r3 = r2 with the measured container): one container per instance, cpu_req 12,860. The r2 results stand on their architecture_used.json copies; the next round runs on r3 with the reach and the packing fix. nvidia-smi pmon gave no per-process rows inside the container (empty), the GPU utilisation samples were kept.
- Note on r3: tools/arch_v3_prepare.py caps a single component at one core (CORE_DMIPS 12,250), so the measured 12,860 of the container becomes cpu_req 12,250 in every prepared architecture (5 % below the measurement, within its uncertainty); the cap stays as the documented assumption. The regression test's SPI relaxation to 3 m applies only to its own temporary 4-cell case configs; the master config and all 30-cell runs keep SPI at 1 m (there was no reach before 2026-11-03).
